notes-synthesis.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Rust rewrite — synthesis (revision 2, 2026-11-01)

Inputs: notes-library.md, notes-clifflab-rocklab.md, notes-grasslab-meadowlab.md, notes-water-shore-dirt.md, notes-language-and-rulings.md, the four notes-variants-*.md, and input-language-design-conversation.md. Revision 2 adds an adversarial review that checked each claim against the code at HEAD 803ba4f and against the conversation. Every change from revision 1 is in CHANGELOG.md. The published pages (index.html, library.html, labs.html, variants.html, mechanisms.html, architecture.html, feasibility.html, changelog.html) are built from this note.

Top findings

  1. The labs, not the library, are the reference for how things should look. The library's grass is a thatch wrapper that ignores species/placement (scatter/prototype.py:121-138, all 30 grass specs pass them). Library stones are a separate "aged" pipeline from rocklab's facet-carved stones. clifflab/rockpile, grasslab v3, waterlab swell-chaos and dirtlab are the approved looks. Port the labs first; port the library only for what has no lab yet (walls, trees, bases, region plumbing).
  2. Each approved look is its own solver built from shared parts. Revision 1 claimed every look was a short composition of 7 verbs "with no special mechanism". Walking each lab through the verbs disproved it: the meadow needed 7 additions, the cliff 11, water and dirt 4–5 each, and two verbs merged different mechanisms (below). So the architecture has two layers: declarative documents that name built-in looks and tune them with natural controls, and a Rust engine in which each look is a built-in written on about twenty shared kit operations.
  3. Every approved look follows one recipe with three scales: a broad form, then mid-scale structure (flow, crack network, facet set, wave sources), then fine grain. Printability is built in at each scale and checked once at the end, and that check must be a gate. Round 11 cliffs still have 1.1–12.6 mm² of overhang per tile and see-through ray holes (s3: 5, s4: 1, 2×2 massif: 262).
  4. Ordering needs two passes. Meadow and shore rocks register footprints before the grass (meadowlab.py:224, shorelab.py:286) and are seated on the grass top after it (meadowlab.py:236, shorelab.py:296). Plan then build solves this. Plans (positions, footprints, tags, fields, curves) run in stack order and see plans below. Builds run in stack order and see solids below plus every plan.
  5. Heightfields carry most of the work; meshes and booleans do the rest; 2D polygons are a fourth kernel. Revision 1 said "every lab unions only once, at export". That is wrong: rocklab unions clusters, rockpile does per-stone trims, differences, guard booleans and a pile union before the dirt layer, and grasslab and meadowlab union more than once. The correct statement is that solids are combined into the scene mesh once, at export, while booleans inside a built-in are unrestricted. SDF + marching cubes is used for grass blades (grasslab.py:909), the wall kernel (walls/masonry.py:440-486, a true LSE-of-planes SDF) and clifflab.py:1013. The library's blur_remesh marches a blurred occupancy grid, not an SDF.
  6. Duplicate mechanisms, counted. Library-only counts unless marked: - about 15 noise sites using 5 algorithms; - 9 seed schemes, including walls that ignore the tile seed (masonry.py:564) and child seeds drawn from the parent stream; - about 16 inline fades; - 6 grid-stamping routines; - 6 rounding methods (including rounded_box, stone/shape.py:61); - overhang checks: 2 real audits (stones on the pre-relief hull, tree 26° strict) plus 3 construction thresholds, and 3 lab copies (rocklab and grasslab at 45°, rockpile at 55°); - 3 heightfield meshers on 3 grid conventions; - 3 crack generators, 3 float32 export checks, 3 paint simulators; - 12 point samplers across library and labs.
  7. The obstacle interface is the glue between labs. It covers footprint, wrap, blocker, seat and emitter kind. In the language it becomes tags (material, kind, owner path) read through queries. Revision 1 put each look's response on the obstacle, e.g. emitter(band: 2mm..4.5mm) on the rock. That is corrected: tags carry only a kind, and the consumer owns the table (water maps kind → wave band; the meadow owns tuck and foot height).
  8. The mutable TileScene grid bag causes real bugs: - displace_terrain (and set_terrain, :146) resets terrain_support_z (core/tile.py:137), erasing support stamps. obstacle_mask and the stone seal written into terrain_z survive. - Every Boundary layer receives the mask of all boundary cells (terrains/tile.py:376,399). Combined with seeds that carry no layer identity (layers/soil.py:68; scatter/prototype.py:108-116), the ten multi-shore tiles add soil and stones 2–4 times (the -x tiles 4×). - Two SoilCarpets in disjoint regions are harmless; the bug needs overlapping masks. - Only one layer state leaks into the OpenLOCK rerun: CutStoneWall writes self.height_mm (masonry.py:692). - Walls, floors and FacetedStones ignore their region mask.
  9. The real tuning surface can't be seen from specs: 233 module-level _UPPER constants (some are caches and tables); labs monkeypatch each other's globals. Seeds are positional and kept aligned with dummy draws (meadowlab U(8,8), grasslab's dead swath amp). Many draws depend on grid resolution (random cell indices, white noise sized to the grid), which is why the OpenLOCK build is a different random tile.
  10. Shawn's language model holds; revision 1 bent several rulings and revision 2 restores them:
    • fractions for all authored lengths (L685);
    • regions only from lines and fill points (L1987); revision 1's self - bare and where(field > t) are removed;
    • water over the whole tile (L2293, L2345); revision 1's connected: water is removed;
    • one definition form (L2582, L3003);
    • unlimited nesting (L2584);
    • named standard heights (L505);
    • variability up/down in h (L883, L930).

Two layers

Documents (what Shawn, agents and the GUI write). Six ideas: - Definition: one form for tiles and components; parameters with defaults; a stack. - Lines and regions: paint bucket only; derived lines offset, contour; a band is two offset lines plus a fill point. - Stack: an ordered list of applications of definitions to self. - Parameters: squares across and h up, with the unit supplied by the parameter's axis; distributions; named heights; fields as values. - Tags: user-named material, kind, owner path. - Seeds by path: ordinal or explicit id for duplicate sibling names; optional pin.

Contracts: printer profile, edge contract, connector, material → colour.

Built-ins: hills, dirt, rocks, scree, cliff, meadow, reeds, water, wall, floor, tree, connector.

Engine values: Region, Field, Points, Curves (including attributed graphs), Solid (tagged).

Engine ops (kit): - field ops: noise (position-keyed, resolution-independent), distance (EDT with indices, geodesic), morphology / masked blur / quantile / slope cap, flow (meander, wrap with axis, steer), grooves (field from curves); - scatter (plane / along curves / on a surface; ordered; until coverage); - partition (gravity | courses | tessellate); - trace (streamline with per-curve stops | walk network | colonise → graph); - carve (hull or prism ∩ half-spaces with guards; round; booleans); - loft (along curve or graph; morphing profile; layer stack + drape); - lift (adaptive heightfield → solid); - relief (depth raster in a face frame → clamp → lift → subtract); - grain (refine + displace along normals, masked); - seat (named bed rule: quantile | plane | cones at overhang | min ground; sink; plinth; clearance); - fill (per column: surface = level + field where top < level, margin, else bury); - queries (top, footprint by tag, distance to tag, ray / depth raster, support); - audit and export (one overhang test with red paint, float32 reload, pinch separation, keep largest with a report, fit to tile).

Merges revision 1 made that the code does not support

Revision 1 Code Now
relieve = one verb for soil, grain, waves, crack grooves face relief is depth raster → lift → difference (rockpile.py:1255–1332); grain is refine + vertex displacement (rocklab.py:283); dirt is grid arithmetic relief, grain, field ops
pack = cliff stack = wall courses = fieldstone fill wall _layout is a deterministic partition with no gravity (masonry.py:286–365); fieldstone is partition then loft partition with 3 methods
stroke blade = sweep with a stroke_stack profile per-layer 2D strokes over different spans, lofted via 2D SDFs, sheared onto H₀ (grasslab.py:812–911) loft layer-stack mode + drape
seat = bed quantile/plane + plinth covers cliff stones and scree cliff: cone-max underside at the overhang limit (rockpile.py:1530–1564); scree: min ground − sink − tilt lift; rocklab: fixed sink seat with named bed rules
fill = level / close / film level is pointwise; close is morphology; film is relief one fill(surface) plus field ops
swell = relieve with an emitters field a global native solver (waterlab.py:719–944) water built-in
carve covers the seat underside and wall blocks seat is a heightfield ∩; wall blocks are an SDF level set seat; carve with level-set option

Representation answer

  • The scene is an ordered list of tagged solids. The mesh is the common format, with native fast paths: a lifted heightfield answers top() without rasterising its mesh. Queries are cached per snapshot.
  • Plan lazily, build eagerly, both cached by (path, seed, hash of inputs). The plan pass is the live preview.
  • Four kernels: grid (f32), plan (2D polygons), mesh (Manifold), rays (BVH).
  • Water is per column over the whole tile: wet = top < level; surface = level + tide + swell inside wet ∪ 0.6 mm margin; buried 0.3 mm elsewhere. There is no connectivity rule (no water lab uses one; ndimage.label appears only in rockpile).

Language decisions

  1. Units. Squares across, h up; printer rules are a contract, not a unit; authored angles are real angles. Open question for Shawn: should print-scale details scale with the square?
  2. Regions are paint buckets only. Derived lines feed them; soft areas are field parameters.
  3. Water over the whole tile.
  4. Plan then build.
  5. Tags carry kinds; consumers carry tables.
  6. Below zero belongs to the connector. Carves record voids applied to the connector, which is built last (the DungeonBlocks peg height depends on the content's maximum Z, bases/dungeonblocks.py:20-37); there is a through-hole where no connector material exists. Open: the OpenLOCK z = 0 datum.
  7. Read anywhere, add anywhere, remove only inside self (to confirm with Shawn; it loosens L3171).
  8. One edge contract at the root: - tide λ = 0.5 square, crests at corners and square midpoints, 0.6 mm amplitude; - calm rim of 4 mm and tide blend of 9 mm; - one ground edge level (the labs disagree: 3.4 vs 4.0 mm); - line anchors; - the cliff end section.
  9. OpenLOCK is a second evaluation, sharing a layout through resolution-independent noise and path seeds.
  10. The audit is a gate.
  11. KDL documents in directories; JSON Schema from the Rust model for the GUI and MCP; a tiny expression grammar; no loops, assignments or call chains.

Orin ranking (where to cut)

  • Simple (v1 core): grid; squares and h; named heights; lines and regions; the stack; one definition form with unlimited nesting; seeds by path; DungeonBlocks; STL.
  • Normal (v1): built-ins with natural controls; distributions; tags; plan then build; printer profile and audit gate; edge contract; OpenLOCK as a second evaluation.
  • Elegant (v1): fields as values; derived lines; user definitions; caching; plan-pass preview; schema.
  • Expressive (v1.x): kit ops callable from documents; richer expressions; STL import; per-face overrides.
  • Feature-rich (later): colour, 3MF/Bambu, CMYK, GUI, MCP. The format is chosen for the GUI and MCP now.
  • Performant (later): GPU fields; caching finer than an entry; a hand-tuned BVH.

Cut first: - the 3D grass growth simulation, FloppyGrass and thatch; - the three rock front-ends; - most rounding methods; - the 233 hidden constants; - dummy draws and resolution-dependent draws; - the plugin system.

Feasibility (summary; detail in feasibility.html)

  • Booleans: start with manifold-csg 0.4.1 (FFI to Manifold 3.5.4, full C API). Keep the mesh kernel behind a trait so manifold-rust 0.19 (a pure-Rust port, claims test parity and bit-identical parallel output) can replace it after spike R1.
  • Rays: obvhs or parry3d, with embree3 over FFI as fallback (R3).
  • Write ourselves: EDT, labels, blur, Poisson sampling, normal smoothing, adaptive lift.
  • Determinism:
  • keyed seeds per item, never shared streams;
  • fixed-order reductions;
  • each Manifold boolean single-threaded, with many in parallel (#1177: parallel CSG is not deterministic);
  • libm if cross-platform identity is wanted.
  • Speed: the big wins are parallel blade meshing (about 50 ms per blade, GIL-bound) and adaptive relief heightfields in the cliff. Booleans are already C++ in Python.
  • Migration: 1. spikes; 2. skeleton plus dirt; 3. rocks and water; 4. grass, meadow and shore; 5. walls, floors, bases and trees; 6. cliff; 7. front ends.
  • Risks R1–R10 are in feasibility.html.

Layout (revision 3, detail in layout.html and notes-layout.md)

  • One Cargo workspace (virtual manifest, flat crates/*, edition 2024, resolver 3, pinned toolchain) at the repo root beside the untouched Python.
  • Six crates: dt-geom (kernels, seeds, units; owns the Manifold backend feature), dt-kit (scene, ops, audit, export), dt-doc (KDL model and look controls, no geometry), dt-looks, dt-eval (imports, plan/build, cache, paths), dharmatiles (one program: CLI, MCP over stdio, preview server). Later: Tauri app in app/, dt-wasm; optional dt-py.
  • Data in library/ (tiles, parts, printers, palettes) embedded with rust-embed and addressed as lib:; optional dharmatiles.kdl project file; output in out/; user tiles in ~/Documents/dharmatiles; config and cache via etcetera.

notes-layout.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Rust rewrite — project layout notes (revision 3, 2026-11-01)

Source notes for layout.html. Checked on 2026-11-01. "Knowledge" means it comes from prior knowledge and was not re-checked on the web in this pass; treat those as stable but unverified.

Recommendation in one tree

dharmatiles/
├─ Cargo.toml            virtual manifest; workspace.package / .dependencies / .lints; resolver = "3"
├─ Cargo.lock            committed
├─ rust-toolchain.toml   exact stable (1.98.1 was current on 2026-09-03; check 1.99+ at start)
├─ .cargo/config.toml    xtask alias
├─ deny.toml             cargo-deny
├─ dharmatiles.kdl       project file for the bundled library (optional for users)
├─ crates/
│  ├─ dt-geom/           grid, 2D, mesh trait + Manifold backends (feature), rays, seeds, units
│  ├─ dt-kit/            scene, ~20 ops, audit, STL/3MF export, CPU preview renderer
│  ├─ dt-doc/            KDL -> typed model, look controls, JSON Schema, miette diagnostics (no geometry)
│  ├─ dt-looks/          built-in looks, one module each, only dt-kit's public API
│  ├─ dt-eval/           project, lib: imports, plan -> build, seeds by path, cache, paths, embedded library
│  ├─ dharmatiles/       the one binary: CLI, mcp (rmcp, stdio), preview server, later lsp
│  ├─ dt-wasm/           later
│  └─ dt-py/             optional (PyO3 + maturin) for parity tests
├─ app/                  later: Tauri 2 (src-tauri/ member but not default member; ui/ plain ES modules + three.js)
├─ library/              tiles/<family>/*.kdl, parts/, printers/, palettes/  (embedded via rust-embed)
├─ xtask/
├─ docs/                 + docs/book/ (mdBook) and docs/reference/ (renders, LFS) later
├─ out/                  git-ignored output: out/<system>/<path>/<name>-<system>.stl
└─ src/ tests/ *lab/ stl/ pyproject.toml   Python, untouched until cutover

Decisions and their sources

Uncertain or fast-moving

  • Current stable Rust (1.98.1 confirmed; 1.99 likely out but unconfirmed).
  • dist's maintenance pace.
  • rmcp 3.x minor versions and the next MCP spec revision.
  • Tauri 2 mobile maturity for a 3D viewport on phones.
  • manifold-rust parity (spike R1) and manifold-csg cross-compiling for iOS/Android.

  • facet-kdl's KDL v2 support.

notes-language-and-rulings.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Notes: description language (as settled) + taste rulings

Source: input-language-design-conversation.md (Shawn ↔ ChatGPT, 2026-10-29 → 10-31), the auto-memory directory, and four docs (tile-design-guide.md, build-provenance-spec.md, browser-python-viability.md, cloud-mesh-design.md). Digest written 2026-10-31. Quotes marked "Shawn:" are verbatim (voice-transcribed, so lightly garbled). Syntax in the conversation is all illustrative: no surface syntax (YAML/KDL/custom) was chosen.

(a) Spec digest — what was settled

a0. Product framing (context, lightly settled)

  • One core, many front ends: CLI with a "beautiful text UI" (colours, progress bars), MCP server for agents, GUI editor that edits the same descriptions, a library of tiles/components in directories selectable by path expression.
  • Shawn wants a static declarative file, not a program: "It seems better for it to be a static definition file as opposed to, you know, turning config itself like that. It can be quite a powerful language. But it should be declarative."
  • Webview UI is fine ("a web view is probably fine"); ChatGPT ranked Tauri 2 > Dioxus > Slint. Single codebase matters more than native look (he won't test Win/Linux himself).
  • Printability is baked into generators, not checked after (Shawn: "the procedural generators are generators for FDM printable terrain. That is for sure the case. I'm already doing that").
  • Third-party scripted plugins: "maybe not that exciting… it can always just accept pull requests on the core platform".

a1. Tile basics

  • A tile has size (rows × cols) → a grid of squares; a horizontal scale (mm per square: 25, 35, 1 in…); a connector (DungeonBlocks, OpenLOCK, bare…).
  • Positions: integer square indices (row,col) or real positions where integer = square boundary; (0.5,0.5) = centre of square (0,0), (1.5,0.5) = centre of square (1,0). Continuous coordinates are fundamental.
  • Logical position is independent of physical scale (a tree at (1.5,0.5) stays there at 25 or 35 mm).
  • Vertical: Shawn: "The tall wall is defined as height 1.0. The vertical scale determines what 1.0 means. So one vertical scale in mm. All standard heights in proportional fractions." Two independent scales: S_h mm/square, S_v mm per logical z-unit. (x,y,z)_mm = (x·S_h, y·S_h, z·S_v).
  • Standard named heights (water, dirt, grass, floor, short wall, tall wall) are configurable defaults in fractions, not properties of the coordinate system.
  • Shawn: "I think we should do everything with virtual fractions." (i.e. detail sizes too — see critical note e1.)

Illustrative snippet (ChatGPT, post-ruling):

tile:
  size: [3, 4]
  scale: 25mm
  vertical_scale: 50mm
  connector: openlock

heights:
  bottom: 0.0
  water: 0.10
  dirt: 0.14
  grass: 0.18
  floor: 0.20
  short_wall: 0.50
  tall_wall: 1.0

a2. Variability

  • Variability is across the tile (spatial relief), the drama-vs-playability knob. Shawn: "High variability adds drama but makes surface less flat for playing. Having a standard scale for this across terrain types makes configuration easier."
  • Per feature, not tile-wide (Shawn: "Nah. Per feature.").
  • Measured in height units: Shawn: "variability is a measurement in the height scale. So one variability would mean it would go from the bottom to the top of the whole tall wall. So normal variability would be like 0.2". Not limited to 0..1.
  • Independent up/down: Shawn: "we need a variability up and a variability down. And you can specify variability and it would do 50% on either side". V = V_up + V_down; shorthand variability: 0.2 ≡ up 0.1 / down 0.1.
  • ChatGPT additions (not contested): these are bounds on the generator, not promises it reaches them; shorthand and explicit forms mutually exclusive in one feature; the character (spatial frequency/shape) belongs to the generator; a feature_scale knob deferred until needed.
grass:
  height: 0.18
  variability: 0.20

water:
  height: 0.12
  variability_up: 0.18
  variability_down: 0.02

a3. Lines (named Bézier curves)

  • Boundaries are Bézier curves with logical control points, arbitrary control, plus controllable randomness ("Organic or non-organic for like walls and stuff").
  • Lines are inert until a region names them. Shawn: "you can make as many lines as you want and they don't impact each other unless the terrain explicitly names the lines in question."
  • ChatGPT (accepted implicitly): organic perturbation is spatially coherent noise, applied once per line so every referencing region sees the same curve; endpoints/junctions pinned.

a4. Regions = lines + implicit perimeter + fill points (paint bucket)

Shawn's formulation (the keeper): "every region is defined by some set of named lines. And that set always is implicitly includes the line that is the perimeter of the tile. And a fill point. And it basically, it defines the area that fills whatever up to all the specified lines in all directions." - Formal: the region is the connected component containing P of interior(T) \ L (closure for the filled area). Holes allowed; dangling curves don't divide (you can walk around the end); a fill point on a curve is invalid. - Multiple fill points → union of faces. Shawn: "Multiple fill points is a great idea. And then we don't actually need union intersection and difference. You can just use different lines and stuff". - No explicit booleans, no left/right, no inside(), no between(). The same face may belong to several regions (regions overlap freely). - Inside a nested definition, the implicit perimeter is the boundary of self.

regions:
  grass:
    lines: [north_south_a, north_south_b,
            east_west_a, east_west_b]
    fill:
      - [0.5, 0.5]
      - [2.5, 0.5]
      - [1.5, 1.5]
      - [0.5, 2.5]
      - [2.5, 2.5]

Note: the current Python already has this shape — Region(id='meadow', selector=FloodFill(0.5, 0.5), layers=[…]).

a5. The ordered stack

  • Shawn: "the ordered [terrain] stack is really just an execution order from bottom to top… Apply that operator… to this selected region on top of whatever is there already." Each op is T_{n+1} = F_n(T_n, R_n, P_n).
  • No separate "features" vs "surfaces". Shawn: "Water surface and hills and rocky slabs seem similar. I don't like extra concepts." Hills, dirt, grass, water, rock, carve, scatter, import, texture are all ops.
  • Each op defines its own semantics against accumulated geometry (hill raises, dirt/grass coat, rock adds volume, carve removes, water floods).
  • Water floods (fills below its level, higher ground sticks out) — Shawn: "One hundred percent". Excavate first if you want a pond basin.
  • Region ≠ visible result. Shawn: "you'd have to do is create a boundary, raise the land on one side, lower the land on the other, and then put water over the whole tile." Shoreline emerges as the contour h(x,y)=z_water. He endorsed it from experience: waves already moved the effective shore, and "I ended up needing to simulate water over the entire surface of the tile underneath things because otherwise there was this weird cutoff at the boundary".
  • Negative z: carve below z=0 into the connector; no material → through-hole ("trap pits and downward stairs"). z=0 is a datum, not a floor.
  • Unbounded geometry: Shawn: "We should be able to go past the other bounds of the tile as well. Use with care but makes sense with overhanging trees, really tall things". Region controls where an op acts, not the extent of what it makes.
  • STL import is an op ("Should be able to place stls on tiles to import things"). ChatGPT: asset declares units; elevation: 0.2 (absolute) vs elevation: surface (sit on accumulated terrain).
  • Connector geometry is generated once at the root, before the stack; Shawn: "so connector geometry lives underneath zero." ChatGPT: allow the geometry, validate the printability.
stack:
  - terrain: base_ground
    region: whole_tile
  - terrain: hill
    region: hill_region
    height: 0.35
  - terrain: rock
    region: rocky_area
  - terrain: dirt
    region: whole_tile
  - terrain: grass
    region: grassy_area
  - terrain: water
    region: pond_area
    height: 0.35

- op: carve
  region: pit
  bottom: -0.3

a6. Definitions (recursion), self, grid frame

  • Shawn: "maybe definitions are sometimes tiles and sometimes other things (like rocks or trees or grass surfaces) and can be applied as a layer inside any other definition. Recursion."
  • Shawn: "it should receive a region (region is the 'self' in programming terms). You can draw new beziers, choose new regions as part of that. At the top level the region you receive is the tile perimeter."
  • Squares still matter: "we probably receive BOTH -- a set of squares with an overlaid region, and relative parameters are wrt the squares we receive."
  • One kind of definition; everything is a parameter. Shawn: "nah -- everything can be inherited or passed --- this is just parameterization of the definition. It could be unspecified (must be passed by caller), specified (as a default…overridable by a caller)." No non-overridable params: "it's ok if somebody changes the shape/size of something not intended to change shape/size -- creative opportunities."
  • ChatGPT (uncontested): applying a definition = inlining its ops into the caller's stack at that point (acts on accumulated geometry; not a finished mesh); connectors only at the root; reject cycles initially (bounded recursion later); grid frame is a rectangle (origin, rows, cols), never a bare set of squares; normalized coords are relative to the frame, never the region.
  • A tile = root invocation with grid, scales, connector resolved, self = tile footprint.
definition: rocky_hillside
parameters:
  height: 0.3
  roughness: 0.1
stack:
  - op: raise
    region: target
    height: $height
  - op: rock_texture
    region: target
    roughness: $roughness

- apply: ruined_staircase
  region: courtyard
  parameters:
    rows: 4
    cols: 1
    steps: 12

a7. Three spaces (Shawn's framing, 2026-10-30)

  1. Area — "a closed set of Bézier curves that defines… some geometric space".
  2. Grid frame — the squares. "if I had a six by six tile, and then I had a tiny area in the middle, and I pass it down to a thing that takes a little area, it would expect say a one by one grid… the coordinates of the Bézier curves would be translated as it got passed down".
  3. Terrain volume — "the volume of the terrain itself also gets passed down and you are restricted inside of it. And Interestingly, that has the original root coordinate system on it. And you're acting on it through a view." - Open (ChatGPT flagged): how the restricted view treats overhanging output — a tree may grow beyond its region without gaining permission to modify unrelated terrain.

a8. Parameters as random distributions; seeds

  • Shawn: "Instead of any place where you can pass a number, you should be able to pass a random distribution… within this range and maybe… flat versus some distribution skew". Not tile size; yes Bézier coordinates and "everything else".
  • Resolved before execution (parameter-resolution phase per invocation; no resampling mid-run). Same definition applied 3× → 3 different samples.
  • Seeds: Shawn: "Every call into either a sub-definition or some more primitive thing… gets its own derived seed from the parent. And so if a particular component fixes a seed, then that's seed. If it doesn't fix the seed, it gets a new seed for itself, derived from the parent seed, so that its calls are random according to the seed, but do not depend on random numbers being generated for other components". Root sets master seed; any call may pin its own.
  • ChatGPT execution model: receive context → resolve parameters & randomness → establish local coords & region → execute ops against shared volume. Correlated randomness via expressions (taller tree → thicker trunk).

a9. Types and expressions

  • Primitives: Shawn lists enums, floats, flags/booleans; strings "I don't know that that has a lot of use" (→ metadata, asset paths); colours: yes ("We probably should support colors"). ChatGPT adds ints, flag sets.
  • Geometric (ChatGPT): 2D/3D points, vectors, Bézier curves, regions, references to definitions.
  • Distributions are a way of supplying a value of a type, not a type (uniform float, random int, weighted enum choice).
  • Expressions everywhere values go: Shawn: "If you pass a parameter in, then it should be able to be used as expressions to calculate sub- subcall parameters".

a10. Colour pipeline

Three stages; Shawn: "Yes, I really love that." 1. Geometry generation carrying semantic material identity (rock, grass, dirt, water, wood) on surfaces/volumes, surviving geometric ops. 2. Procedural colour (material + convexity + AO + elevation + slope + water distance + noise → desired RGB) — "a miniature painter" (drybrush ridges, wash crevices, moss on up-faces). 3. Printer mapping: mono STL (ignore colour), multi-filament 3MF, Bambu-ready project (separate target — Bambu metadata ≠ generic 3MF), CMYK/layered/dithered. - Shawn's motivation: Bambu project files with pre-coloured models; interest in CMYK ("highlight outcroppings and put more shadow in lowlands… that is, again, I think in your material to color transformation step"). - Principle: "Geometric texture changes the shape. Procedural colour changes its appearance." Colour resolution ≠ geometry resolution (keep colour as surface attributes until export). - Shawn declined to design material-declaration syntax next ("Nah, that's fine").

material: heroic_rock
color:
  base: "#77736C"
  highlights:
    color: "#B6ADA0"
    where: convexity
    strength: 0.35
  shadows:
    color: "#34383C"
    where: ambient_occlusion
    strength: 0.45
  moss:
    color: "#536A3F"
    where: upward_facing
    coverage: 0.2

a11. Geometry representations

Catalogued (not chosen): heightmaps, voxels, triangle meshes, analytic SDFs, CSG (a strategy not a format), parametric surfaces (Bézier/NURBS/subdiv), swept geometry (trunks, roots, mouldings), B-reps (connectors), plus point clouds/particles/tet meshes. Likely mix: hills = height functions, outcrops = SDF, trees = sweeps, walls = CSG, imports = meshes; final watertight triangle mesh near export.

(b) Open questions (options + leaning)

# Question Options discussed Leaning
1 How ops see foreign representations universal format (sparse SDF); pairwise conversions; common query interface ("lenses"); hybrid lazy Shawn proposed lenses; ChatGPT: hybrid — common query interface (inside?, ray hit, nearest point+normal+material, elevation at xy, geometry in bbox) + composable volume + representation-specific ops + caching. "A common interface doesn't imply a common storage format."
2 Lazy vs eager lazy construction (deferred op graph) vs lazy spatial evaluation (final mesher pulls point queries down the stack) Shawn: "Sounds to me like… a lazy model would be better" then "I dunno. Is this better? Open question I think". ChatGPT: lazy graph yes, not dogmatically lazy evaluation — many ops are non-local (scatter, erosion, curvature); each layer sees an immutable snapshot below; materialize/cache when useful; prototype hill + rock scatter + water first.
3 Accumulated terrain: implicit volume vs object collection one implicit mathematical volume vs collection of objects exposing a common interface Explicitly left open ("not enough evidence").
4 Texture: op or object parameter? separate texture op (reusable across wall/cliff/import) vs creation parameter; plus surface targeting (surfaces: vertical, exposed faces) ChatGPT: both (op, packaged as a param convenience). Shawn: "I don't know if that's just a parameter of the object creation" — unresolved.
5 Normalized 0..1 vs squares 0..1 = whole frame vs square units Shawn: "relative 0..1 references still work… I wonder though if 0..1 should be the full tile width/height… Hmmm... dilemma." ChatGPT: keep both, explicitly tagged grid(1.5,0.5) vs relative(0.5,0.5); never let an unqualified coordinate change meaning. Settled later in the conversation (L2819, L3121–3131): "we probably receive BOTH -- a set of squares with an overlaid region, and relative parameters are wrt the squares we receive"; the frame is re-based when an area is passed down. So coordinates are in units of received squares.
6 Restricted-view write semantics clip writes to region; allow overhang additions; forbid modifying unrelated terrain Unresolved.
7 OpenLOCK thickness vs DB thin-terrain z=0 datum per connector; each connector generator owns floor thickness ChatGPT answer; Shawn didn't respond.
8 Seed-moved fill points persistent face identity (CAD profiles) vs validator warnings ChatGPT raised; no decision.
9 Scene-of-objects vs recipe "is a cliff one object with 20 params or an assembly of ops" Shawn: "I don't know yet" — later effectively answered by "everything is a definition with a stack".
10 Flexible definitions: stretch vs repeat detail at constant scale both, per generator ChatGPT only.
11 Surface syntax KDL (comment-preserving Rust parser), CUE, Pkl, HCL, RON, YAML/JSON; serde + schemars for JSON Schema shared by CLI/GUI/MCP Deferred deliberately.
12 Axis naming (row,col) vs (x,y) Flagged, not settled.

(c) Rejected / amended ChatGPT proposals

  1. Mixed height units (fraction / 6mm / floor - 2mm) → rejected: one vertical scale, all standard heights fractions.
  2. Variability as dimensionless 0–1 "drama" intensity → rejected: it is a height-unit magnitude.
  3. Tile-wide variability with per-surface override / global GUI drama slider → rejected: per feature.
  4. Symmetric ± variability around nominal → amended: independent up/down.
  5. Transitions/bands as a third concept ("shore" as boundary feature) → rejected: Shawn: "Is the shoreline not a terrain type like grass and water?" Width-from-a-curve is just a region constructor; then "asymmetry isn't very valuable then."
  6. Regions partition the tile + overlapping "features" (two spatial systems, affects: lists) → rejected ("I don't like extra concepts. Plus you need to define the footprint somehow").
  7. Global planar subdivision by all boundaries → rejected: lines inert unless named.
  8. left_of/right_of (directional paths) → rejected: "Left and right are wrong."
  9. Region algebra (inside, band, face, | & -) → reduced: Shawn's paint bucket + multiple fill points eliminated booleans and inside. (Status of band(path, w) is unclear — see e3.)
  10. Composition-mode enum (replace/add/subtract/modify_surface) → not adopted; each op owns its transform.
  11. Water "replace" mode → rejected in favour of flood.
  12. Fixed-footprint vs flexible definitions (grid: inherit vs grid: fixed: [2,3]) → rejected: just parameters with defaults.
  13. Non-overridable parameters → rejected ("creative opportunities").
  14. Scripted plugin language for library extensions → deprioritized (PRs).
  15. Next step = material/colour declaration syntax → declined ("Nah, that's fine").

(d) Taste rulings from memory

Floors Floors = walls laid flat (any family × {wall, floor}) Old grid stamper
Clifflab Straight top edge intended; massive blocks; ceilings fixed by construction (seat from bed); overhang limit 55° from vertical ("45 is too conservative"); stones shrink to touch tile edge, never clipped ("clipping is bad"); seams read as grooves; standard end section adequate; strong grain Voronoi "flake" chips (hated); stricter end-zone rules; stretched grain (cloth folds); cracks hugging block edges (upholstered welt)
Renders / QA Paint overhang faces red in every render incl. final painted; judge by eye in MeshLab/Bambu; render from spec not STL Waiting for renders (dislikes); numeric-only overhang reports

Relevant points from the four docs

  • tile-design-guide.md: edge-anchor compatibility contract — boundary endpoints at normalized edge coords (0.5; 0.33/0.67 for corridors) must match across tiles "even when the path is organic"; canonical orientations, rotate the print instead of new specs; filename terrain order water < soil < grass. → The language needs pinned-endpoint organic lines and probably an explicit edge contract concept.
  • build-provenance-spec.md: embed immutable BuildInfo (version, commit, dirty, build timestamp) at build time. → Determinism is "same definition + seed + generator version"; outputs (STL/3MF metadata) should record generator provenance + seed.
  • browser-python-viability.md: manifold-3d exists as WASM (npm) and is the CSG workhorse; OpenLOCK base needs CSG; recommends desktop shell (PyWebView / Tauri) for distribution. Consistent with a Rust core + Tauri webview UI.
  • cloud-mesh-design.md: tree tubes built watertight by construction (shared rings at forks, caps only at root/tips, no booleans). → Swept geometry as a first-class representation; "by construction, not by boolean cleanup" is a house value.

(e) Critical observations (MINE — not from the conversation)

e1. "Everything in virtual fractions" collides with printability. Shawn's own rulings are in physical units: 0.45 mm lines × 0.2 mm layers (grass strokes), 0.1 mm joints, 55° overhang, ~2 mm water relief. And because S_h and S_v are independent, any non-1:1 ratio shears angles: a 55° overhang authored in logical units is a different physical angle at 25 mm vs 35 mm squares. Engine needs (i) a printer-profile unit (mm / line-width / layer) for fine detail and (ii) a rule that solid objects (rocks, trees, blades) are built in isotropic physical space and only placed via logical coords. Variability in z-units is fine; detail geometry isn't.

e2. Distributions on Bézier control points + fill points = per-seed region flips. If control points are random, the fill point can land on the other side for some seeds — region silently changes. Needs: fill points expressible relative to lines (e.g. "side of line L at t"), or per-seed validation that is an error, or a persistent face identity. ChatGPT flagged the edit case, not the random-sampling case, which is worse.

e3. Strips/bands have no home in the pure paint-bucket model. A road, shore strip or wall footprint needs two derived offset lines. Shawn earlier listed "inside boundary width" and "perimeter operations based on the line intersection points being addressable lines" — so derived lines (offset(L, w), segment of L between intersections) are needed as line constructors, else booleans creep back. The current Python Boundary(width_mm>0) strip is exactly this case.

e4. Ops need non-geometric channels. Today's pipeline threads terrain_support_z, vegetation_support_z, obstacle_mask, grass_mask — grass steers around rocks/trees placed earlier; swell-chaos water needs emitter type by object (land vs rock) and geodesic distance through water. The lens/query interface must expose occupancy, provenance ("which op/material made this"), and support surfaces — not just inside/ray/normal/elevation.

e5. "Restricted view" vs "unbounded output" contradict until defined. Proposal to pin down: reads unrestricted (or region-prism + margin), additive writes unrestricted, subtractive/reshaping writes clipped to region prism. Also: does a tree's canopy overhanging a later region count as obstacle for that region's grass?

e6. Water flood semantics: per column over the whole tile (revision 2). Shawn's own model (L2293, L2345) is water laid over the whole tile, with land raised or lowered across a boundary, so not "fill below level within region". waterlab floods per column unconfined (z = max(land, water), waterlab.py:1050); shorelab confines to the per-column wet mask dilated 0.6 mm (shorelab.py:245). No lab uses 3D connectivity. Undercuts don't fill. Water also has output semantics (it's a printed solid with its own material) and depends on edge profiles matching neighbours.

e7. Seed derivation by "position in the operation stack" (ChatGPT) is fragile. Inserting an op rerolls every later sibling — the exact thing the memory warns about (mesh-hash invariants, "would reroll every stone"). Derive from a stable key: parent seed + op id/name (+ instance index), with explicit seed: pin.

e8. Spatial random fields ≠ random variables. Variability is a field over the area (with spatial scale, envelope up/down); distributions are scalars sampled per invocation. The type system needs both (a Field type, sampled lazily, seeded) — the conversation blurs them.

e9. Evaluation is mostly non-local in practice. Grass field sim, Poisson leaf/rock placement, clifflab gravity stacking, geodesic water, blur-remesh, manifold unions are all global within the op. That argues for eager per-op materialized snapshots behind a query interface, with the deferred graph used for caching/invalidation and preview LOD — not pull-based point evaluation.

e10. Materials through booleans. Material identity must survive CSG and remeshing. Manifold's originalID/property channels can carry it for meshes; SDF/marching-cubes paths (blur-remesh, plane-SDF blocks) lose face identity unless a material field is co-sampled. Decide early.

e11. 3D selection is missing. Regions are 2D; walls, cliffs and texture ops need face/orientation/exposure selectors (the memory notes walls became separate solids precisely because a heightmap can't texture vertical faces).

e12. Underspecified basics: axis naming/handedness and which way rows run; whether (row,col) integer addresses are (r,c) or (x,y); expression language scope (references to sibling params → dependency order + cycle detection); STL import scaling under S_h ≠ S_v; connector minimum-thickness rules per system (OpenLOCK vs DB) and how carve validates them; multi-object STLs (slot leaves are separate objects in one STL per walls ruling); edge-contract matching for seamless neighbours.

notes-library.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Library analysis notes — input to the Rust-rewrite synthesis

Scope: src/dharmatiles/ (Python, ~20.6 k LOC), tile specs (src/tiles/**, 43; plus 23 experiment specs in docs/walls, docs/rocks), design docs. Read-only analysis, 2026-11-01. All paths relative to src/dharmatiles/ unless noted. Guiding filter: simple >> normal >> elegant >> expressive >> feature-rich >> performant.

CLAUDE.md is stale in several places (verified against code): - cells_per_square default is 128, not 256 (core/config.py SurfaceConfig). - stone/relief_field, aged_relief, grass/growers/flat.py, grass/layer.py, core/terrain.py do not exist. Relief is stone/finish.py:stone_relief; grower is grass/grower.py:FlatGrassGrower. - Grass is no longer the field simulation: it is a shim over grass/thatch.py:ThatchGrass that ignores species and placement (scatter/prototype.py:121-138). The old sim survives as FloppyGrass (unused by any tile). - GrassCarpet no longer embosses 2D stamps into terrain_z; it adds noise into terrain_z and returns flat 3D blade-tube meshes (layers/grass_carpet.py). - Water does not reshape the pool floor; floor = 0 mm comes from the IDW terrain pass. No "quadratic slope into water zones" exists. - Colour IS encoded: core/color.py:export_color_stl writes RGB15 face attributes; 3MF export also. - trees/mesh.py module docstring says edge solids are manifold-unioned; code concatenates (_union_edge_solids, trees/mesh.py:547).

1. Architecture as implemented

1.1 Spec language (spec.py, 344 LOC)

  • .tile.py = real Python module; must bind tile: Tile or tiles: list[Tile]. load_tile() (spec.py:~270) creates a synthetic package per directory so specs can import siblings (src/tiles/shared/species.py).
  • Tile(surface: SurfaceConfig, areas: list[Region|Boundary], systems=[DungeonBlocks(), OpenLOCK()]).
  • Region(id, selector: FloodFill, layers, height_mm=None); height_mm=None ⇒ first layer's class attr height_default_mm (5.0 for everything except Water 3.0).
  • Boundary(id, from_anchor, to_anchor, path='organic'|'straight', waypoints, amplitude_mm=3.6, wavelength_mm=10.0, detail_fraction=0.25, seed_offset=0, width_mm=0.0, layers=[]); layers require width_mm>0.
  • Edge.TOP(t) etc. → Anchor(edge, t); FloodFill(x,y) or FloodFill((x,y),…) normalized seeds.
  • TileLayer protocol: height_default_mm: ClassVar[float]; apply(scene, *, placement_mask) -> list[Trimesh]. Optional terrain_material: ClassVar[Material] colours the terrain faces of that region (first layer with the attr wins, terrains/tile.py:_color_terrain_faces).
  • repeat_sizes(base, [(c,r),…]) — shallow dataclasses.replace on surface. Unused by any tile.
  • dist.py (185 LOC) — D[a:b] uniform, .power(k), D.triangular, D.normal, D.fixed, WeightedChoice; sample(value, rng) accepts plain numbers. Used pervasively as Sample[T]. This is a good, small mechanism — keep.

1.2 Orchestrator (terrains/tile.py, 1535 LOC; ~500 are pipeline, rest = CLI, parallel

batch, PNG render, 3MF/PDF catalog, rich UI) build_tile_from_spec (:510): 1. Region mask per distinct grid shape (_get_region_mask, cached by (grid_h, grid_w)). 2. For each system in tile.systems: sys_surface = system.surface_for(surface); content is cached by (square_mm, grid_h, grid_w); _build_tile_content(sys_tile, …); then system.export(...). _build_tile_content (:345): - terrain_z, water_surface_mm = _build_spec_terrain(...) (:656). - TileScene(terrain_z, terrain_support_z=copy, obstacle_mask=zeros, region_mask, water_surface_mm). - Walk tile.areas in order. Region ⇒ mask = region_mask == idx. Boundary with layers ⇒ placement_mask = boundary_mask = (region_mask < 0) (:376, :399) — ALL boundary cells and unassigned (-2) cells, not this boundary's strip (see §4 bugs). - Terrain solid: make_heightmap_solid(scene.terrain_z, tile_w, tile_h, base_h, error_threshold=0.02, simplify_stride=16). - Group parts by mesh.metadata['material']. _NO_UNION = {GRASS, FLOWER, WOOD, FOLIAGE, LEAF} ∪ DEBUG are concatenated; others (SOIL terrain, ROCK, WATER, BASE) get trimesh.boolean.union(engine='manifold') of volume parts + concatenation of non-volumes. - If water: _carve_soil_from_water — water slab (full tile) minus soil solid via manifold3d (:267). - TileContent(colored_meshes, scene).

1.3 Terrain heightfield (_build_spec_terrain, terrains/tile.py:656-716)

  • Water regions contribute 0 mm (pool floor); their height_mm is stored as water_surface_mm[idx].
  • If all region heights equal ⇒ constant field.
  • Else: region cells get exact height; only boundary cells (region_mask<0) get IDW: w = 1/(edt(region_mask != idx) + 0.5) per region, z = Σ h·w / Σ w (:711-714). ⇒ slopes exist only inside width_mm>0 boundary strips (or the 1-cell zero-width line). One distance transform per region (O(R·N)).

1.4 Region mask (core/region.py, 224 LOC)

  • boundary_path_mm: control points = anchor, waypoints, anchor; 4000 samples by arclength. 'organic': perpendicular offset = natural CubicSpline through random knots (n_knots ≈ len/wavelength + 3, endpoints pinned), × taper sin(π·local_t)^0.75 pinned at every control point, renormalized to amplitude_mm; plus a 4× frequency detail layer at detail_fraction. RNG: default_rng(surface.seed ^ spec.seed_offset ^ 0xB04DA7).
  • _rasterise: point-sample 4000 samples into cells (no Bresenham, despite CLAUDE.md). Width>0: EDT of the line ≤ half width.
  • build_region_mask: scipy.ndimage.label(mask == -2); each region's seeds pick a connected component (first seed wins; warn on collision). Values: -2 unassigned, -1 boundary, N region.
  • This is the "paint bucket from named lines" model already, minus: lines are not reusable by name, the tile perimeter is implicit, and boundaries are simultaneously dividing lines AND areas with their own layers.

1.5 TileScene (core/tile.py, 177 LOC) — mutable bag of grids

field type writers
terrain_z (H,W) f64 displace_terrain/set_terrain (Soil, GrassCarpet, Thatch mounds+skirt); direct writes: scatter/stones.py:745-771 (seal+skirt), walls/masonry.py:1533 (laid_flat film)
terrain_support_z (H,W) reset to terrain_z by every displace_terrain (core/tile.py:137) — wipes prior stamps; raised by _stamp_stone, CutStoneWall._stamp, _stamp_tree (soft exp falloff), _stamp_flower
vegetation_support_z (H,W) grass only; GrassCarpet resets it to terrain_support_z.copy(); thatch rebases it; _stamp_segment raises it
obstacle_mask (H,W) bool stones, walls, trees (r=max(1.8·root_r,1.6)), flowers
region_mask (H,W) int32 orchestrator
water_surface_mm dict[int,float] orchestrator; read by Water
- derive_seed(master, label, layer_idx=0) = blake2s(4 bytes) — the intended seed mechanism.
- Docstring documents a layer contract (only-raise support, never write terrain_z directly) that
stones and walls violate.

1.6 Heightmap solidification (core/mesh.py, 301 LOC)

  • Default path _make_heightmap_solid_adaptive: all perimeter cells + coarse stride-16 grid + cells with |∇²z| > 0.02 mm → scipy.spatial.Delaunay top; vertical side walls from the full perimeter ring; bottom = fan from centre at -base_h (base_h default 0.0 ⇒ bottom at z=0).
  • Uniform path (full grid quads, omit_top_mask, subsample) is only reachable with terrain_simplify_threshold=None; omit_top_mask/subsample are used only by src/scripts/archived/* → dead parameters. _rdp_edge is dead.
  • Vertex x = i · tile_w/(n-1) (:69) — see grid-convention inconsistency §4.

1.7 Bases & systems (systems.py 146, bases/ 309)

  • DungeonBlocks.surface_for = identity; OpenLOCK.surface_for = square_mm=25.4, cells_per_square=min(cps,64) when square_mm<30; BareSystem = no base.
  • DB base (bases/dungeonblocks.py:make_base): per square a 4-ring prismatoid (col_size 26, col_bevel 1.5, flare_height 5.2, peg 5.7 short / 11.4 tall, auto-tall when max Z of all meshes > auto_threshold_mm 15.0), minus logo inset (manifold3d), unioned.
  • OL base (bases/openlock.py): manifold3d box WALL_HEIGHT 8.0 minus T-slot sockets (_T_POLY, widths 14/12/10, depths 2/5/7) at CUTOUT_START_Z 1.4, CUTOUT_HEIGHT 4.2, retention slots (8.35 offset, 4.7 w, 9.0 d) on perimeter squares, minus logo 0.4 mm.
  • _attach_and_export: concat base + coloured meshes, stone.separate_pinches(combined), export_color_stl. Final STL is a concatenation of shells (not one manifold).

1.8 DB vs OL dual-scale rerun

  • The whole content pipeline reruns per distinct square_mm with the same layer instances (dataclasses.replace(tile, surface=…) is shallow). Feature sizes (blade widths, stone mm, wall top_mm) are absolute mm and do not scale; densities are _per_square; spines/anchors are in square units and do scale. Region mask recomputed at the OL grid (64 cps).
  • Layers are stateful during apply, but only one write leaks across runs: CutStoneWall.apply writes self.height_mm when None (walls/masonry.py:692), so the OL run inherits the DB-derived, seat-dependent wall height. The other attributes (_sq, _embed_eff, _op_profiles, _ruin_env, _merlons, _crenel_z) are reassigned before they are read on each run; FieldstoneWall clears _beds/_drifts/_leans at the start of every _cells (walls/fieldstone.py:305-307); StoneFloor resets spine/height/course/bay before every super().apply (walls/floor.py:84-95). Passes inside one wall still couple through self (_hearting_parts reads _op_profiles). Hidden coupling; pure apply(params, scene) -> scene' would remove it.

2. Subsystem algorithms

2.1 SoilCarpet (layers/soil.py, 312; SoilConfig 21 fields)

_compute_bump_field at 2× oversample: two tiers of super-Gaussian blobs exp(-d^P/2), P=blob_power 3.5, clipped at blob_cutoff 2.6σ, renormalized (g-g_R)/(1-g_R), max-composited (np.maximum) not summed. Placement per tier: cluster process (blob_cluster_count 30, spread 6 mm) OR jittered grid OR uniform. Primary: elliptical (blob_aspect), angular harmonic shape noise (blob_shape_noise_amp 0.06, 4 harmonics), height = σ × blob_h_scale D[0.14:1.12]; n_blobs 277/square. Plus two smoothed-white-noise textures (0.06 mm @0.27 mm, 0.03 mm @0.12 mm). Clamp ≥0; cosine edge fade. apply: zoom down, "arc-length reparameterisation" via cumulative slope stretch + map_coordinates, × nz, cosine fade against tile edge and edt(placement_mask), displace_terrain. Seed derive_seed(seed,'soil-blobs') — no layer identity (two SoilCarpets ⇒ identical bumps).

2.2 GrassCarpet (layers/grass_carpet.py, 312)

Noise base: 0.5·blur(σ=0.1mm)+0.5·blur(σ=0.4mm) white noise, normalized to [-1,0], field = noise_top_mm 0.5 + noise_amp 1.0 · n, cosine edge fade (_compute_edge_fade, tile edge shifted +1 cell), displace_terrain. Blades: grass.grow.plant_seeds (Voronoi Grouped), walk the curl trajectory _blade_step_geometry until leaving mask/obstacle; z = terrain + quarter-sine sink; mesh via FlatGrassGrower.build_mesh with thickness = 0.9 mm; concatenated, GRASS.

2.3 3D grass

Live: grass/thatch.py:ThatchGrass (509) — no growth sim. 1. Mound substrate: reuses layers.soil._compute_bump_field with a hard-coded SoilConfig (30 blobs σ 1.4–2.5 mm …), faded at mask (2.0 mm) and obstacles (2.5 mm); displace_terrain then manually restores terrain_support_z (works around §1.5 wipe). 2. Soil skirt: 0.8·exp(-(d/2.5)²) berm around obstacles. 3. support_eff = terrain + solid obstacle heights clamped at +_CLIFF_CLAMP_MM 4.0 (undoes the tree's soft falloff stamp, which is "not geometry"). 4. Direction field θ = blend(down-slope of blurred terrain (weight 0.6·clip(slope/0.2)), unit vector of two blurred white-noise fields σ=6 mm). 5. Roots: jittered grid at 3.0 mm, relocated off rocks along ∇edt, sorted lowest-first. Sheaves of 4–9 parallel blades, fan 1.0 mm; plus a "crowding ring" of tangential blades 0.2–3 mm from obstacles. 6. Walk each blade with constant curl; deflect along wall tangent when entering "wall" cells (rise > 0.9 mm); stop at mask edge. Drape z on blurred terrain or ride stone + 0.2 mm. 7. _build_draped: lift onto vegetation_support_z + 0.25 mm, truncate at blade_rise_cap, FlatGrassGrower.build_mesh, clamp verts to tile, push verts out of obstacle footprints along -∇edt(obstacle), _stamp_segment into vegetation_support_z. ~20 module constants are the real parameters (_SPACING_MM 3.0, _SHEAF_MIN/MAX 4/9, …). Legacy: FloppyGrass + grass/grow.py (226) + grass/mesh.py (93) + FlatGrassGrower.step — segment-by-segment growth against an occupancy grid; only plant_seeds survives (used by GrassCarpet). grass/grower.py (532): ring cross-section (1/2/N top facets), V-keel (keel_fraction 0.6), spine smoothing, _stamp_segment profile-aware rasterizer.

2.4 Scatter/distribute (scatter/distribute.py 373, scatter/config.py 64)

  • Uniform(count_per_square=None, gap_mm=2.0, seed=0): count or area/(footprint+gap)²; positions = random allowed cell + sub-cell jitter, clamped to footprint margin. No overlap rejection.
  • Grouped(groups_per_square=3, count_per_square=None, gap_mm=0.3, group_dir_mode='random', seed=0): random_spread_sites (sampled farthest-point), nearest_site_labels (brute Voronoi over cells), jitter_grid_xy in a rotated frame with a (1-t)² density ramp toward the "source edge".
  • Used by: Rocks/Trees/Flowers (Uniform), GrassCarpet (Grouped), StoneField (both).

2.5 Stones (scatter/stones.py 889 + stone/ 726)

Rocks (scatter/prototype.py) maps legacy _RocksConfig (r, aspect, flat, angle) onto StoneSpec (17 fields); n_cuts, cut, roughness, sink, az_segs, el_segs accepted but ignored. build_stone (:371): 1. Convex hull of M (facets) Fibonacci-sphere points, tangent-jittered (0.35·spacing), lumpiness ±0.12, egg taper (widest at t=-0.25, top shrink 0.35), optional crown flatten; _seat_rotation. 2. Identify largest upward facet as protected "hero" face. 3. _weather_bites (dish + spall blobs) computed; round_edges if roundover ≤0.8. 4. Footprint ≥4 mm: subdivide, low-freq fBm warp (4% of footprint); if roundover ≤0.15: blur_remesh(σ=0.9) (fallback Taubin) + stone_relief. 5. Apply bites via manifold difference (check survives_stl32). 6. roundover >0.15: blur_remesh(σ=1.2) + Taubin 16–30 iters + one spall scar (manifold difference, euler_number==2 check) + stone_relief with env + hero calm. 7. Overhang audit loop on v_conv (the pre-weathering hull): faces above ground+1 mm with nz < -0.72 and chord > 1.6 mm ⇒ reduce lean 2°, then burial +0.05 up to 1.40; warn if unsolved. The returned mesh is the weathered one ⇒ audit is of a proxy. _build_and_stamp: big→small; seat z = 80th percentile of terrain over disk; clip below _FLOOR_MM 0.3 (manifold intersection); _stamp_stone: 2D convex hull of mesh, top = min of upward hull planes, bottom = max of downward planes; seal terrain up to underside+0.15 (cap 1.3 + ramp), skirt smoothstep annulus 3 mm (cap 2.4); raise support; obstacle_mask |= inside; then engrave_cracks (stone/cracks.py: surface-projected random-walk wedge chain, boolean difference, fork once). All ROCK concatenated (later manifold-unioned by orchestrator). StoneField: cluster seats (3 batches of scatter positions, greedy farthest-point ≥11 mm), dominant + 0–3 companions from _CLASS_PARAMS table {lump, slab, shard}. FacetedStones: explicit list. Both unused by production tiles (used in docs/rocks experiments). stone/ primitives: fibonacci_sphere, rubble_stone (rough hull chip), rounded_box (hull of icospheres at inset corners), round_edges (rolling-ball hull with per-sample random radius), blur_remesh (voxelize→fill→Gaussian→marching cubes, pitch=clip(foot/56,0.18,0.32)), stone_relief (THE surface finish: Taubin-relaxed normals, curvature damping 1/(1+(cd/0.35)²), random rotation, carve -carve_mm·smoothstep((fbm-0.05)/0.35) + dish + grain 0.10 mm@1.5 mm + optional env/hero/base fade/refine), fbm/value_noise (hash lattice, smoothstep trilinear), clip_to_box, survives_stl32, separate_pinches (cKDTree pairs < 2e-4 mm nudged inward along normals 1.5e-4/3e-4, ≤4 iterations).

2.6 Walls (walls/ 3643)

Chassis = CutStoneWall (walls/masonry.py, 1564; 24 ctor params + 14 class-attr knobs). apply (:678): spine (square units) → _segments (right angles only) → seat z (80th pct of max(terrain, support) over footprint; "paved" ⇒ embed 0.25) → height = top_mm 33.1 − seat if None → _cells (_layout: course heights summing exactly to H; bay cuts with min-bond nudging, or running bond; corner ownership alternates per course = quoins) → crenellate → openings (_apply_openings + fit.fit_cells) → ruin envelope (2 sines) → core boxes (rounded_box, recessed by reveal_mm) minus opening passages → posed surround units → per-cell _place_block (_stone_box jitter → _unit_mesh → yaw/tilt) → _extra_parts (rubble hearting, foot rubble) → assemble_masonry (manifold union, tile clip, separate_pinches) → slot cuts → laid_flat bottom clip → _stamp (support = cap z over footprint box, obstacle). Unit kernel _block_mesh (:366): two jittered planar rings (chip pull per side, not per corner) → convex hull planes → SDF = τ·logsumexp(planes/τ) on a grid, marching cubes at level 0 (exact faces, LSE roundover τ≈0.7·roundover) + optional extra cut planes; pitch nudges ×1.073/0.941 on non-volume; then stone_relief (carve ≤0.62). _TEXTURES presets {chipped, worn, hewn, dressed} = (chip_mm, roundover_mm, relief_mm, relief_wl). Families (subclass hooks _cells, _stone_box, _unit_mesh, _place_block, _core_boxes, _extra_parts, _heart_cap + class attrs surround_*, hearting, yaw_max_deg …): - BrickWall (brick.py): small ranges, worn texture, missing_prob 0.04, spall_prob 0.10. - RegularBrickWall: bond='running', uniform brick L/H, near-zero wobble. Pure preset. - FieldstoneWall (777, 14 params + 32 constants): crack-network-first — shared bed wobble (2 sines, cached per (seg, zkey)), head-joint drift/bow; outline polygon via shapely buffer(-joint/2-r).buffer(r); outline noise; sphere-morph: K=17 latitude rings blending outline ↔ drifting poles via superellipse (1-|u|^a)^b; throughstone merges, h-splits, coping. roundover_mm is a tuple here but a float in the base class (type overloading). - StoneFloor (floor.py): CutStoneWall(laid_flat=True) with spine = south edge, height = tile depth, course/bay = square/slabs_per_square, missing/spall/crack_prob. Laid-flat remaps (t, q, z) → (run, depth-down, plan) via _lay; seat writes terrain_z ≤ 0.15 directly. - Openings (openings.py 247, fit.py 401, leaf.py 363): Opening(at, width_mm, sill_mm, head_mm, head arch|lintel, rise_mm, profile auto|circle|points, surround auto|jambs|ring, leaf, slot); profile polygon in (t,z); surround units = _Posed rotated blocks (voussoirs = rotated blocks with taper); fit.fit_cells is a deterministic solver that cuts bond cells against the dilated forbidden region with one straight least-squares line per side (absorb / thin-end / short-brick fallbacks); cut lines become extra planes in the LSE kernel. Leaf = planks/bars/ portcullis solids, optionally hinged open; slot=True cuts a channel.

2.7 Water (layers/water.py, 665)

Height = explicit / water_surface_mm[region] / 3.0. Displacement = blurred white noise (3 mm, 1 mm RMS) × Hann mask falloff + "ripple" segments emitted from internal shoreline and stones-in-water (distance-transform with nearest-source indices, arc segments with Hann ends, amplitude decays with offset). Downsample to 128 cps. make_water_volume: its own grid mesher (gw+1 corner vertices, cell_x = tile_w/gw :542), flat bottom z=0, top = height+0.10+disp, tile-edge sine profile (_force_tile_edge_water_profile) so adjacent tiles match, manifold simplify. Full-tile slab; orchestrator subtracts the soil solid.

2.8 Trees (trees/ 5127)

Tree (trees/layer.py, 39 ctor params + BarkConfig 12): per placed position sample a CanopyEnvelope (axisymmetric; pointiness/curve end profiles, smooth-min), then grow_skeleton (trees/skeleton.py, 739): - attractors on the envelope side surface, area-weighted in z + golden-angle azimuth (_sample_canopy); - optional k-means (Lloyd, 20 iters, z-scaled) grouping (_voronoi_group_attractors) + per-group dome bulge; - _branch_skeleton BFS: lookahead from next_pos, stray = outside split cone (eagerness·cos(split_angle)) OR below target_fdm_angle_deg 35; strays spawn sub-branches before stepping (group-level, else PCA 2-split _cluster_pca); single primary ⇒ _grow_to_leaf lands exactly on attractor; step budget force-split; - _simplify_skeleton, _compute_radii_bottom_up (pipe model, (Σ r^e)^(1/e), e=3). build_branch_mesh (trees/mesh.py, 1488): per edge a closed cubic-Bezier tube with parallel-transported rings (12 sides, 48 with bark), bark = grooved lines advanced through the tree with twist/wave/noise; terminal edges get a foliage cluster (now a structured rounded-cone ring sweep, trees/mesh.py:780-790, giving cap+cone+dome, per-position Gaussian hash noise 0.10 mm + coarse value noise 1.0 mm @4 mm); strict_fdm_angle_deg 26 check = warning only. All edge solids concatenated (union too slow). Leaves (placement_organic.py 620 + placement_leaf.py 770 + leaf.py 913): manifold-union the clusters into one surface → area-weighted candidates (×10) → maximal Poisson-disk dart throw with a normal-aware 3D grid (_ROOT_BLOCK_COS 0.26) → direction = down-slope rotated by 3-sine positional field (±15°, 7 mm) → height-sorted shingle standoff (step 0.3, cap 1.2) → per-leaf generator pipeline (seat oval at equal depth, rigid blade frame, skew for tip clearance, belly-dip, tube-skewer cull) with batched embree ray / contains queries (_drive_batched) → solidify_leaf. Blade shape blends with normal z (_ORGANIC_ZONE_HI 0.30, LO -0.45, bare below -0.75). 26 module constants; a "sheet solidification" block is explicitly parked, not wired (placement_organic.py:~150). _stamp_tree: obstacle disk + soft support = terrain + H·exp(-(d-r)/5mm) (a hint for the dead grass sim; thatch has to clamp it away).

2.9 Flowers (scatter/flowers.py, 378; FlowerConfig 10)

Dome-on-column petals + centre; _stamp_flower ellipse stamping. Used by one tile.

3. Mechanism inventory — duplicates and near-duplicates

Mechanism Instances (file:function) Rust consolidation
Seeding / hashing (≥7) core/tile.py:derive_seed (blake2s); core/region.py seed ^ seed_offset ^ 0xB04DA7; scatter/stones.py spec.seed ^ 0x2CAFE / 0x3AB5 / 0x0DDA / 0x5EED; walls seed*1_000_003 + hash(cell.key), seed*31+hash, seed*61+hash (masonry.py:1090,1425, brick.py:80, floor.py:102,111, fieldstone.py:258,608); trees/_utils.py:_hash01 (FNV-1a+fmix64); trees/mesh.py:_hash01_int (copy of _hash01 without fmix!); stone/noise.py:_hash_lattice; per-layer derive_seed(...,label) with no layer index (soil-blobs, grass-carpet, thatch-grass, rocks-scatter idx always 0) one Seed::derive(parent, path) hierarchy (tile → definition → instance → feature key)

Seat height stones np.percentile(patch[disk], 80) (stones.py:690); walls _SEAT_PERCENTILE=80 (masonry.py:49,1542); trees bilinear at centre; flowers centre one seat(footprint, field, pct)
Placement / scatter (≥6) distribute._uniform_positions, _voronoi_positions + random_spread_sites + nearest_site_labels + jitter_grid_xy; soil tiers (cluster / jittered grid / uniform — re-implemented); thatch jittered grid (re-implemented); StoneField greedy farthest-point seats (re-implements random_spread_sites); masonry hearting staggered grid; tree _voronoi_group_attractors (k-means, 3D); leaf maximal Poisson disk one point-process module: uniform, jittered-grid, Poisson-disk, cluster (parent/child), farthest-point; all mask-aware
Golden-angle / Fibonacci stone/shape.fibonacci_sphere; trees/skeleton._sample_canopy golden-angle azimuth one fn
Rounding / smoothing (5 methods) stone/shape.round_edges (rolling-ball hull), blur_remesh (voxel blur + MC), _block_mesh LSE-SDF + MC, Taubin (stones.py, stone_relief), shapely buffer(−r).buffer(r) (fieldstone 2D) SDF + one mesher would cover hull rounding, blur remesh, LSE blocks
Marching cubes stone/surface.blur_remesh, walls/masonry._block_mesh (skimage both) one SDF→mesh
Overhang / printability checks (≥6, inconsistent thresholds) stones audit nz < -0.72 (≈44°) + chord 1.6 (stones.py _OVERHANG_*) on the proxy hull; tree skeleton target 35° / strict 26° elevation (warn only); leaf placer normal-z zones (0.30 / -0.45 / -0.75) + tip skew margin; thatch slope gates (_ROCK_TIP_RISE_MM); walls fit min keep / block lips; clifflab 55° (outside lib). Overhang render policy paints faces red in renderers. one overhang(mesh, max_angle, min_chord) analysis on the final mesh + a declared printability budget
Watertight / repair fix_normals in 14 files; separate_pinches (systems.py final + assemble_masonry); survives_stl32 (stones bites, cracks); clip_to_box (stones floor, walls tile clip, floor bottom); try/except manifold difference with watertight fallback (stones ×2, cracks); blur_remesh returns None → fallback one robust boolean kernel (manifold) and one export guard
trimesh↔manifold conversion terrains/tile._to_m3d, bases/dungeonblocks, bases/openlock, layers/water._simplify, core/logo goes away with a native mesh type
Heightfield → solid meshers (3) core/mesh uniform; core/mesh adaptive Delaunay; layers/water.make_water_volume (corner grid + simplify) one
Grid conventions (3, inconsistent) sampling x = i·cell_w (core/grid.sample_grid, make_xy_grids); terrain mesh x = i·tile_w/(n-1) (core/mesh.py:69); water corners x = i·tile_w/n (water.py:542); thatch clamps to (gw-1)·cell_w (thatch.py:97,463) pick one (node-centred, n+1 nodes)
Grass mesh build loop grass/mesh.build_meshes vs grass/thatch._build_draped (documented "variant", copy) one
Config shapes dataclass configs (SoilConfig, SpeciesConfig, StoneSpec, BarkConfig, Opening); flat ctor kwargs (Tree 39, CutStoneWall 24); class-attr knobs (walls surround_*); module constants "while prototyping" (233 _UPPER constants total: stones 41, fieldstone 32, masonry 30, organic leaves 26, thatch 20, wall leaf 17) one parameter schema per definition, with presets
Per-family variants that are just presets RegularBrickWall (pure preset), StoneFloor (= laid_flat + grid pin), wall _TEXTURES, stone _CLASS_PARAMS, Grass vs ThatchGrass vs FloppyGrass, Rocks vs StoneField vs FacetedStones (three front-ends to _build_and_stamp); missing_prob/spall_prob duplicated in BrickWall and StoneFloor (_place_block overrides near-identical) parameters on one definition

4. Working well vs accidental complexity

Beautiful / keep the idea

  • Layer list in spec order + apply(scene, mask) — trivially understandable stack.
  • Paint-bucket regions: lines rasterized, scipy.label, seeds pick components. Already the proposed model; just needs named reusable lines and separation of "line" vs "strip area".
  • D[...] distribution DSL — tiny, composable, used everywhere.
  • stone_relief as THE single surface finish shared by rocks, blocks, fieldstone, slabs.
  • Masonry chassis + laid_flat: "a floor is a wall lying on its side" — one layout engine (courses × bays × quoins) serves walls, bricks, floors, drystone floors, openings.
  • LSE-SDF block kernel: convex planes → smooth max → MC; roundover and opening cut planes are the same mechanism ("cut = one more plane"). This is the seed of an SDF-first geometry core.
  • Fieldstone crack-network-first: shared curves guarantee complementarity by construction.
  • fit.py pure deterministic solver; openings as posed blocks (voussoir = rotated block).
  • By-construction guarantees culture (attractor-terminal invariants, maximal Poisson coverage, course heights summing exactly, tile-edge water profile for tiling).
  • Batched embree generator protocol (placement_leaf._drive_batched) — clean perf seam.
  • Material tags → grouped union vs concat policy; colour STL + 3MF.

Accidental complexity / leaky abstractions

  1. TileScene is a mutable bag of 2.5D grids with a documented contract that layers violate: displace_terrain resets all of terrain_support_z to terrain_z (core/tile.py:137), so any terrain-texture layer after an obstacle layer silently erases rock/wall/tree stamps (thatch copies old_support and restores it twice). _stamp_stone and walls laid_flat write terrain_z directly (stones.py:745-771, masonry.py:1533).
  2. terrain_support_z has two meanings: solid geometry top AND soft "grass may grow tall here" hint (_stamp_tree exp falloff). Thatch needs _CLIFF_CLAMP_MM + support_eff to undo it.
  3. Heightfield-only obstacle model: obstacle_mask + support z cannot represent overhangs, arches, tree canopies, wall openings; everything is 2.5D except final meshes. Slopes ignored ("locally horizontal" assumption, TileScene comment block lists planned terrain_normal).
  4. Ordering contract is implicit: Rocks before Grass, Soil before stones, StoneFloor before walls (walls read support to seat on pavement), Water after rocks (ripple sources). Nothing checks it.
  5. Boundary layers get the union of ALL boundary cells (terrains/tile.py:376,399) incl. unassigned −2. With two shore boundaries (e.g. 1x1-water-corridor+grass), each boundary's SoilCarpet + Rocks runs over both strips; with the label-only seeds both runs produce the same blobs/stone positions → doubled soil and duplicate stones (second set re-seated on the first's sealed terrain).
  6. Seeds lack layer identity (see §3): two SoilCarpets, two Rocks, two Grass layers share RNG streams; only Uniform.seed/walls seed= distinguish them manually.
  7. Ignored parameters accepted silently: Grass(species, placement) (30 uses in tiles!), Rocks(n_cuts, cut, roughness, sink, az_segs, el_segs); repeat_sizes, tiles= unused.
  8. Stateful layer objects mutated during apply, then reused for the OL rerun (§1.8).
  9. Three grid conventions (§3) — off-by-one-cell drift between sampling, terrain mesh, water mesh.
  10. Overhang audit on a proxy (v_conv pre-weathering hull) while shipping the weathered mesh; tree FDM failure is warning-only; checks scattered with different angles.
  11. Water as full slab minus soil boolean + its own mesher + tile-edge sine profile special case.
  12. Material grouping/union policy in the orchestrator knows per-material perf hacks (_NO_UNION), soil special case for terrain colours, water carve special case.
  13. CLI/infra mass in terrains/tile.py (parallel batch, rich live UI, PNG, PDF catalog, 3MF) — ~1000 LOC mixed with the 500-LOC pipeline.
  14. Prototype knobs as module constants (233) — the real parameter surface is invisible to specs.
  15. Exceptions → silent fallbacks (blur_remesh None, boolean try/except "left unbitten", clip_to_box "left unclipped") — output quality depends on which fallback fired.

Dead / superseded code

  • FloppyGrass + grass/grow.grow_all + grass/mesh.build_meshes + FlatGrassGrower.step
  • _leading_edge_cells etc. (only plant_seeds used, by GrassCarpet); GrassConfig exists only to thread species into these. grass/config.py is a re-export shim.
  • _RocksConfig legacy fields; _GrassUnderlayConfig is just GrassCarpet's ctor in a dataclass.
  • core/mesh.py: _rdp_edge, uniform path's omit_top_mask, subsample (archived scripts only).
  • trees/placement_leaf._points_inside_any, _project_to_surface (scalar wrappers), trees/mesh._rotate_vec, _two_perp; parked sheet-solidification constants in placement_organic.
  • Material.DEBUG_COLOR_* docstring says 12 slots, enum has 7.
  • src/scripts/archived/, docs/meta/history — history only.
  • StoneField, FacetedStones used only in docs/rocks/*.tile.py experiments.

Relation to the "definitions + regions + stack" idea

  • Regions: already paint-bucket over rasterized lines; missing: named reusable lines, implicit perimeter, multiple fill points as union (exists: FloodFill((..),(..))), lines as first-class (Boundary conflates line + strip area + layer host + IDW slope source).
  • Stack: already ordered per area, but per-area stacks are interleaved by area order; a single global stack with each element declaring its region would make cross-area ordering explicit and would fix the boundary-mask bug.
  • Definitions: today = Python classes + presets (SpeciesConfig shared via import, src/tiles/shared/species.py; wall _TEXTURES; stone _CLASS_PARAMS). Derived seeds would replace the 7 ad-hoc seeding schemes.
  • "Accumulated geometry" today = 4 grids + list of meshes; the stack ops read grids, not meshes. A Rust core that keeps an SDF/mesh scene (with a derived heightfield cache) would remove the 2.5D obstacle limits and the stamping duplication.

5. Geometry representations and libraries

Representation Where
2D heightfield grids (f64, 128 cps DB / 64 OL) TileScene terrain_z, support, vegetation, obstacle bool, region int32; soil/carpet/thatch/water fields; all stamping
2D distance transforms (scipy distance_transform_edt) region strips, IDW, fades, thatch, stones seal/skirt, water ripple sources (7 files)
Polylines / 2D polygons (shapely) boundaries (numpy+CubicSpline); wall spines, opening profiles, fit regions, fieldstone outlines (shapely buffer/segmentize/union)
Triangle meshes (trimesh) every part; convex hulls (qhull via trimesh/scipy) for stones, blocks, rounded_box, round_edges
Mesh booleans (manifold3d via trimesh.boolean(engine='manifold') and direct m3d) material-group union, water−soil, stone bites/scars/cracks/floor clip, walls union/clip/slots/opening cuts/leaves, foliage cluster union, DB/OL bases, logo, water simplify
Voxels + Gaussian + marching cubes (trimesh voxelize, scipy, skimage) blur_remesh (stones)
Sampled SDF + marching cubes _block_mesh LSE of planes (walls/floors) — the only SDF
Ray casts / containment (trimesh + embreex) leaf placer (batched), project-to-surface, contains
2D Delaunay (scipy) adaptive terrain mesh; fieldstone (1 use)
KD-tree (scipy cKDTree) separate_pinches
Skeleton graph (nodes/parents arrays) + cubic Bezier tubes trees
Rendering render.py pyrender off-screen PNG; PIL catalog PDF
Libraries: numpy, scipy (ndimage, spatial, interpolate), trimesh, manifold3d, shapely, scikit-image,
embreex (a required dependency, pyproject.toml:13; big perf), pyrender, PIL, rich. No numba/C extensions of its own.
