notes-water-shore-dirt.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Rust-rewrite groundwork — waterlab, shorelab, dirtlab

Analysis 2026-10-31, read-only. Code at HEAD 803ba4f. Paths are relative to the repo root; file:line refers to the current files.

All three labs are standalone top-level scripts (<lab>/<lab>.py, plus REQUIREMENTS.md, DESIGN.md and a gitignored out/). They import each other by sys.path.insert and reuse a handful of dharmatiles primitives (core.mesh.make_heightmap_solid, bases.dungeonblocks, stone.noise.fbm, stone.solidify.separate_pinches, render.render). The working method is the same in all three: generate, render a timestamped contact sheet (painted, oblique, top and low views), let Shawn judge, and log each iteration in DESIGN.md.

Dependency graph (labs only):

dirtlab ──imports──► rocklab (make_rock, to_manifold, to_trimesh)
waterlab ─imports──► rocklab, dirtlab (lazy, in build())
shorelab ─imports──► grasslab, rocklab, meadowlab, waterlab   (grasslab → dirtlab)
dirtlab.dirt_relief ◄── grasslab, rocklab (lazy, circular), waterlab, clifflab{,rockmass,rockpile}

1. waterlab — fantasy water (waterlab/waterlab.py, 1125 lines; contact_sheet.py)

1.1 Pipeline (the winning generator, swell-chaos)

The scene is built on a 0.1 mm grid (701², CELL_MM 0.1, :30). The output is z = max(land, water).

  1. Scene (Scene.__init__ :279). - Land is the union of per-side banks, s = max(pos − dist). - The shoreline wobble is a 1-D Gaussian-filtered noise (σ 9 mm) at inset 17 mm, or 14 mm when land is on several sides (MULTI_SHORE_MM :260). - land = (WL − BED_DEPTH) + (LAND_TOP − WL + BED_DEPTH)·smoothstep(s/BANK_W) plus 0.25 mm noise. Constants: WL 2.6, LAND_TOP 6.0, BANK_W 7, BED_DEPTH 2.2. - Obstacles (make_obstacle :263) are a rocklab rock, a cube or a pyramid (Manifold hull), randomly yawed and sunk 1.4 mm below WL. Their footprint is Manifold.slice(WL) → polygons → matplotlib Path.contains_points (_footprint :357). - Scene.from_fields (:317) builds a scene from an external ground height field plus obstacle solids. It is the composition hook used by shorelab.
  2. Derive (_derive :335). - obstacle = land > WL ∪ rock footprint. - EDT with indices gives the distance and nearest-source point. The distance is smoothed inside water only by normalised convolution, blur(d·w)/blur(w), σ 1 mm. - rim = smoothstep(dist_to_tile_edge / 4 mm). - tide = tide_field (:375).
  3. Tide (tide_field :375). - Interior: band-limited noise, σ 9 mm, RMS 0.35 mm. - Within 9 mm of the edge it is blended to the shared edge sine 0.6·sin(2π(t − (17.5 − λ/4))/λ) with λ = 17.5 mm. The two edge profiles are inverse-distance weighted at corners. - This is the same phase convention as layers/water.py::_force_tile_edge_water_profile (water.py:174), so any two water tiles butt.
  4. Emitter sources (_emit_sources :719). - Land and rock each get a geodesic distance through water: Dijkstra on an 8-connected sparse graph whose grid is really 0.2 mm (f = round(0.25/0.1) = 2, :723) while the edge weights still use 0.25 (:743, :678), so land and rock distances are 1.25× too large (_geodesic :660, scipy csgraph); a faithful port keeps the factor or retunes the bands. Upsampled ×2 and smoothed inside water (σ 1.2 mm). - Land distance additionally goes through _round_offsets (:688), which blends toward blurred copies at σ ∈ {2, 4, 8, 16} mm with target σ = 0.45·d. Crests hug the shore, then round off with distance (a diffraction look). - The nearest-source coordinates are smoothed σ 2.5 mm, for the crest break gates. - The source tuple is (strength, decay, λ₀, growth, d, ny, nx). EMIT_SOURCES (:109): land (1.0, 34 mm), rock (0.55, 10 mm), edge (0.28, 50 mm, unused by swell-chaos).
  5. Corner and far emitters. - _corner_sources (:973): a radial emitter at each open-water tile corner (water fraction in a ±20-cell window clipped at the corner, effectively a 2×2 mm in-tile box, > 0.5; :981). CHAOS_CORNER = (0.30, 70 mm, λ 4, g 0.012), strength jitter ×U(0.8, 1.2). - _far_sources (:705): when there are fewer than 2 open corners (enclosed water), 3 emitters 220 mm away at random bearings (near-plane waves).
  6. Per-source wave packet (_swell_train :849). - n components with λ log-spaced over the type's band (jitter ±e^0.1). SWELL_BANDS :142: land 5–16 mm (g 0.012, n 4); rock 2.0–4.5 (g 0.035, n 3); corner 5–14 (n 3); reed 1.8–3.0 (n 2). - Each component gets:
    • coherence wobble wob = 1 + 0.06·min(d, 80)·(λ/λhi)^−0.3, so short waves decohere faster;
    • warped distance dw = d + wob·noise(σ = max(1.2λ, 2.5));
    • log phase ln(1 + g·dw)/(g·λ), so spacing widens outward;
    • amplitude (λ/λhi)^1.4.
    • Components are split at the band's geometric mid. Long components are sharpened 0.15 (they stay round); short ones 0.9. sharp = u' = 2.5·tanh(u/2.5); u' + k(u'² − 1)/2, a Stokes-like 2nd harmonic. The sum is normalised to unit RMS.
  7. Combine (gen_swell_chaos :877). - Each source's weight is w = st·exp(−d/decay). - Rock weight is ×1.15, plus a wake term ×(1 + 0.9·clip(∇d_land·∇d_rock, 0, 1)·e^(−d/(2.5·dec))). - Enclosure: f = clip((land share of tile perimeter − 0.4)/0.6), and land decay ×(1 − 0.75 f). - dominant = Σw⁸·tr / Σw⁸ (partition of unity: the strongest source rules). - chaos = Σw·tr / √Σw² (amplitude-preserving interference). - order = max over physical w; c = smoothstep((order − 0.12)/0.33). - z = (c·dominant + (1 − c)·chaos)·(0.7 + 0.3c), with the mean removed, ×0.8 mm. - Optional sc.reed_mask from shorelab: reed rings are added, not blended (a blend would drown them in the land waves). EDT from the stems, amplitude 0.45·e^(−d/4.5). - Soft trough floor: softplus at −1.1 mm (k 0.25). - Contact lip (:480): 0.3·exp(−((d − 0.6)/0.5)²), a meniscus.
  8. Build (build :1032). - water_z = WL + relief·rim·water·smoothstep(d/0.4) + tide. - Exposed land gets dirtlab.dirt_relief, faded in over WL + 0.1…0.6. sc.land stays plain, so the waves are unaffected. - z = max(land, water_z), then a Gaussian σ 0.6 cell to remove kinks. - make_heightmap_solid(subsample=2) gives a uniform 0.2 mm mesh (the adaptive mesher faceted the swells), then ∪ rock (manifold3d), ∪ the DB base (select_peg_height, make_base). - Keep the largest body, then the float32 + merge watertight check.
  9. RNG: mode_rng (:1069) seeds each generator with [seed, crc32(name)], so the order on the sheet doesn't change the results. The tide uses [seed, 0x71DE].

Other generators are kept in the code as references and rejects: legacy (:525, a port of make_water_displacement + make_water_ripple_displacement), rings (:484), combed/lapped strokes (:436, Poisson strokes along a direction field), sim (:557, a damped wave equation, 1400 steps), spectrum (:641), emitted, emitted-sum, emitted-chaos, and spectral-chaos (:819, using _band_train :798).

1.2 What makes it look good: Shawn's rulings

  • Loved: swell-chaos. "holy crap, that's amazing" (memory waterlab-direction). The ingredients:
  • waves come from emitters typed by object;
  • geodesic distance, so waves wrap obstacles;
  • a wavelength band per emitter type;
  • long components round, short ones sharp (amplitude ∝ λ^1.4);
  • coherence decays with distance, giving order near sources and non-directional chaos far away;
  • weak corner emitters fill open water;
  • about 2 mm relief over a shared tide.
  • Cube and pyramid shape-following was "perfect". Rounded land offsets fixed square ponds.
  • Rejected (failure modes W1–W8 in REQUIREMENTS §4):
  • cells/bowls, targets (concentric rings), wave rows/corduroy, swirls, busy lines, porridge, pasted decals, clones;
  • all-directions chop from the wave sim ("a whole bunch of spiky shit");
  • single-wavelength emitters, which read as too regular;
  • strokes as "worms/rice";
  • spectrum, which was lumpy;
  • pool-radius scaling of land reach, which broke bank-rock.
  • Don't reintroduce all-directions forcing.

1.3 Printability

  • The water is a single-valued height field, so it cannot overhang.
  • The rock band is ≥ 2 mm (minimum printable chop wavelength).
  • The trough softplus floor plus a bed at WL − 2.2 mm prevent terraces. max(land, water) used to clip troughs flat.
  • σ 0.6-cell smoothing of the final z.
  • Rim fade gives a calm, matching edge (REQ-W-9).
  • The watertight check after float32 merge raises on failure.
  • stats (:1075) reports relief range and maximum water slope (audit only, not enforced).

1.4 Parameters (module constants; CLI --mode --seed --scene --open --check)

Group Constants
Scene WL 2.6, LAND_TOP 6, SHORE_X 17, wobble 4, BANK_W 7, RIM_FADE 4, DIST_SMOOTH 1, BED_DEPTH 2.2, MESH_SUBSAMPLE 2
Tide σ 9, RMS 0.35, edge amplitude 0.6, λ 17.5, fade 9
Emitters EMIT_SOURCES, EMIT_SHARP 8, EMIT_WOBBLE_MM 1, CHAOS_WOBBLE_GROW 0.06, CHAOS_ORDER_LO/HI 0.12/0.45, CHAOS_FAR_AMP 0.7, CHAOS_CORNER
Swell SWELL_BANDS, AMP_EXP 1.4, LONG/SHORT sharpen 0.15/0.9, AMP 0.8, ROCK_GAIN 1.15, WAKE 0.9, TROUGH 1.1, ENCLOSE_FREE 0.4 / CUT 0.75, FAR_SRC 3 at 220 mm, ROUND_K 0.45, ROUND_SIGMAS (2, 4, 8, 16), REED amplitude 0.45 / decay 4.5
Lip 0.3 mm at d 0.6

1.5 Representations and libraries

  • 2-D float64 height fields on a regular grid: rows = y, both edges included.
  • Distance fields: EDT with indices, and geodesic Dijkstra on a 4× coarser grid.
  • manifold3d for the obstacles, their slice and the final union.
  • trimesh for export and the check.
  • scipy.ndimage, sparse.csgraph, matplotlib.path, PIL.
  • A thread-parallel bit-exact separable Gaussian (gaussian :203).

1.6 Mechanisms

Mechanism Where Classification
Band-limited noise (Gaussian-filtered white noise, normalised) noise :234 general. Duplicated in dirtlab :145, clifflab :222, grasslab _smooth_noise, layers/water.py
smoothstep smooth01 :229 general, duplicated in every lab
Masked normalised-convolution blur _derive, wsmooth :748 general ("blur within region")
EDT plus nearest-source indices _derive general distance transform
Geodesic distance through a passable mask _geodesic :660 general (Rust: fast marching or Dijkstra)
Distance-iso-line rounding _round_offsets :688 general field op ("rounded offsets")
Emitter → crest-train on a distance field _swell_train, _band_train water-specific, but generic as "periodic profile of a warped distance field" (also used by rings, legacy ripples)
Partition of unity w^p / interference Σw·f/√Σw² / order→chaos blend :903–927 general multi-source field blend
Edge-profile forcing for tileability tide_field general "tile-edge boundary condition" (dup of layers/water.py :174)
Soft floor (softplus) / soft max :942, ripple_strokes general
Heightfield solid → union → base → stl check build general export path (dups dirtlab assemble, grasslab stl_safe)
Painted preview by local relief (z − blur 2 mm / 6 mm) contact_sheet.py::paint_colors :71 general "drybrush paint" evaluator; same idea in dirtlab, shorelab
Poisson-disc darts poisson :239 (O(n²)) general scatter

1.7 Declarative generalisation

  • Water is a region filled by flood to a level (WL) over the ground, with a surface relief op.
  • Emitters should be derived, not authored. Every obstacle region contributes an emitter whose (strength, decay, band, growth) comes from its type (land / rock / reed / corner / far). Defaults live in a type table, overridable per instance.
  • Proposed op sequence: 1. water(level=2.6, bed=-2.2) 2. distance(geodesic, through=water, from=obstacles) 3. emit(bands by type) 4. combine(partition_p=8, chaos_lo/hi) 5. soft_floor 6. edge_profile(tide)
  • Enclosure and far-emitter fallback are rules on the region topology: derived, not configured.
  • Seeds derive from (tile seed, path of the op), as mode_rng already does with crc32(name).
  • The shorelab hooks (from_fields, reed_mask) show the needed interface: water takes any ground field plus any obstacle set, plus "point emitters" from other layers.

2. shorelab — meadow meets water (shorelab/shorelab.py, 462 lines)

A pure composition lab. It owns only the bank, the mud-strip mask, shore steering, reeds and the water basin, and imports grasslab, rocklab, meadowlab and waterlab unchanged. It applies one monkeypatch: WATER_OVERRIDES sets wl.SWELL_SHORT_SHARPEN = 0.45 (:79–83).

2.1 Pipeline (build :268, assemble :316)

  1. Rocks are scattered and posed on a worker thread with a separate RNG (seed + ml.ROCK_SEED_OFFSET, :272), using rl.scatter and rl.pose.
  2. Ground (make_shore_ground :92). - Start from grasslab's rises-only hills (gl.make_ground). - The shoreline is x_s(y) = 33 + 11·n₁₄(y) + 5·n₅.₅(y). Each wobble is normalised to 2σ and clipped, not to its max, so one peak can't flatten the rest. - G = BED + (hills − BED)·smoothstep((s + 3)/8) with BED 0.4. The waterline appears where G crosses WL 2.6, so it follows the hills.
  3. Flow and swaths (gl.make_direction, gl.Swaths). Rock footprint masks come from ml.footprint_masks.
  4. Soil: free lenses via gl.place_lenses(land_only(ground)) (:134, water scores 99 so it is never chosen), plus the ShoreSoil mud strip (:112). - Strip width = 4·clip(0.5n + 0, 0, 1)^0.8, with n a noise of σ 9 mm. It is zero along part of the shore (avoids "moat" S3 and "lawn edge" S2). - mask = smoothstep(0.5 + (width − EDT_to_water)/edge).
  5. Flow: gl.finish_flow wraps around lenses and rock circles, then steer_shore (:143). - Within 9 mm of the water, the heading angle φ relative to "toward water" is limited: angles beyond 80° are compressed by the weight w, then everything is pulled ×(1 − 0.3w). - The rotation delta is Gaussian-smoothed (σ 1.5 mm). - Clone of grasslab steer_rim.
  6. Grass: gl.build_grass(blocker = water ∪ rock footprints) (stroke blades since grasslab v3).
  7. Rocks: ml.seat_rocks, with the effective top over water = WL + 0.6, so wet rocks emerge. Wet rocks (centre in water) are re-seated separately; they become waterlab obstacles.
  8. Reeds (make_reeds :164). - 3–5 clumps, rejection-sampled where G − WL ∈ (−1.1, 0.2), at least 12 mm apart. - 5–9 blades per clump. Each blade:
    • a lemon cross-section, (hw·cos t, ht·sign(sin t)|sin t|^1.4), 24 points;
    • Manifold.extrude with 48 divisions;
    • warp_batch taper: linear 1 → ts, then an ogive √(1 − u²) cap of length 2.2·hw·ts, minimum 0.03 (no seam, no flat top);
    • flat face rotated toward the clump centre;
    • a second warp_batch bend offset = k·z², with k = tan(tip)/(2h) and tip angle = fan 14–34° plus 8° downhill;
    • rooted 0.8 mm below the ground.
    • Union by batch_boolean.
    • The stem base circles feed waterlab's reed_mask, which adds ripple rings.
  9. Water (build_water :227). - wl.Scene.from_fields(G on the 0.1 mm grid, wet_rocks), then gen_swell_chaos, then an extra 0.3 mm blur. - zw = WL + relief·rim·water·smoothstep(d/0.4) + tide. - Basin confinement: water exists only in the water mask dilated by 0.6 mm, and only where zw > G. Elsewhere the surface is G − 0.3 (buried). Without the basin, the tide flooded low lenses into puddles (v0a). - Heightfield solid, uniform 0.2 mm.
  10. Assemble:
    • (grass ∪ slab box).simplify(tol) ∪ water ∪ rocks ∪ reeds;
    • DB base, with the tall peg if top > 15 mm;
    • keep the largest body;
    • gl.stl_safe.
  11. Preview (painted_meshes :356): separately coloured parts are resolved by the z-buffer, with no union.
    • Water by relief (zw − blur 2 mm).
    • Grass by T − blur 1.5 mm, with mud where soil > 0.5 or the grass is thin.
    • Walls dark (|n_z| < 0.35).

2.2 Look and rulings

Requirements S1–S5 are named failures: pasted water, lawn edge, moat, reed fence, drowned meadow.

Shawn's iteration rulings, in order: - reeds are simple chunky lemon blades, no cattails ("nothing more sophisticated"); - reeds rise from a near-common base and curve out gently; - reed tips are sharp but fairly blunt, blending smoothly; - reeds emit small ripple rings; - smoother ripples at the shore (short-sharpen 0.45, plus blur); - grass leans toward the water.

The long grass-undercut saga (v0h–v0k: TIP_CARVE, SIDE_CARVE_ALL, OPEN_AIR_MM, SLICER_LAYER_MM) was superseded by grasslab v3 stroke blades (commit 0616212). Those options no longer exist.

2.3 Printability

  • Water and ground are height fields.
  • Reeds are chunky (base 3.4–4.6 mm wide) and rooted below the surface.
  • Rocks are seated (meadowlab).
  • gl.stl_safe checks float32 merge and a single watertight body.
  • History shows the use of a slicer-style island check (shapely, 0.2 mm layers) and a cross-section void audit. Both were obsoleted by v3 blades.

2.4 Parameters (:36–79)

Group Constants
Bank SHORE_X 33, WOBBLE 11 @ σ 14, COVE 5 @ σ 5.5, BANK_W 8, SHIFT 3, BED 0.4
Mud MAX 4, σ 9, BIAS 0
Free lenses 0.35–0.6 per square
Steering 9 mm, OK 80°, PULL 0.3, smooth 1.5
Rocks 6–8 placements, WET_SURFACE 0.6
Reeds 3–5 clumps, separation 12, band (−1.1, 0.2), 5–9 stems, spread 0.35, fan 14–34°, h 8–13, half-width 1.7–2.3, thickness 0.5–0.65, TIP_SCALE 0.25–0.35, CAP 2.2, TIP_MIN 0.03, lean 8°
Water BURY 0.3, SOFTEN 0.3, WATER_OVERRIDES

CLI: --seed, --sheet --seeds.

2.5 Representations

  • Ground: grasslab Field2D, a sampled height field with bilinear call; indexing is x-major (indexing='ij').
  • Water: waterlab's y-major 0.1 mm grid. Note the two axis conventions meeting: ground(Xw, Yw) resamples between them.
  • Grass voxel Grid (VOX_MM).
  • manifold3d extrude/warp for the reeds; union for everything.

2.6 Mechanisms

Mechanism Classification
Bank = smoothstep ramp of signed distance to a wobbly curve, lerped from bed to the hill field general "slope transition between regions" (the library does this with IDW and boundaries)
Mud strip = EDT-to-water band of noise-varying width general "variable-width boundary band"
Shore steering = direction-field rotation toward a distance gradient general (same op as grasslab steer_rim; a duplicate)
Basin confinement = dilate the region, then max where above general "flood fill with containment"
Reeds = extrude, then warp (taper and bend) specific asset, but a generic "swept-section blade" primitive (cf. trees/leaf, grass)
Reed footprints → water emitters general cross-layer emitter registration
Monkeypatched module constants as overrides lab hack. In Rust: per-instance parameters
Wet/dry rock classification by region at the centre general "region membership of a placed object"

Duplicated code: smooth01 (:86); _tm/_ramp (same as dirtlab _ramp :582); the contact-sheet scaffold (same as waterlab and dirtlab); the assemble/base/peg block (same as waterlab build and dirtlab assemble).

2.7 Declarative generalisation

shorelab is exactly what a tile language should express in about 15 lines:

ground = hills(rises_only) ; bank(curve=wobble(33, [(11,14),(5,5.5)]), width=8, to=bed 0.4)
region water = flood(level 2.6)                      # paint-bucket below level, basin-confined
region land  = not water
land:  soil_lenses ; mud_band(from=water, width=noise(0..4)) ; grass(steer=toward(water, 9mm))
all:   rocks(6..8)  → membership(wet|dry) → wet rocks are obstacles/emitters for water
shallows(-1.1..+0.2 about level): reeds(clumps 3..5) → emits(ring band)
water: swell_chaos(short_sharpen 0.45, soften 0.3)

Key needs: - Ordered ops with typed outputs. Masks, distance fields, emitters and obstacles flow downstream. - Height bands relative to a named level (the reed band). - Per-instance overrides instead of module monkeypatching. - Derived seeds per op. shorelab already separates the rock RNG stream for byte-stability.

3. dirtlab — bare earth (dirtlab/dirtlab.py, 791 lines)

3.1 Pipeline (build_field :449, assemble :545)

The grid is 0.15 mm (468², n = round(70/0.15)+1; true spacing 0.14989 mm). Every layer except the larger stones is one height field, combined by polynomial smooth max smax (:137), h = clip(.5 + .5(b − a)/k); a(1 − h) + bh + k·h(1 − h), not by summing. Summing gives porridge; smax keeps lumps as shapes with soil fillets.

  1. Field (make_field :175): coordinates plus dist to the tile edge.
  2. Ground (ground_undulation :184). - Noise σ 14 mm + 0.15 × σ 5 mm. - Subtract the 35% quantile (plains), smax with 0 (soft floor), shift the minimum to 0. - Scale the peak to U(2.2, 3.2) mm. - Cosine fade over 9 mm from the rim. - Global slope cap at 24°: the whole field is scaled down if the maximum gradient exceeds it.
  3. w_tex = smoothstep(dist/1 mm). A 3-octave fBm (0.22 mm @ 1.6 mm, dharmatiles.stone.noise.fbm) gives soil roughness. ground = EDGE 4.0 + w_tex·LIFT 0.3 + und + w_tex·mid.
  4. Stones chosen (make_stones :395). - 4–7 rocklab make_rock at D 4.5–7.5 (skewed), scaled z ×0.65 and yawed. - Dart-thrown, at least 2 mm from the rim and 4 mm apart.
  5. Clods (clod_layer :205). - Patch field: noise σ 6 → density = 0.3 + 0.7·smoothstep(0.5 + (n + 0.15)). - Poisson(0.7/mm²·area) candidates, largest first, radius 0.45–2.4 skewed U^2.2. Rejected by the density field, the rim, and overlap < 0.6(r₁ + r₂) (spatial hash). - Each clod has 1–3 lobes; each lobe is:
    • an ellipse with aspect 1–1.7;
    • angular radius harmonics k ∈ {2, 3, 5}, σ 0.22/√k;
    • profile lh·(1 − ρ^2.6)^0.8 (flat top, soft shoulder);
    • tilted top (σ 0.05), sunk U(0, 0.5)·h, h = min(r·U(0.16, 0.35), 0.75).
    • Lobes smax into each other, then into the surface (k 0.06).
  6. Grit (grit :269): fBm with 2 octaves at 0.45 mm, ±0.08, plus sparse sand grains (0.12/mm², r 0.15–0.3, h 0.05–0.12, max-combined).
  7. Optional cracks (crack_layer :432): random-walk polylines → rasterised → EDT → −0.25·(1 − d/0.35)^1.5.
  8. Stone bedding (bed_stones :417). - A 0.3 mm Gaussian soil bank beyond 0.7R. - Rock base z0 = min(min ground under 0.85R − 0.25, median − 0.4h).
  9. Pebbles (place_pebbles :351, pebble_shape :300). - Cluster field (cluster_field :286): 4–7 Gaussian blobs (σ 3–7 mm) on a 0.12 floor, ×(1 + 0.8·lowness of the 3 mm-blurred ground). - 8–13 medium (2.2–4.5 mm) and 45–75 small (0.5–1.5 mm) per square, medium first. 60 darts each, accepted by the cluster probability, with a gap of 0.25 mm. - Shape: a flattened cap hp·√(1 − ρ^p), p ∈ U(2, 3.5).
    • Faceted (75%): the min of K tangent planes (6–11 medium, 4–7 small) at stratified angles and area-uniform radii. Each plane is tilted (σ 12°), lowered 0.03–0.18 hp, and its slope capped at 65°. This is rocklab's facet-per-seed idea in 2.5D.
    • Worn (25%): the cap with K/3 shallow flats.
    • Then the min with a polygonal skirt: 5–8 side planes at 58°, so the outline is angular (no discs) and there are no vertical aliased walls.
    • Seat at the 0.5 mm-blurred surface minus 25–45% of the height. Medium pebbles first get a soil heap (0.2hp Gaussian).
    • smax with k 0.08 (small) / 0.22 (medium) to fillet the foot.
    • The pebble mask is kept for paint.
  10. Rim: z[rim] = EDGE exactly, and z = max(z, EDGE) (rises only).
  11. Assemble:
    • make_heightmap_solid(subsample=1) (uniform mesh);
    • batch_boolean [top, 1 mm slab box, stones] → ∪ DB base;
    • stl_safe (:529, a copy of grasslab stl_safe): separate_pinches, float32 merge, keep the largest, raise if not watertight.
    • audit_overhang (:561): area where n_z < −sin 48°.
  12. Shared texture dirt_relief(seed, W, H) (:507).
    • Same stages without hills or stones, RNG [seed, 0xD1127].
    • Returns a DirtTexture (relief ≥ about 0, 0 at the rim, a pebble mask; bilinear __call__ onto any grid). Memoised.
    • Since commit 20c51e8 it is the exposed-ground texture for grasslab, meadowlab, shorelab, waterlab, rocklab and clifflab. Old mud and soil textures were retired.

3.2 Look and rulings

  • Failures D1–D7: porridge, bubbles, cells, rough concrete, teeth, pasted pebbles, freckles. The iteration log maps fixes to failures:
  • lobes and flat tops vs blisters;
  • facet planes vs tents and domes;
  • 58° polygonal skirt vs discs and vertical walls;
  • stones ×0.65 in z vs teeth;
  • grain density halved vs freckles.
  • Round 2 (Shawn, after slicing):
  • more relief;
  • no rim frame;
  • pebbles that register at tabletop distance;
  • stronger cloddy/smooth contrast;
  • REQ-D-TEX: texture must survive 0.2 mm layers. Within any 1.5 mm window, relief must be ≥ 0.2 mm over ≥ 97% of the interior. This went from 32.8% failing to 1.9% via a clod density floor of 0.3 and larger soil roughness.
  • Global ruling (2026-10-29): exposed ground = dirt everywhere.

3.3 Printability

  • A height field cannot overhang, so the earth and pebbles are supportless by construction.
  • The skirt at 58° and facets capped at 65° are on height-field slopes, so there is no undercut.
  • Stones are convex rocklab hulls, flattened and sunk; measured overhang 0.00 mm².
  • Rises-only plus the exact edge give the tiling condition.
  • The texture-survives-layer metric (a scratchpad script, not in the lab).
  • stl_safe.
  • Note: overhang-red painting (memory overhang-faces-red, 2026-10-31) is not implemented in dirtlab, waterlab or shorelab yet.

3.4 Parameters

All are module constants (:43–128) and documented in DESIGN §3, as updated in round 2: UND (14, 5)/0.15, relief 2.2–3.2, slope 24°; CLOD R 0.45–2.4, H max 0.75, floor 0.3; GRIT 0.08; MED 8–13 per square at 2.2–4.5; STONES 4–7; and so on.

CLI: --seed --cols --rows --cracks --sheet --seeds.

3.5 Representations

  • A y-major height field on the 0.15 mm grid.
  • Local windows (Field.window) for stamping.
  • manifold3d for the stones and the union.
  • trimesh.
  • pyrender, with a matplotlib software fallback (render_fallback :645, a ray-cast top surface via trimesh ray, Lambert lighting) for when the macOS session is locked.

3.6 Mechanisms

Mechanism Classification
smax stamping of local shapes into a height field core general mechanism (should be the heightfield combine op)
Rises-only undulation (quantile shift + soft floor + rim fade + slope cap) general (dup of grasslab make_ground)
Density-field-modulated dart throwing with spatial hash, big-first general scatter
Cluster field (sum of Gaussians, low-ground bias) general "placement density"
Radial-harmonic lumpy ellipse lobes general 2.5D stamp primitive
Min-of-tangent-planes facet pebble + polygonal skirt general 2.5D "faceted stone stamp" (2.5D sibling of scatter/stones.py and rocklab)
fBm (value noise) library stone.noise.fbm
Crack polylines → EDT → V profile general "engrave curve" (cf. scatter/stones.py crack engraving)
Bedding: soil bank + seat below min ground general "seat solid in height field" (cf. meadowlab seat_rocks)
Exact rim/edge level for tiling general tile boundary condition
DirtTexture relief offset resampled to any grid general "material texture = relief field"
stl_safe, audit_overhang, painted preview by multi-scale relief general export/eval (duplicates grasslab and waterlab)

3.7 Declarative generalisation

  • dirt should be a material whose surface op is a relief stack applied to any region with exposed ground. dirt_relief already is this, as an informal library. Proposed stack:

material dirt = [ roughness fbm(1.6, .22) ; stamp clods(density=patch(σ6, floor .3), r 0.45..2.4, smax .06) ; grit fbm(.45,.08) + grains ; stamp pebbles(cluster(4..7, low_bias .8), small/medium, facet|worn, smax .08/.22) ]

  • The host supplies the base height and the mask. Exposure = "not covered by water/grass", fading in above the waterline as waterlab does.
  • Stones are a separate placement op (solids, bedded).
  • Region-level params:
  • edge level 4.0;
  • rises-only;
  • per_square densities (already scaled by n_sq / 4 in code — note the awkward "_2X2" constants);
  • per-op seeds [seed, 0xD1127].

4. Cross-lab observations for the Rust engine

  1. The height field is the main representation. Water, dirt, bank, mud and tide are all 2-D fields. Solids (rocks, reeds, grass blades) are unioned with manifold3d at the end. Core Rust needs: - an f32/f64 Field2D with consistent axis order (the labs mix y-major and x-major, indexing='ij'); - bilinear resampling; - masked blur (normalised convolution); - EDT with indices, geodesic distance; - smax/softplus/smoothstep; - a stamping API (window + smax); - a uniform heightfield→solid mesher (the adaptive one faceted swells).
  2. One export guarantee. stl_safe exists three times: grasslab, dirtlab copy, waterlab inline variant. Make it one routine, along with overhang audit and red paint.
  3. One preview evaluator. The "paint by local relief" drybrush preview exists three times with different σ. A shared paint(material → ramp, relief scales) is enough.
  4. Composition hooks that emerged ad hoc, which the language should make first-class: - Scene.from_fields (any ground + obstacles → water); - reed_mask (layer-registered emitters); - dirt_relief (material texture); - blocker masks (grass avoids water and rocks); - wet/dry membership.
  5. Determinism: per-op RNG keyed by name (crc32) or a fixed salt (0x71DE, 0xD1127, a rock offset). This should become derive_seed(tile_seed, op_path). The labs rely on byte-identical outputs (sha256) across refactors.
  6. Tiling contract: - water edge = shared sine (λ 17.5, amplitude 0.6, phase origin 17.5 − λ/4) at a calm WL; - dirt edge = exact 4.0 mm; - both are boundary conditions on the field near the rim.

notes-variants-grass.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Grass surface variants, oldest to newest: lineage, mechanics, rulings

Read-only analysis, 2026-11-01. Sources: git log over src/scripts/archived/, src/dharmatiles/{grass,layers,scatter}/, grasslab/, meadowlab/, shorelab/; docs/design/grass-{heightmap,underlay,mound-thatch}.md, docs/grass-scene-v1.md, docs/floppy-grass-algorithm.md, docs/grass/*, docs/meta/history/2026-05-30-15-30-grass-tile-code-review.md, docs/meta/history/2026-07-03-grass-greenfield-requirements.md (it holds the "grass graveyard" that Shawn asked to be reconstructed from git), grasslab/{REQUIREMENTS,DESIGN}.md (§5 superseded designs, §6 iteration log, "v3" table), and memory notes grass-stroke-blades / grass-rock-labs / leaf-method-for-grass-rework. The grasslab v3 pipeline itself is covered in depth in notes-grasslab-meadowlab.md; this file covers the lineage.

Images are in docs/analysis/rust-rewrite/img/variants/ (listed in §6). The grasslab v1a and v1k images were made by re-running those commits' own grasslab.py in the scratchpad: 2×2 tile, seed 1, 5 s and 13 s. All other images are committed PNGs pulled from git history, or renders of STLs that already existed (committed in git history, in stl/test/, or in grasslab/out/). Overhang faces are painted red, per policy.

1. Family tree

Two lineages. Library grass (src/dharmatiles, May to July 2026) went through Eras 0–8. grasslab (standalone, 27–29 Oct 2026) went through v0 to v3. The two lineages share no code: grasslab imports only the base, heightmap-solid and render helpers from the library.

Rejected sub-variants named in the brief, and where they lived:

Sub-variant Where Fate
Height-field blades ("theoretical ideal" domes, knife edges, needle points) grasslab v1–v2, sculpt_frond, mass map T, mesh_field deleted in v3; the slicer drops anything narrower than a line
Turf mass (skirts, pads, swath envelope) grasslab v1f–v2 deleted in v3: "why do we ever want the pad?" → "blades on dirt, no turf mass"
Voxel FDM backstop (support filter, hole fill, sealed-pocket fill, slicer islands) grasslab v1–v2 deleted in v3
Carved tip and side undercuts (TIP_CARVE, TIP_GAP_MM, SIDE_CARVE_ALL) grasslab v2 for shorelab deleted in v3: neighbouring skirts walled the undercuts and pocket fill sealed them
Sickle bends (curvature field κ = 0.24·tanh(1.6n)/mm) grasslab v1j–k rejected: "clumps of commas" (F24)
Catmull-Rom interpolation between layers v3 development rejected as lumpy (overshoots the whole-line jumps); linear loft plus blur kept
< 1,500 faces per blade v3 800 faces read faceted in a viewer
45° cone underside; hull of nested sections; J-curve (concave) rise v3 development dropped (blunt front face, vertical keel post, wrong rise)
Global soft frond ends v1h "fish scales" (F7)
Noise-ellipse puddles; seam-lane soil v1c/d, v1e–k "terrible", then superseded by lenses that the flow wraps
Single sweeping flow (σ 25 mm, ±35°) v1 "Not just one big sweep"
Fringe rows, 2×/4× edge density, edge-row half-length blades E5 (06-07, 6 commits in one day) replaced by a source-edge-biased jitter grid
Smother rule; blade_smooth on XY vs Z E5 added then reverted the next commit; the axis flipped three times
Crown ceiling near rocks E8 tried twice, "re-carved the moat both times"

2. Per-variant: pipeline, look, why superseded

E0: 2D grayscale heightmap, embossed by OpenSCAD (05-26→27)

Files: src/scripts/archived/generate-grass-heightmap{,-v1,-flowfield}.py, prepare-heightmap.py, docs/design/grass-heightmap.md. 1. Ground layer: low-frequency noise at about 25 % of the height range. 2. Edge layer (v1): short inward blades on a perimeter walk, spaced 26 px, 22 % of them leaves. 3. Blades: quadratic Bézier spines with width w(t) = base_w·(1−t)^0.7, a ridge cos(π/2·d/w), and height sin(π t^0.55)·(1+amp·sin…). 4. v2 tip-priority compositing: every blade uses the same strictly increasing blade_h(t) = GRASS_BOTTOM + t·LAYER_STACK_H + ARC·t(1−t), so in np.maximum the pixel nearer a tip always wins (the first "proud-of-lower" rule). 5. Flow-field edition: streamlines through linear, swirl, radial, inward, corner or dipole fields plus curl noise, with branching side-shoots. 6. Compositing is ground + max(edge, blades, leaves), followed by blur, edge fade and a 256 px resize, then surface() with 1.5–2 mm texture_depth.

Look: an embossed relief at most 2 mm deep, with ridged strokes (image grass-e0-*). Died of a dark border where clipped edge blades were skipped, uneven density, and the move to real 3D. One day of ridge-sharpness and shadow-groove churn (BLADE_RIDGE_K 5→10→3, blur 0.75→0→0.3) shows the pattern.

E1: 2D painter's-algorithm scene (05-28)

generate-grass-scene.py + blade.py; docs/grass-scene-v1.md. 1. 700 px canvas (20 px/mm), seed 42. 2. Swirl flow: (fx,fy) = (yn/r, −xn/r) blended with "up" at FLOW_STRENGTH 0.45. 3. Blade bend comes from flow curvature: κ = ∇θ·f̂, normalised to p95, then cur = κ·U(0.3,1)·0.70. 4. Jittered-grid placement: 125 tall blades plus 300 fill blades, rejected if they leave the canvas. 5. Painter's sort by base_y, then render, prepare-heightmap and OpenSCAD. 6. A composite version puts the blades over a dirt heightmap (textures/grass-composite.png, stl/grass-composite.stl).

Look: a crisp graphic whirlpool of lens-shaped blades (grass-e1-*). As an emboss it is still a texture. The postmortem notes the scanline spine model "breaks at steep tilt" ("geometry is completely broken").

E2: support-field 3D blades, the first real STL (05-28→30)

generate-grass-support-stl.py → generate-grass-stl.py (1,065 LOC). 1. Sinusoidal terrain_z on a 256² grid; a swirl flow field. 2. Jittered-grid seeds, sorted downstream-first so upstream blades arch over. 3. Gravel stones (half-ellipsoids) are rasterised into support_z. 4. XY: a chord-preserving arc with lean(t) = LEAN·(1−cos(tπ/2)). 5. Z: an envelope solve (piecewise cosine, then 2- and 3-segment cubic Hermite, then the LCM / "knot envelope") over support_z + CLEARANCE, plus Möller–Trumbore intersection repair (8 passes, 32 direction retries). 6. Cross-section went from ribbon to triangular prism to a 4-vertex crease to a V-keel. The blade top is rasterised into support_z, then everything is concatenated.

Died of blade-height blowup, two z-solver rewrites in three days, and about 30 one-line tuning commits on 05-29 (some reverted). Vectorising took it from 218 s to 6.6 s (94df104). Image grass-e2-single-blade.jpg is the committed blade heightmap test strip. No tile STL was committed.

E3: arrangement archetypes (05-30→31)

layers/grass.py (c73e14f, 504 LOC); output stl/grass-burst-circle.stl, rewritten by about 12 commits: circle burst → radial spray → curl → random zones → flow-divergence density → "tuned" default. Same blade solver as E2, plus a circle cross-section with a sub-hull support strut, and vertical support hulls and cones under bridging blades. Look: a single radial tuft, then a dense zoned mat with gravel (grass-e3-*). None of the arrangements was kept.

E4: GrownGrass (06-01→05)

layers/grown_grass.py (322 LOC at 0a7feee). 1. Seeds come from E3's zone-weighted place_blades. 2. All blades grow at once, one segment per round, in a processing order shuffled each round. 3. z = max(terrain, occupancy) + clearance. 4. If the rise exceeds rise_cap, try ±15/30/45° turns (steering), else stop. 5. A Gaussian plus PCHIP path smoother, then build_tube_mesh. 6. Support posts and cones under bridging spans; occupancy is rasterised after every step.

Look (06-04 STLs 1x1-half-grass-soil, 1x1-corner-grass): spiky crossing tubes over soil (grass-e4-grown.jpg). Died of the "support-post saga" (posts reshaped four times in one day) and bald spots.

E5: FloppyGrassLayer (06-06 → replaced 07-03)

docs/floppy-grass-algorithm.md; today the surviving parts are grass/{grow,seed,grower,mesh,_geometry}.py. 1. plant_seeds: Voronoi groups (groups_per_square 50, 20–30 blades per group), with gap_mm spacing and a source-edge-biased jitter grid. 2. Sort: downstream-first, later GrassSeed.sort_key = (upstream_dist, direction). It flip-flopped. 3. grow_all rounds: each live blade steps blade_segment_length 0.8 mm. Stop at the tile or mask edge or past rise_cap 2 mm. 4. Z rule: nz = max(terrain, sz_t) + FLAT_CLEARANCE, where sz_t ignores the blade's own trail (own_stamps). It "lies flat, rises only over obstacles, drops back automatically". 5. Stamp a perpendicular strip (one cell along the blade, ±hw across) into occ_z right away. 6. Mesh pass: raise interior points to support and snap the tip. FlatGrassGrower.build_mesh sweeps rings with a V-keel (keel_fraction 0.6), an n_top_facets top and a quarter-cosine taper, then rasterises the top into vegetation_support_z.

Speed: 1×1 tile about 3.6 s. Look: total coverage of near-flat ribbons (grass-e5-floppy-carpet.jpg). Shawn's verdict (07-03): "the reference ends up being lumpy and organic, current grass renders flat with grass flat across a surface". The diagnosis: it is a relief-minimiser, flat by construction, so no parameter could fix it. Shawn also said "blade shape is fine", which is why FlatGrassGrower survived.

E6: GrassCarpet underlay (06-09 →, alive)

layers/grass_carpet.py (312 LOC), docs/design/grass-underlay.md. 1. Noise base: gaussian_filter(white, σ=3 mm), scaled to noise_amp 0.2 mm and clipped ≥ 0 (later bidirectional, as noise_top_mm), added into terrain_z. 2. Seeds from the same plant_seeds (Voronoi groups). 3. Trace each 2D curl path without growth. 4. Originally stamped cross-sections into the heightmap via _stamp_segment (staircased). Since 13b63f9 it builds flat tube meshes with FlatGrassGrower.build_mesh, with the ridge set to noise_top + blade_raise. 5. Obstacle cells are treated as a mask edge (a fix from the E8 rock saga).

Verdict: "papered over the flat-mat problem rather than solving it", but it survives as E8's fine second scale. Image grass-e6-carpet-only.jpg (the carpet tile was deleted three days after it was added).

E8: ThatchGrass mound-thatch, the "bushy" default (07-03, alive)

grass/thatch.py (509 LOC); docs/design/grass-mound-thatch.md. 1. Mound substrate: a mound-tuned SoilCarpet with super-Gaussian blobs (a primary tier of 30/sq at σ 1.4–2.5 plus a swell tier of 6/sq at σ 3–4.5), feathered at the tile and mask edges and capped at 40° slope. Relief is an input, not something that emerges. 2. Under-carpet: GrassCarpet. Rocks must come before it. 3. Sheaf sites: a jittered grid at _SPACING_MM 3.0. Blocked sites are moved outward along the distance gradient, not dropped. 4. Direction = 0.6·downslope(substrate smoothed σ 1 mm) ⊕ 0.4·a coherent angle field (6 mm). Within a sheaf, 4–9 blades are fanned 1.0 mm apart as parallel offsets, so they can't cross, and share curl sign and size (jitter ×0.75–1). 5. Drape: the spine is a 2D curl arc, z = substrate + clearance. Lowest root first, each blade is lifted over vegetation_support_z +0.25 mm (sorted standoff accumulation). The stack cap is 1.2 mm (×1.5 near rocks), truncating at the first violation. 6. Rock rules: soil skirt 0.8 × 2.5 mm, climb ≤ 1.3 mm, tip entry ≤ 0.9 mm and ≤ 2 steps, wall-slide deflection, a tangential crowding ring, and the crown rule r·flat − sink ≳ 3.4 mm. 7. Mesh with FlatGrassGrower (bushy species: width 1.4–2.0, length 5–9, curl 0.15–0.5π, thickness 0.8, taper 4, 4 facets), clamp to the tile footprint, rasterise the tops.

Speed: 2.4–3.5 s per 1×1. Look: a lumpy mat of plump curled locks (grass-e8-*). Ruling: "The bushy one is great. Good as defaults. Good as default grass." It was regenerated across all 35 tiles (7364820). The rock interaction took six rounds ("Not loving the grass/rock interaction… doesn't look natural at all"; "This interaction is fundamentally flawed… render top view, fix, loop"). The root cause was a phantom footprint: the rock stamp was the uncut ellipse. The library was never superseded formally. On 10-27 work moved to the standalone grasslab, which Shawn prefers.

grasslab v0: voxel lens blades (pre-commit)

About 1,800 small lens blades in random order inside a binary voxel volume. Watertight and printable, but it "read as crumpled foil" (F9). Lessons: specify top-down by visual scale, design the undercuts, never stack in random order, never use binary voxels plus blur, and look at S1 before building 2×2. No output survives.

grasslab v1a–v1k: swath → frond → groove, height field (10-27)

64572fb 778 LOC → 1613bc1 877 LOC. 1. Hills H₀: σ 10 + 0.35·σ 5 mm noise, 7 mm relief, 34° cap, edge 4.2 mm. 2. A singularity-free angle field (θ σ 25 mm, ±35°). 3. Swaths: a warped anisotropic Voronoi at 11–13 mm; the swath envelope gives the height wave. 4. Fronds fanned ±12° off each swath axis (v1a). In v1j–k, spines bent by a coherent curvature field ("sickle"). 5. Each frond = skirt + domed blade, soft-maxed into a mass map T; 45° tip lips as column intervals; bare soil along swath seams; prism or faceted pebbles. 6. Field F = max(T − z, intervals) → FDM voxel backstop → marching cubes at 0.10–0.12 mm → DB base.

Re-run timings: 2×2 in 5.2 s (v1a) and 13.4 s (v1k). One body, but it fails the float32 STL reload check (this is why stl_safe came on 10-28). Rulings, in order: "increase the hills a bit… a few patches of bare ground"; "bare patches are terrible"; grass "chopped down like it was all made of clay"; "our grass is very straight… should be more curved, but not randomly"; "more curves… not just one big sweep". The sickle answer gave "clumps of commas" (F24).

grasslab v2a–v2g: flow-following ribbons, still height-field blades (10-27→29)

3429f18 988 → 6d553a5 1,148 → abe2b77 1,549 LOC. Triggered by Shawn's lodged-grass photo: "go with the flow-following ribbons". 1. Rises-only hills from a consistent perimeter (v2f). 2. Flow: regional angle (σ 15 mm, ±60°) + in-phase meanders (λ 15 mm) + downhill pull. 3. Soil lenses: flow-aligned eyes that the flow wraps (tanh parting), with rim steering as a smoothed scalar rotation. 4. Strands: 15–30 mm streamlines at 2.65 mm Poisson spacing; fronds traced along them and shortened at the rim, soil or obstacles. 5. Height-field frond rendering as in v1 (skirt + dome + lip + side ledges with 45° undercuts on banks), plus the dirt floor (dirtlab.dirt_relief). 6. Voxel backstop, open_ledges, pocket fill, slicer-island removal (SLICER_LAYER_MM), MC, stl_safe.

Shorelab sub-variants: TIP_CARVE (undercut tips), TIP_GAP_MM 0.6 (tips as separate lifted geometry), SIDE_CARVE_ALL. Look: meandering combed ribbons, soft and slightly clay-like up close (grass-gl-v2-*). Shawn on the labs (10-28): "spectacular job on both grass and rocks… I love your work". But on single blades: "there is no undercut". Cross-sections showed the wedge was sealed by the blade's own dome edges and the pocket fill. In Bambu: "the top of the blade doesn't fill the volume we define", and "detect thin walls" made it worse. Diagnosis (abe2b77): "half the 'undercuts' were hidden internal voids."

grasslab v3: stroke blades on dirt (10-29, current)

0616212 1,189 LOC (−360 vs v2); blades_demo.py 108 LOC. Full stage detail is in notes-grasslab-meadowlab.md §A. Same field stages 1–4 as v2, then: 5. blade_layers: N = round(h/0.2) layers. Layer k is a round-capped stroke along the streamline, n_k = round(n_max·sin(π(k+½)/N)^0.6) lines of 0.45 mm (keel ≥ 2, top = 1, n_k ≤ n_{k−1}+1, so about 45°). Back end u_back = L(1−(1−(k+½)/N)^½) (a convex rise). Front end u_front = L − (N−1−k)·0.2 (a raked bow, so every tip overhangs air down to the last layer). Ends lose one line per 0.45 mm, so they come to a point. 6. blade_mesh: close each outline by 0.3 mm → 2D SDF at 0.05 mm → linear loft between layer centres in height-above-ground → blur 0.05/0.15 mm → marching cubes → lift by H₀ → quadric-decimate to 1,500 faces. 7. Dirt ground solid ∪ all blades (batch union, threaded) ∪ 1 mm slab ∪ DB base → keep the largest body → stl_safe.

2×2 seed 1: 3,288 blades, 1.74 M faces, 87 MB, about 100 s (meshing is about 80 s). Rulings: "make that the … only way to generate grass blades". Memory: don't bring back skirts, a voxel backstop, Catmull-Rom, or fewer than 1,500 faces.

3. Comparison table

Variant Look Printability Controls LOC Speed
E0 heightmap embossed ridged strokes ≤ 2 mm; dark border trivially printable (a heightmap); detail lost below the nozzle ~15 px constants, 6 flow types 401–572 per script seconds (2D)
E1 painter scene graphic swirl of lens blades emboss only flow type/strength, blade ranges, MAX_CURVE 351 + blade.py seconds
E2 support-field 3D sparse arched tubes over gravel height blowup; posts needed LEAN, CLEARANCE, Hermite knots, keel 1,065–1,142 218 s → 6.6 s
E3 arrangements single burst / zoned mat support struts and cones pattern mode, density, curl 504 (+604 archived) ~s
E4 GrownGrass spiky crossing tubes, bald spots posts reshaped ×4 rise_cap, turn angles, smoothing 322 + grass.py ~s
E5 Floppy full flat mat of ribbons good (flat, keel ≤ 45°) ~20 SpeciesConfig/GrassConfig knobs ~1,200 (grow/seed/grower/mesh/_geometry) 3.6 s / 1×1
E6 Carpet fine flat blade texture good noise_amp/scale, noise_top, raise 312 <1 s
E8 Thatch bushy lumpy plump curled locks good; mounds ≤ 40°, stack ≤ 1.2 mm species + ~20 module constants 509 (+ carpet + grower ~840) 2.4–3.5 s / 1×1
gl v1 swath/sickle combed swaths on hills; seams; commas voxel backstop; reload not watertight ~60 constants 778–877 5–13 s / 2×2
gl v2 ribbons (height field) meandering ribbons, lenses; soft, clay-like close up undercuts sealed; sub-line features dropped by slicer ~90 constants + carve flags 988–1,549 not recorded; ed438c8 says grass builds became ~3× faster
gl v3 strokes meandering ribbons of separate pointed raked blades on dirt model = what prints: whole lines and layers, about 45° by count v2 field constants + 9 stroke constants 1,189 ~100 s / 2×2 (80 s meshing)

4. What is genuinely different about v3, and why it looks better

  1. Blades are defined in the printer's units. Every earlier variant defined an ideal shape (Bézier ridge, swept ring tube, soft-max dome) and hoped the slicer would keep it. v3's blade is the slicer's own output: whole 0.2 mm layers × whole 0.45 mm lines. A 1-line ridge is the sharpest crest FDM can make. Nothing sub-line exists to be dropped, and the overhang limit is a line count (≤ ½ line per layer), not a filter. What Shawn sees in the viewer is what Bambu prints.
  2. Undercuts come from how the blade is built, not from carving. The raked bow (each lower layer is 0.2 mm shorter) makes every tip overhang open air down to the last layer. In v2 the lips, ledges and tip gaps were carved into a shared field, where neighbouring skirts walled them in and pocket fill sealed them. So v3 is the first variant with real shadow under the tips: the drybrush and silhouette read that every earlier variant lacked.
  3. Blades on dirt, no turf mass. Earlier variants hid the ground with a mass (E5/E8 carpet + mat; v1–v2 skirts). v3 has only blades plus dirt relief between them. Separate objects give crisp grooves and depth, and individual blades read at arm's length.
  4. Pointed in every horizontal section, convex rise, keel. Shawn's "every cross section… ends in a point" and "like a keel". Earlier tubes had round or blunt ends in plan.
  5. Arrangement from the flow, not per-blade randomness. It inherits v2's in-phase meandering streamlines in strands, wrapped around soil lenses. Neighbouring blades are parallel because they sample the same streamline field. E5 and E8 used i.i.d. or Voronoi-clump directions, and v1 used per-frond sickles.
  6. Smoothing is honest. The linear SDF loft between layer outlines melts the steps without changing the plan shape. For resin, the smoothed blade is the FDM blade minus the stair-steps.
  7. Cost: about 30× slower per tile than E8 (100 s / 2×2 vs about 3 s / 1×1), with 1.7 M faces. Per-blade marching cubes is the hot spot, which makes it the prime Rust target (embarrassingly parallel, 24 ms per blade).

5. Shared mechanisms, and one parameterised definition

Mechanisms that recur, often reinvented:

Mechanism Variants
Proud-of-lower ordering (higher thing sits on lower) E0 tip-priority, E2 support raster + downstream sort, E4/E5 occupancy stamps, E8 root-z standoff, v3 shingling and strand order (6×)
Coherent direction field E0 flow types, E1 swirl, E2 swirl, E5 Voronoi groups, E8 downslope⊕angle field, v1 angle field, v2/v3 meandering streamlines
Spine = curve in plan + z rule all (Bézier, chord arc, grown polyline, curl arc, RK2 streamline)
Cross-section swept along spine E2 prism/keel, E5/E8 rings + keel + facets, v3 per-layer line counts
Taper / point (1−t)^0.7, quarter-cosine, blade_taper mm, v3 line drop per 0.45 mm
Placement jittered grid, Voronoi groups, sheaf fans, Poisson strands
Substrate relief none (E5), noise (E6), mounds (E8), hills + dirt (v1–v3)
Obstacle interaction stop / steer / climb / tuck / side_low factor
Edge handling skip → edge ring → fringe rows → clamp at the wall → rim inset + field steering
Height budget rise_cap, max_stack_height, stack cap, DB band, h = layers·0.2

Every variant is a point in one space: substrate × direction field × placement × spine × body × stacking × finish. A declarative sketch:

grass "meadow" {
  substrate   = hills{relief 7mm, sigma [10,5]mm, rises_only, edge 3.4mm, slope_cap 34°}
              + dirt{…}                       # E8: mounds{per_sq 30+6, cap 40°}; E5: flat
  flow        = angle{coherence 15mm, swing 60°}
              + meander{wavelength 15mm, amp 10..30°} + downhill 0.3
              + wraps(lenses, obstacles) + rim_steer{…}
                                              # E8: downslope 0.6 ⊕ angle 6mm; E1: swirl 0.45
  clearings   = lenses{per_sq 1.3..1.8, half_len 5..9mm, aspect 0.45..0.65}
  placement   = strands{spacing 2.65mm, len 15..30mm, root_every 0.5·L}
                                              # E8: sheaves{spacing 3mm, n 4..9, fan 1mm}
                                              # E5: voronoi{groups 50/sq, 20..30}
  spine       = streamline{len 3.5..6.5mm, stray 5% ±20..35°}
                                              # E8: curl_arc{curl 0.15..0.5π}; E5: grown{step .8, rise_cap 2}
  body        = strokes{layer .2, line .45, keel 2, top 1, max_lines 2..4,
                        lens_exp .6, rake .2, tip_step .45, rise "convex" 2}
                                              # E5/E8: swept{width 1.4..2, thick .8, keel .6, facets 4, taper 4mm}
  z_rule      = seat_on(substrate, embed .3) # E8: drape + standoff .25, stack_cap 1.2
                                              # E5: max(terrain, occ)+clear (flat)
  order       = strand | root_z | upstream   # library lineage only; v3 has no ordering (blades are independent, then unioned)
  obstacles   = {tuck .5mm, side_low .5, side_mm 1.2} | {climb 1.3, tip_entry .9}
  edges       = {rim_inset .2..1.5mm, soil_spill .8..3.2, shorten_not_slice}
  finish      = smooth{loft linear, blur [.05,.15], faces 1500} | raw_strokes
  budget      = {db_band 4.4..5.9mm, overhang 45°}
}

The grammar's key point is that body and placement are independent axes. E8 sheaves with v3 stroke bodies, or v3 strands with the E8 mound substrate, are both legal compositions. The only variant-specific code left would be the z_rule (flat growth sim vs drape vs seat), and only "seat" (v3) and "drape+standoff" (E8) are worth keeping.

Notes: every rock / stone and cliff variant, old → new

Sources: git history (git show sha:path), docs/design/rocks-faceted-stones.md, docs/design/stone-surface-texture.md, docs/meta/history/2026-07-03-rocks-greenfield-requirements.md, docs/rocks/, rocklab/DESIGN.md+REQUIREMENTS.md, clifflab/DESIGN.md (revs 1–6), clifflab/rockmass.py, clifflab/rockpile.py at every commit (HEAD 803ba4f; the working tree has an uncommitted "round 12" diff, +160/−141, not covered), clifflab/out/ (file mtimes used to date untracked renders/STLs), session scratchpad review folders, memory notes. Companion to notes-clifflab-rocklab.md, which already documents the current rocklab and rockpile pipelines line by line; this file is the history and the comparison. Images listed in §6.

2. Per-variant pipelines

R0 — gravel half-ellipsoids (2026-05-29 → 06-01)

  1. add_gravel() (fea523e): 6000 pebbles, r 0.048–0.42 mm, power-law size (size_power 3→6→12).
  2. make_stone(): watertight half-ellipsoid ring mesh (az×el segments), base follows terrain, GRAVEL_SINK.
  3. R0a: ry = rx·U(0.65,1) (no slivers).
  4. R0b: each ring vertex jittered ±45 % r (XY) / ±18 % (Z) — immediately replaced.
  5. R0c: n_cuts=5 random planes; vertices past a plane projected onto it; normals biased horizontal; residual roughness 0.06.
  6. Rasterised into support_z so grass sits on stones (fb3a614). Look: pebble gravel / chunked gumdrops. Superseded: grown into R1.

R1 — dome kernel (layers/rocks.py, 326 LOC; deleted 4ec9fb8)

  1. RocksConfig samples r, flat, cut, sink, roughness, n_cuts; positions from Uniform/Grouped.
  2. _build_rocks_mesh_core: per rock a half-ellipsoid of EL×AZ rings (20×8 after af3f52d), apex + base centre, vectorised for N rocks.
  3. Plane cuts: n_cuts normals with |z|·0.3, offset cut × mean_r; vertices projected (pts -= max(0, n·p − d)·n), z ≥ 0.
  4. Residual roughness roughness·mean_r uniform jitter.
  5. Slope alignment: Rodrigues rotation local +Z → terrain normal (6eb3184).
  6. base_z = terrain − sink; batched box rasterisation of terrain_support_z + obstacle_mask. Look: smooth domes with a few flat chops — "melted gumdrops"; pebbles fine at 1–2 mm. Superseded: requirements interview 07-03 — Shawn: "the current domes suck" ("confirmed — the domes suck"); named defects: ellipsoid always reads through, perched not bedded, no paint-catching edges. Also had to be hand-sized to crown above thatch grass (cd2e9ed). Speed: "rocks are currently nearly free" (perf parity was a requirement). Measured here: judgment scene 0.2 s.

R2 — faceted convex hull gen-1 (E1–E5, 07-03)

  1. StoneSpec (footprint, class lump/slab/shard, facets M, lean, burial, cracks…); _support_points: M jittered Fibonacci directions × semi-axes × lumpiness 0.12.
  2. ConvexHull → planar faces; _seat_rotation onto a stable face.
  3. Yaw/lean/burial transform; bed-to-widest burial (search widest slice in bottom 65 %); corrective audit loop (lean −2°/burial +0.05 until n_z ≥ −0.72 or chord < 1.6 mm).
  4. Floor slice at 0.3 mm (convex ∩ half-space); terrain sealed to the underside + 3 mm soil skirt.
  5. Cracks (E5): top-3 faces >8 mm² by area×(0.4+n_z), surface-projected random walk ~6×1.1–1.9 mm, ±24°, stops at arrises (dot<0.6), sin^0.6 taper, width 0.5 / depth 0.55; one fork 35–60°; boolean difference (manifold).
  6. Cluster placement: dominant 4.5–8.5 mm + 0–3 companions at (r_d+r_c)·0.92–1.12, yaw jitter 25°.
  7. Rocks adapter maps legacy params → StoneSpec (5b5e193) so all tiles switched with zero spec edits. Look: crisp low-poly asteroid stones; big flat triangles; reads "cut", not "cleaved". Superseded: same day — "iterate until natural" → weathering. Crack ruling: "don't look like cracks AT ALL" (1.3 mm slots read as router pockets).

R3 — weathered / aged faceted stones (E6–E15, 07-04; shipped 4ec9fb8)

  1. Hull + seat rotation (≤32°), egg/crown_flat profile shaping.
  2. _weather_bites on the crisp hull: face dishing (3–8 %·√area) + spall scars (0.22–0.42·footprint) in the upper band.
  3. round_edges wobbling rolling-ball fillet (radius re-rolled per corner and every ~1.2 mm) — light weathering only.
  4. Facet-edge domain warp (E12): fBm displacement of space, amplitude 4 %·footprint (injective, cannot fold).
  5. blur_remesh: marching cubes on Gaussian-blurred occupancy (σ 1.2 aged / 0.9 fresh) + Taubin 16–30 iters.
  6. One crisp post-aging spall scar (E11), cut before relief.
  7. Broadband relief (E14): log-uniform wavelengths ~1/f, along smoothed normals, curvature damping 0.35, patchy envelope (quiet ×0.2), protected hero face (×0.15), micro amp 1.2 %·footprint (0.10–0.25 mm).
  8. Crack lofts (single V-channel solid per crack), floor clip by manifold ∩, float32 survival gate. Look: weathered boulders, pillowy, soft arrises, calm faces with one incident. Hero quartet tuned to Letipea/Sylt/Yeager/Doane photos. Rejected: constant fillet ("rounded dice"), normal-displacement warp (sliver pleats in MeshLab), binary MC (1 mm terraces "NO smoothing removes"), narrowband relief (egg-carton), "noise, not regular waves". Ship: "this is amazing."

R4 — common stone relief (E36/E37, 07-05/06; current library)

  1. Same hull/aging chain as R3 (code moved to stone/: shape.fibonacci_sphere, shape.round_edges, surface.blur_remesh, cracks.engrave_cracks, solidify.separate_pinches/survives_stl32).
  2. stone/noise.fbm: isotropic value noise (hashed lattice), no plane waves.
  3. stone/finish.stone_relief: carve −carve_mm·max(0,n−t)^shape at ~7 mm (≈55 % plateau untouched) + dish at footprint scale; curvature damping.
  4. Amplitudes per family; target stats from measured DungeonBlocks floors (RMS 0.2–0.4, skew ≤ −0.6). Look (render here): almost identical silhouette to R3, slightly crisper calm plateaus with worn recesses. Trigger: Shawn rejected plane-wave relief on floor slabs ("it doesn't look good" — choppy water / wrinkled fabric). Speed: judgment scene (1×1, ~26 stones) 1.2 s here.

R5 — rocklab (2026-10-27, current lab)

  1. make_item: D = 6+9·U^1.4 mm; p 0.25 clusters of 2–4 overlapping stones.
  2. make_body: hull_points of 75–145 pts on a non-increasing profile (dome/slab/block/wedge) with angle-only lumpiness Σk=2..5.
  3. cut_facets: variable-radius blue-noise seeds (0.9–2.5 mm·√(D/15)); tangent plane tilted ≤14°; half-space intersection; 30–165 facets.
  4. chip_corners_edges: corner/edge planes (0.3–0.9 / 0.2–0.55 mm), ≤3 % loss each, 8–16 chips.
  5. texture: refine_to_length(0.25) + fbm grain 0.12 mm @0.45 mm, halved near edges, zero on base/down faces.
  6. Dart-throw scatter largest first; sink 0.3+0.25 into dirtlab ground; DB base; float32 reload check. Look: sharp, crisp mosaic-faceted rocks of mixed facet size with chipped corners and tooth — a different family from R3/R4's soft aged boulders. Measured overhang 0.00–0.02 mm²; ~3 s per 2×2. Rulings: v1d "different degrees of texture… rough surfaces… chipped corners"; v1e "the fracture scallops are horrible… just turn up the fine grain"; v1f "make the largest faces smaller"; flake scars declined; final "spectacular… I love your work."

C1 — clifflab.py depth map, revs 1–5 (10-30)

  1. Bodies = plan footprints (shapely) with face lines; per grid point d = signed distance, s = arc position.
  2. Activity field (rank-transformed noise → exact 60/30/10 quiet/active/shattered).
  3. Face depth map D(s,z): base line + wobble + batter; LARGE masses (anisotropic warped Voronoi 8–35 mm, per-cell plane), MEDIUM plates (3–9 mm), ledges/shelves/diagonals, V-groove fissures on every cell boundary.
  4. Weathering F ← max(F, blur F) (convex edges round, recesses sharp) + fine shatter + grain ≥ 0.15 mm.
  5. Ends: rev3 standard end profile; rev4 ends are faces; rev5 contact faces shifted so the proudest point touches the boundary, cuts "never meet" (≤ half chord).
  6. Upward clamp per column F(z) ≥ F(z−dz) − dz·tan 42° ⇒ overhang ≤45° by construction, no islands.
  7. Implicit f = max(G−z, min(y−F, P−z)) on a 0.18 mm voxel grid → marching cubes → decimate → ∪ slab, scree, DB base; voxel support backstop. Look: a carved wall — prismatic Voronoi masses with V-crack network, soft. Rev 3 got "spectacular… the best cliffs I've ever seen." Superseded: rev 5's limits flattened masses into "a crack network on a flat wall"; fundamentally a Voronoi network on a depth map cannot make stacked prismatic blocks with flat chipped tops.

C2 — clifflab.py rev 6, stacked blocks in the depth map (10-30)

  1. Columns along the face line, widths 3–12 mm, joints ±7° (12 % at 18–32°), 25 % joints bridged.
  2. Blocks per column every 7–24 mm, 55 % snapped to 2–3 shared strata; column joints deep 1.5–3.2, row joints 0.45–1.0.
  3. Offsets: 1–2-column masses forward 0–8 mm, block steps back p 0.18, 8 % voids.
  4. Per block front plane + 4 chisel facets (18–40°) + 1 mm 45° edge chamfer; top edge per column −5…+3 mm.
  5. Rest of C1 (clamp, MC, ends); butt-joint fix: contact faces referenced to their proudest points, no clearance. Look: vertical columns of blocks, crisp but still a relief on one surface — blocks have no backs, no real stacking. Superseded within the hour by object-based approaches (C3/C4). Shawn's butt ruling: "you're clipping adjacent edges flat. They should be regular textured surfaces whose outside limits of texture touch the edge."

C3 — rockmass.py (10-30 afternoon, abandoned)

  1. Volume = union of footprint prisms.
  2. Recursive splits by tilted planes (joints ±14°, courses ±10°) to target sizes W 7–22, H 10–24 mm, depth-split at 4.5 mm.
  3. Each surface cell → a rock filling the cell, stepped out along the outward normal, 45° support cut, chisel facets, rocklab chips + grain.
  4. Core = volume shrunk back; ∪ dirt cap, ground, scree, base. Look (iter-144434/155553): a mosaic of tilted tiles stuck on a flat wall, triangular wedges, 132–324 mm² overhang, flat grey core visible between rocks. Why abandoned (inferred — no recorded ruling): space partition gives no gravity/support logic and reads as crazy-paving; replaced the same afternoon by rockpile.

C4 — rockpile gen-1 + forms + finish (58e8105, 1559f0a, 8e30d6e, 156ab80, 5b69555)

  1. FORM: target height field T(x,y) (tiers, gullies, buttresses, standard end band); later FORMS table: band (run/corner/canyon/escarpment) and rings (plateau/massif/mound/spire/needles).
  2. STACK: support grid S; repeatedly the lowest spot short of T → pick kind (column/block/slab/odd/monolith) → grow footprint over the pocket → rest on highest supports (tilted) → fill underside to S.
  3. FINISH while convex: whole-plane cleaves (dressed off true, face breaks ending in roofs/shelves, broken corners, 45° V joints), rocklab chips + grain; zones concentrate detail.
  4. 45° keel under each resting rock (no ceilings).
  5. ASSEMBLY: rocks − bending/branching V-wedge fissures ∪ dirt top ∪ banked ground ∪ rocklab scree.
  6. 156ab80: block proportions, rounded edges/joint grooves, broad sub-facets, joint-following tapered fissures, angular block scree; 5b69555: mass per form, per-kind aspect ranges, dirt on ledges. Look: tall stacked columns and blocks — reads as a pile of real masses for the first time. 2×2 run ~54 s.

C5 — seated stones (48af9ea, 34572d4)

  1. _seat: underside = cones at tan(90−55+3) from bed + 0.6 mm bevel, clipped into the stone below (replaces keel).
  2. OVERHANG_DEG 55 drives seat slope, support cone, audit; red faces in renders.
  3. _fit_tile: stones past the tile edge scaled back, never cut.
  4. Grain ±0.4 mm @ ~2 mm (was ±0.05 @0.45) to read at print scale.
  5. Hidden core (S eroded 2 mm, dropped 0.5 mm) so joints never show daylight; thin dirt film on tops; joint-to-joint plate cracks; area-scaled sub-facets.
  6. Export retry with coarser simplify if float32 opens a tangency. Rulings: "45 is too conservative"; "clipping is bad"; seams must read as grooves; after printing s4: fine texture doesn't read, back wall too flat. C5x flakes (Voronoi 0.05–0.45 mm sunk tilted planes on every face): hated, reverted.

C6 — column dressing + plates (a1df7be, rounds 3–7)

  1. Union-find columns (≥60 % shared footprint); dress per column (plane yaw 3–12°, batter −3…+10°, ≤2.5 mm).
  2. Each open face split into 2–6 tilted plates; crease lines kink at plates, meet in T/Y junctions.
  3. Hairline cracks follow creases and taper into joints; plate edges recessed V bevel.
  4. Guards: refuse sliver corners, large triangles, fans; fin tests vs kept wedges. Look: faceted plates with crease cracks; scored ~5/10 vs ref N. 2531 LOC (peak).

C7 — per-face height-field relief (803ba4f, rounds 8–11; current)

See notes-clifflab-rocklab.md §2.1 steps 7–8 for constants. In short: 1. _plan_faces: coplanar open faces merged across stones/columns. 2. _crack_net: cracks seeded at the barest spot, random-walked up/down until both ends hit an edge, joint or crack (T/Y), ≥35° meeting angles, never hugging edges. 3. Rasterise V-groove G (depth 1.2–1.8, mouth 0.8–1.2, pinch, taper, fade) + lip F (upper side sunk 0.5–1.0 on 50 %). 4. _relief: ray-cast face depth, h = F + w·G, wall guard ≥1 mm, row clamp at 47° top-down, 0.08/0.3 mm blur, heightmap solid subtracted in face frame. 5. Isotropic grain 0.25 mm @1.4 mm over one tile-wide field; core gets the same relief. Look: crisp blocks whose faces carry a joined fracture network with lips — "the last one" Shawn likes. Still ~5/10 vs ref N ("ours read crumpled"). 1×1 run ≈ 30–35 s (review11 logs).

4. What is genuinely different about the newest

rocklab (R5) vs library stones (R3/R4). The library chain starts from a convex hull of few points and then spends 8 stages making it look natural (fillet, bites, warp, voxel remesh, smoothing, relief) — every stage softens. rocklab instead starts from a dense, lumpy, non-increasing body and only removes material with planes at three scales (facets 2–7 mm, chips 0.2–0.9 mm) before a tiny grain. Consequences: (1) facet sizes are a mosaic by construction (variable-radius blue noise), so no giant flat triangles and no uniform "dice"; (2) every edge stays a crisp plane–plane arris that catches drybrush; (3) convexity + n_z ≥ −0.5 gives printability for free — no audit loop, no voxel round-trip; (4) it's ~3× less code. It looks like fresh-fractured rock; the library looks like river-worn boulders. Both are valid looks; they are two parameter regimes of the same idea (planes + noise) — R2 was the crisp extreme without the mosaic.

rockpile (C4–C7) vs clifflab.py (C1/C2). clifflab carved one surface (a depth map), so blocks had no backs, no beds, no stacking; every structural cue was painted into relief. rockpile builds objects with a history: stones are placed lowest-first under gravity onto a support field toward a target silhouette, so joints, tiers, bridges, stand-backs, ledges and the anti-coursing stagger fall out of the process; each stone is then a real convex solid dressed by whole planes per column (faces run across joints), seated on its bed (no ceilings by construction), and only then textured by a per-face relief whose cracks are a joined network anchored on stone edges. That is why it reads as "big piled-up slabs" (the thing rev 6 lost), and why each later round could add detail without breaking printability: the guarantees live in the construction (seat cones, row clamp, fit-to-tile), not in a final clamp. What the newest round (C7) adds over C6: cracks are a network (ends terminate on edges/joints/other cracks), grooves have lips and pinch, the core and grain are continuous across stones — the face reads as one fractured mass rather than individually crumpled plates.

5. Shared mechanisms → one parameterised "stone" definition

Shared across all variants:

Mechanism Used by
Convex body from points (hull) R2–R5, rockpile _block_stone, scree
Half-space carve with guards (max loss, min n_z, no fin, no undercut) R0c, R1 cuts, R5 facets/chips, C2 chisel facets, C3, C4–C7 dressing
Seat/bed on support (burial / sink / seat cones / keel) R1 sink, R2 bed-to-widest + skirt, R5 sink, C4 keel → C5 seat
Edge rounding R3 ball fillet, C4+ tangent-plane round_edges, C1 max(F, blur F)
Crack/fracture lines (walk on surface, V-groove, taper, fork, stop at arris) R2/R3 engrave_cracks, C1 Voronoi fissures, C4 V-wedges, C6 crease cracks, C7 _crack_net
Noise displacement (fbm value noise), with masks (edge keep, base fade, facing, envelope) R3/R4 relief, R5 grain, C5–C7 grain
Placement: dart throw / clusters / Voronoi groups R1 Uniform/Grouped, R2 clusters, R5 scatter, C4 scree
FDM overhang guard + audit + red paint all
Export guarantees (float32 reload, pinch split) R3+, R5, C4+

A single declarative Stone definition:

stone:                       # one solid; scatter, pile, wall and cliff all make these
  body:
    kind: hull_points        # hull of sampled points (R2/R3: few dirs; R5: dense lumpy profile)
    profile: dome|slab|block|wedge|egg|prism   # r(t), non-increasing → grounded
    size: {footprint_mm, aspect, height_ratio}
    lump: {harmonics: 2..5, amp: 0.08}
  carve:                     # ordered list; every carve has the same guard contract
    - planes: {seeds: blue_noise(r: 0.9..2.5 * sqrt(D/15)), tilt_deg: 14}      # facets
    - planes: {seeds: corners|sharp_edges, depth_mm: 0.2..0.9, cap: 8..16}     # chips
    - planes: {seeds: per_group(column), yaw_deg: 3..12, batter_deg: -3..10}   # dressing (cliff)
    guards: {max_loss: 0.03, min_nz: -0.5, min_wall_mm: 1.2}
  age:                       # optional; empty = rocklab/fresh, full = library boulder
    roundover_mm: 0..1.2     # 0 → crisp arrises
    bites: {dish, spall_scars}
    warp: {amp_frac: 0.04}
    remesh: blur(sigma)      # only when aging
  relief:                    # one op, scale/sign/mask parameters
    - fbm: {scale_mm: 0.45, amp_mm: 0.12, mask: [edge_keep, base_fade, facing]}   # grain
    - carve_down: {scale_mm: 7, threshold: 0.55}                                  # worn plateau
    - cracks: {net: joined|walk, groove: {depth, mouth, pinch}, lip, stop_at: [arris, joint, crack]}
  rest:                      # how it meets what is under it
    on: terrain|support_field
    mode: sink(0.3) | bed_to_widest(burial) | seat(slope_from: printer.overhang)

Arrangements are separate definitions that emit stones:

scatter: {stone: rock_fresh, place: dart(largest_first, clearance: 1.5), clusters: {p: .25, n: 2..4}}
pile/cliff: {target: Form.band|Form.rings, fill: GravityStack(kinds, stagger, zones, mass),
             group: columns(share: .6), face: relief_per_face(network), core: hidden, fit: scale_to_tile}

and a single printer contract (overhang_deg: 55, min_wall_mm, nozzle_mm, float32_safe) consumed by every guard, seat slope, row clamp and audit. In this form R1 = profile: dome, carve: few random planes, age: none; R2 = hull few dirs, no carve; R3/R4 = age: full + relief carve_down; R5 = dense profile + facet/chip carves + grain; a cliff stone = prism footprint + dressing carve + seat + cracks.

notes-variants-water-shore-dirt.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Variants: water, shore and dirt surfaces, from oldest to newest

Research notes, 2026-11-01, written as groundwork for the Rust rewrite. Read-only survey of src/dharmatiles/layers/{water,soil}.py, waterlab/, shorelab/, dirtlab/, grasslab/ (old bare-soil texture), git history, contact sheets and memory notes. Images are in img/variants/ (§6).

Shawn asked: "I like the look of the last one, but I'm curious what the other ones, how they worked, and what's different about the new ones." This file answers that for water, shore and dirt.

1. Family trees

1.1 Water
