2. Each variant: pipeline, look, and why it was replaced

Notation: WL = calm water level (2.6 mm in the labs, 3.0 mm in the library); d = distance from the obstacle in mm; n(σ) = Gaussian-smoothed white noise.

2.1 Water: library

W-L1, EDT damped-cosine rings (8bd4bfd, _build_ripple_displacement, WaterRippleConfig) 1. Sources = the inner ring of the shoreline plus stone footprints that lie in water (at first the tile edges counted too). 2. d = EDT to the nearest source. 3. z = A·e^(−d/decay)·cos(2πd/λ) + a₂·A·e^(−d/0.6decay)·cos(2πd/λ₂+φ₂). 4. Bilinear interpolation to vertex corners; smoothstep fade over 4 mm at the tile edges.

Look: perfect concentric offsets of the shore and the rock, which also squared off against the tile edges (img/variants/water-lib-8bd4bfd-edt-rings.jpg, re-run on a synthetic scene). Why replaced (d0c71f4): "wavefronts … mirror the shore shape". This is the W2 "targets" failure.

W-L2, point-source interference (d0c71f4 → a4a8e78) 1. N discrete point sources: 14 (later 4) spaced along the shoreline ring, 8 (later 3) where grass tips touch water (EDT to grass < 6 mm), and the centroid of each stone. 2. Each source emits a circular damped cosine (λ 3.5 → 1.8 mm, decay 8 → 5 mm), with per-source phase jitter σ 0.5 → 0. 3. Sum the sources; blur σ 1.5 cells (removed in a4a8e78). 4. 2c7da27: crests above 0 spill onto a 1.5 mm dilated band of the bank, and troughs below −0.1 mm show the floor.

Look: separate raindrop-ring circles and interference moiré (water-lib-a4a8e78-point-sources.jpg). It was replaced two days later by structured waves. No ruling was recorded, but this is the W2 "raindrop rings" failure.

W-L3, structured waves (91c14bb; spec in docs/design/water-surface-model.md) 1. AmpField = shore amplification (×1.35 within 5 mm, smoothstep) × wake suppression (Gaussian teardrop behind each rock, ×0.45). 2. Primary waves: 3 sinusoidal trains at 30° ± 0.3 rad, λ 12 mm ± 30%, A 0.22 mm. The wavenumber is ×1.6 near the shore (shoaling). 3. Capillaries: 10 random-direction sines, λ 2.5–5 mm, A 0.045 mm (e74b99b gave them s·|s| sharp crests). 4. Rock bow wave: an elliptical Gaussian upstream. Meniscus: a Gaussian ring at the rock radius. 5. Clamp to the riverbed; bilinear interpolation onto the water-volume top.

Look: global diagonal corduroy with a ring collar on the rock (water-lib-91c14bb-structured-waves.jpg). This is waterlab's later W3 "wave rows" failure. It was tuned 7 times in one evening, then deleted on 06-05 (0817b39): "wave texture removed in favour of flat adaptive water solid". The mesh had grown to 14k+ faces and gone non-manifold (7e6010d).

W-L5, noise + scattered-segment ripples: the current library and waterlab "legacy" (be4b1bb; make_water_displacement, make_water_ripple_displacement, _force_tile_edge_water_profile) 1. Noise: white noise blurred at σ 3 mm, normalised to the RMS amplitude, faded by Hann within embed_mm of non-water cells. 2. Sources: the internal shoreline plus obstacle cells in water. Take the EDT distance and nearest-source indices, both Gaussian-smoothed at 2.5 mm inside water to kill Voronoi seams. 3. Segments: about one per 6 mm of source perimeter. Each picks a random source cell, offset d₀ ∈ U(0, 20) mm and length ∈ U(10, 20) mm. 4. Each segment is one sine period of λ = 3 mm in the band d₀ ≤ d ≤ d₀+λ. It has full amplitude along the arc within half its length of the centre, then a Hann^0.4 fade over 15 mm. The amplitude falls linearly with d₀. 5. Sum; fade at the mask boundary; downsample to about 128 cells/sq. 6. make_water_volume: a full-tile slab minus the soil solid. The top is blended inside a 5 mm rounded-square inset to the shared edge sine (λ = square/2, phase origin square/2 − λ/4, IDW at corners), so neighbouring water tiles meet.

Look: low isotropic lumps plus a few shore-parallel stripe arcs and the occasional "pasted ring" (water-lib-legacy-1x1-water+soil.jpg, water-lib-legacy-3x3-water.jpg, and the lab port water-lab-legacy.jpg). It is still the production water. waterlab kept it as the baseline row and judged it this way in v0: "shore stripes plus a single big loop (reads as a pasted ring, W2/W7); quiet elsewhere". Its lasting contribution is the shared tile-edge sine, which waterlab adopted as the "tide".

2.2 Water: waterlab

Shared by every option (DESIGN §1): a 0.1 mm grid on 70 × 70 mm, final z = max(land, water). Land = bank smoothstep over 7 mm on a wobbly shoreline at x = 17 + 4n(y). One rocklab rock of 15 mm, sunk 1.4 mm. Obstacles O = land above WL ∪ rock footprint. The rim fades the relief over 4 mm. From R2 on, a tide is added: n(σ 9 mm) at 0.35 mm RMS, forced within 9 mm of the rim to the library's edge sine (0.6 mm, λ 17.5 mm). The solid is make_heightmap_solid ∪ rock ∪ DB base, checked by stl_safe.

lapping → rings (gen_rings) 1. d to O ∪ rock, smoothed σ 1 mm; warp d_w = d + 1.2 mm·n(3 mm). 2. Log phase with widening spacing: φ = ln(1+g·d_w)/(g·λ₀), g = 0.09. 3. Crest c = ((1+cos 2πφ)/2)^1.6: narrow crests over broad troughs. 4. Break gate per crest index k = round(φ), noise sampled at (nearest obstacle point + k·offset). 5. Envelope A·e^(−d/decay): rock 1.0 mm/9 mm, shore 0.9 mm/8 mm, blended by the type of the nearest obstacle. Contact lip 0.3 mm at d ≈ 0.6. 6. Open water gets only the tide.

Look: tidy rings round the rock and shore-parallel crests (water-lab-rings.jpg). Shawn "still liked" it in R3, and it stayed on the sheet as a reference ("clearest rock reaction"). But it is target-like, and "open water is only the tide: empty".

crescents (v0 only). Lapping, plus Poisson crescent arcs 9 mm apart in open water (radius 4–9 mm, height 0.25–0.45 mm, soft max). It "read as raindrop commas (W2)" and was dropped in R2. No output survives.

strokes → combed (gen_combed) 1. An oriented direction field: a meandering field at σ 12 mm with ±70° swing. Its component normal to obstacles is removed with weight e^(−d/7) (R2 change; v3 had blended in doubled-angle space). 2. Poisson seeds 5.6 mm apart. Trace a centreline both ways along the field; length 10–20 mm near obstacles, 7–15 mm far. 3. Cross-section: Gaussian crest e^(−2.2q²) with shallow −0.28h troughs. Along the length the height follows a sin(πs)^1.3 bell, the width tapers 35% → 100% → 35%, and the height jitters ±20%. 4. Overlaps take a smooth union. A blanket softmax caused the v2 "box edges" bug. 5. Add the contact lip and the tide.

Look: wind-combed feather or palm-frond strokes wrapping rock and shore (water-lab-combed.jpg). History: v1 "worms / rice", v4 "feather / palm-frond". Shawn, R2→R3: "the strokes are too short and too uniform … scales are unrelated: big swells and short uniform ripples, nothing between."

hybrid → lapped (gen_lapped): rings own d < 8 mm (break bias 0.45), strokes beyond. In v3 the "radial strokes burst out of the rock". By R2 it read "contact rings … still too broken this seed" (water-lab-lapped.jpg).

sim / sim-wind / sim-rings (_wave_sim, gen_sim*) 1. A 2D damped linear wave equation on a 0.25 mm grid, 1400 steps. Wave speed c = 0.45·√(depth/1 mm), clamped at √0.35. 2. Land and rock are reflecting boundaries. 3. Forcing is injected in a 5 mm band at the 3 water edges: 6 wavelengths (2.5–14 mm, log-uniform) × 3 directions, amplitude ∝ λ^0.7. Directions are uniform (sim) or within ±30° of one wind (sim-wind). 4. Zoom the snapshot to 0.1 mm and normalise. Sharpen with u + k(u²−1)/2 (k 0.9). 5. Scale to 0.32 mm RMS × (1 + 0.9e^(−d/6)). sim-rings additionally quiets the sim chop up to 85% within 7 mm and adds the rings field ×1.25.

Look: real-water chop with all directions crossing (water-lab-sim.jpg, -sim-wind, -sim-rings). sim-wind made directional rows (a tiling risk). Shawn rejected it in R4: waves should come from the obstacles, and all-directions chop "ends up with a whole bunch of spiky shit".

spectrum (gen_spectrum): 70 plane waves (λ 2.5–22 mm, a ∝ λ^0.65, ±75° about a wind) plus a Stokes 2nd harmonic and phase compression near obstacles. "The weakest: lumpy, with an odd calm zone round the rock" (water-lab-spectrum.jpg).

emitted / emitted-sum (_emit_sources, _geodesic, _train, gen_emitted) 1. Sources are land (strength 1.0, decay 34 mm, λ₀ 3.8, growth 0.022), rock (0.55, 10 mm, 3.0, 0.05) and 2 random non-land tile edges (0.28, 50 mm, 5.0; straight distance). 2. Geodesic distance for land and rock: Dijkstra through water on an 8-connected grid that is really 0.2 mm while the weights assume 0.25 mm (so distances read 1.25× long; waterlab.py:723,743), so land waves wrap round the rock. It is smoothed inside water only (normalised convolution). 3. One train per source: wobbled log-phase, crest ((1+cos)/2)^1.7, long rare breaks (bias 0.55, σ 7 mm), plus a same-source rider at 0.45× wavenumber and 0.55× amplitude. 4. Combine with a w⁸ partition of unity (w = s·e^(−d/decay)), so the dominant source rules its territory. Amplitude × (0.35 + 0.65√w_max). emitted-sum sums the w-weighted trains instead.

Look: shore-parallel crests sweeping out, wrapping the rock (water-lab-emitted.jpg). The sum version is busier (water-lab-emitted-sum.jpg). Shawn liked it but found it "still too regular. A source's influence should get less clear the further you go".

emitted-chaos (_corner_sources, gen_emitted_chaos) 1. Emitters: land and rock as before, plus each open-water tile corner as a weak radial emitter (0.3, 70 mm, λ 4). The edge fallback is dropped. 2. Coherence loss: phase wobble = 1 mm + 0.06·d (corners at half that rate). Physical crests rarely break (bias 0.9). 3. order = max physical weight; c = smoothstep((order − 0.12)/(0.45 − 0.12)). 4. z = (c·dominant[w⁸] + (1−c)·chaos[Σw·train/√Σw²]) × (0.7 + 0.3c). 5. --open gives an all-water tile with only the four corner emitters.

Look: crisp near sources, dissolving into non-directional interference far away (water-lab-emitted-chaos.jpg, -open.jpg). Shawn: "the best I've seen so far." He then asked for a range of frequencies per emitter, depending on the object type.

spectral-chaos (_band_train, gen_spectral_chaos) 1. Each emitter sums n trains at log-spaced, ±12%-jittered λ across its band: land 3.2–11 mm (4 trains), rock 2.2–4.8 (3), corner 4–12 (3). 2. Amplitude ∝ λ^1.0. Each component has its own phase and wobble; shorter waves decohere faster, (λ/λ_hi)^−0.3. 3. tanh-tame, then Stokes-sharpen (0.85), so the interference forms wave groups. 4. Combine as in emitted-chaos; 0.42 mm per unit RMS. (v6a: land 2.6–9 read as "wood grain".)

Look: about 1 mm relief, wave groups (water-lab-spectral-chaos.jpg, -open.jpg). It was superseded once Shawn shared a ChatGPT acrylic painting of it, which showed deeper relief, a few broad swells and busier chop behind the rock.

swell-chaos: the winner (_swell_train, gen_swell_chaos, _round_offsets, _far_sources) 1. Scene → emitters: land (geodesic, smoothed 1.2 mm, then _round_offsets blends toward blurrier copies, σ ≈ 0.45·d at levels 2/4/8/16 mm), rock (geodesic, sharp offsets), open corners. Far emitters (3, about 220 mm out) are added when fewer than 2 corners are open. 2. Enclosure: f = clip((land share of the perimeter − 0.4)/0.6). Land decay × (1 − 0.75f). 3. Band per kind (λ_lo–λ_hi, growth, components): land 5–16/0.012/4, rock 2.0–4.5/0.035/3, corner 5–14/0/3, reed 1.8–3/0/2. 4. _swell_train: components above the band's geometric mid λ are summed as long and kept round (sharpen 0.15); shorter ones as short, crest-sharpened (0.9). Amplitude ∝ λ^1.4, so swells dominate. Coherence wobble as in spectral-chaos. 5. Rock: weight ×1.15 × (1 + 0.9·down·e^(−d/2.5·decay)), where down = ∇d_land·∇d_rock (the downstream wake). 6. Order → chaos hand-over exactly as in emitted-chaos; × SWELL_AMP_MM 0.8 (about 2× relief, −0.74…+2.2 mm). 7. Optional reed rings are added: 0.45 mm · e^(−d/4.5). Soft trough floor = softplus at −1.1 mm (the bed moved to WL − 2.2 mm to stop terraces). 8. z = WL + relief·rim + tide; land gets dirt (R7d); uniform 0.2 mm top mesh (the adaptive mesher faceted the swells); ∪ rock ∪ base.

Look: a few broad, round swells rolling off the bank with sharp small riders, strong rings plus chop behind the rock, and calm crossing chaos far out (water-lab-swell-chaos.jpg, -open.jpg). Scenarios: cube and pyramid rings take the obstacle shape ("perfect"). Square land surrounds "look a bit odd" before the fix: nested U and square contours (water-lab-swell-chaos-land34-before-fix.jpg). After the fix they show rounded shore waves and rock rings in the middle (-land34-after-fix.jpg, all 8 scenarios in water-lab-swell-chaos-scenarios.jpg). Build about 2.3 s (after ed438c8 perf).

2.3 Shore (shorelab)

Pipeline (DESIGN): 1 make_shore_ground: grasslab rises-only hills plus a bank, x_s(y) = 33 + 11n₁₄ + 5n₅.₅ (2σ-normalised); G = bed + (hills − bed)·smoothstep((s+3)/8); the waterline is where G = 2.6. 2 rocklab rocks (6–8), wet or dry. 3 ShoreSoil: meadow lenses plus a mud strip of noise-varying width 0–4 mm; water is a grass blocker. 4 steer_shore: within 9 mm, headings pointing away from the water are compressed to along-shore. 5 grasslab.build_grass(blocker = water ∪ rocks). 6 build_water: waterlab.Scene.from_fields + gen_swell_chaos, applied only inside the basin (water mask dilated 0.6 mm), otherwise buried at G − 0.3. 7 seat_rocks. 8 make_reeds (3–5 clumps in the shallows, built before the water so their stems emit rings). 9 union + stl_safe. 10 painted contact sheet.

Ver What changed Trigger / ruling
v0a first build: nearly straight shore, blue puddles on land, mud ≈ 1% self-check
v0b/c two-scale wobble, 2σ normalisation, water confined to the basin, mud 0–4 mm self-check; v0c handed to Shawn
v0d reeds in the shallows emit tiny rings (band 1.8–3 mm), added not blended Shawn: "small ripples circling out from them"
v0e lemon-section tapered blades; cattails gone Shawn: "nothing more sophisticated"
v0f shorter grooved shore grass; reeds 8–13 mm fanning from one base Shawn
v0g water at the shore softened: SWELL_SHORT_SHARPEN 0.9 → 0.45 + 0.3 mm relief blur; reeds curve (k·z²) Shawn: smoother ripples at shore and rocks
v0h TIP_CARVE undercut tips Shawn: emphasise tips
v0i lifting tips, 0.6 mm air gap under a 45° underside Shawn: "more — a 45° cutout, so the tips are separate geometry"
v0j side undercuts on every blade; wider blades Shawn: "little tufts at this scale"
v0k SLICER_LAYER_MM 0.2 removes 15 slicer islands Bambu "floating regions"
10-29 mud strip → dirtlab texture; reed tip ogive; OPEN_AIR_MM fills sealed pockets Shawn: dirt everywhere; tips "sharp … but fairly blunt"
latest grasslab v3 stroke blades everywhere; shore overrides deleted; s3 ≈ 40 s, 0.95 M faces, 48 MB, tall peg stroke-blade rework

Images: shore-v0a.jpg … shore-v0j.jpg, shore-latest-stroke-blades.jpg.

The shore's surface ingredients are the bank, the strip (mud, later dirt) and water softened at the shore. Everything else is grass, reed and rock work layered on top.

2.4 Dirt / soil

D-L0, multi-octave noise (165fc04). Three σ/amplitude noise tiers summed into terrain_z with a cosine edge fade. Porridge (D1). Replaced the same day.

D-L1…L4, SoilCarpet: the current library (52c2a3a, 9edb15d, fcba31b, c53c1de) 1. Bump field at 2× the grid (_OVERSAMPLE). 2. Primary tier: 277 blobs/sq at clustered positions (30 centres, 6 mm spread). σ is triangular(0.22, 0.434, 1.026) mm, aspect 0.78–1.0, profile super-Gaussian exp(−d^3.5/2σ^3.5) cut at 2.6σ, h = σ·U(0.14, 1.12). Angular harmonic shape noise 0.06 × 4. 3. Max-blend accumulation (no stacking); plus surface texture n(0.27 mm) at 0.06 mm. 4. Downsample; arc-length draping (resample at cumulative √(g²+1) coordinates) × n_z, so bumps sit normal to slopes. 5. Cosine fade at the tile edge and the placement mask; scene.displace_terrain.

Look: a dense, even field of rounded sub-mm blisters (dirt-lib-soilcarpet-1x1-soil.jpg). It was never explicitly ruled on, but it is the dirtlab D2 "bubbles" / D1 failure. It is superseded in the labs by dirtlab and not yet ported.

Grasslab bare soil / shore mud (grasslab v1c–v2a, soil_relief). Lens- or lane-shaped patches, a soft −0.4 mm dip (mask blurred 0.8 mm), fine roughness and faceted "dome" pebbles. Shawn: "bare patches are terrible" (puddles, trench rims, bubble pebbles) and "bare patches have some weird artifacts". Retired 10-29. The function is still called only to preserve the RNG stream. No standalone output survives; shore v0a–v0j images show it as the brown strip and patches.

dirtlab v1a–v1g (build_field, all one height field on a 0.15 mm grid, soft-max combined) 1. ground_undulation: n(10) + 0.35·n(4); subtract the 35% quantile, soft-floor to plains, peak U(0.9, 1.6) mm, 6 mm rim fade, 14° slope cap; plus fBm 2.2 mm ±0.07. 2. clod_layer: patch density n(6 mm) → smoothstep; Poisson 0.7/mm², largest first, gap ≥ 0.6(r₁+r₂). r = 0.35–1.5 (U^2.2). 1–3 lobes with harmonics k = 2, 3, 5, profile h(1−ρ^2.6)^0.8, tilt, partly sunk. Soft max k = 0.06. 3. grit: fBm 0.45 mm ±0.045 plus sand grains 0.12/mm². 4. cluster_field + place_pebbles: 4–7 Gaussian clusters biased low; 5–9 medium + 45–75 small per sq. pebble_shape = min of 6–11 tangent planes of a flattened cap (75% faceted, 25% worn) with a 5–8-sided 58° skirt. Sink 25–45%, soft-max fillet, foot heap. 5. make_stones + bed_stones: 2–4 rocklab rocks of 4.5–7.5 mm, ×0.65 in z, 0.3 mm soil bank, sunk. 6. Optional crack_layer: 5 random-walk V-grooves, 0.25 mm deep. 7. max(z, EDGE 4.0), 0.4 mm flat rim; uniform heightfield solid ∪ 1 mm box ∪ stones ∪ DB base; stl_safe.

v1a: blisters and pyramid pebbles (dirt-v1a.jpg). v1b: crumbly clods (dirt-v1b-c.jpg). v1d: fewer facets (dirt-v1d.jpg). v1g: polygonal skirts, no freckles (dirt-v1g.jpg); 2.8 s, 445k faces, 22 MB, 0 overhang.

dirtlab round 2 (897430c). Shawn after slicing v1g: more relief, no rim frame, pebbles that register at tabletop distance, stronger cloddy/smooth contrast, and texture visible at 0.2 mm layers everywhere. Changes: undulation σ (14, 5), mid weight 0.15, 9 mm fade, 24° cap (rises 2.2–2.5 mm); rim band 0.4 → 0, texture ramp 2 → 1 mm; clods r 0.45–2.4, up to 0.75 mm high, density floor 0.3; soil roughness 0.22 mm @ 1.6 mm, grit 0.08; medium pebbles 8–13/sq at 2.2–4.5 mm, stones 4–7, background 0.12. New metric REQ-D-TEX: share of 1.5 mm windows with relief < 0.2 mm went 32.8% → 1.9%. About 3 s. Images: dirt-round2-draft.jpg, dirt-round2.jpg.

3. Comparison tables

3.1 Water

Variant Look Printability Controls Complexity Speed
L1 EDT rings perfect offsets of the shore, squared at the edges fine (0.15 mm) A, λ, decay, 2nd harmonic trivial instant
L2 point sources raindrop circles, moiré fine n sources, λ, decay, phase jitter trivial instant
L3 structured diagonal corduroy + rock collar ok; mesh blew up 18 WaterSurfaceConfig fields moderate fast
L5 / legacy low lumps + a few stripe arcs + pasted ring ±0.5 mm, safe noise σ/RMS, λ, seg_len, offset, fade, edge sine moderate fast
rings tidy rings; empty open water good λ₀, g, wobble, p, A/decay per kind, break low fast
crescents raindrop commas ok spacing, arc radius/len, height low —
combed feathered wind strokes ok (crest ≥ 1 mm) spacing, length/height near/far, profile high (tracing + smooth union) slower
lapped broken rings + strokes ok near radius + both high slower
sim* realistic chop, all directions, "spiky" marginal (grid spikes at the bank) λ band, directions, depth→c, damping high (1400-step PDE) slowest
spectrum lumpy, odd calm zone ok N, λ range, spread, Stokes low fast
emitted crisp shore-parallel trains, regular good per-source s/decay/λ₀/growth, rider, break, w-exponent moderate (Dijkstra) ~fast
emitted-chaos order near sources → chaos far good + wobble growth, order lo/hi, corner emitters moderate ~fast
spectral-chaos wave groups, ~1 mm good + band per kind, amp exponent, sharpen moderate ~fast
swell-chaos broad round swells + sharp riders, rock chop + wake, 2× relief good: rock band ≥ 2 mm, soft trough floor, uniform mesh + long/short sharpen, λ^1.4, wake, enclosure, rounding, far emitters, reeds moderate ≈ 2.3 s for the whole tile

dirtlab R2 bigger rises, contrast, visible pebbles, no frame 0 overhang, 98% of windows ≥ 1 layer same + density floor same ~3 s

4. What is genuinely different about the newest

swell-chaos versus everything before it. All the older water applied one texture to the whole tile. Each was one of: a distance-offset pattern (rings, legacy, L1), point circles (L2), global directional trains (L3, spectrum, sim-wind), or isotropic noise or chop (sim, legacy noise). swell-chaos is the first to make the water a field of emitters:

  1. Structure comes from the scene. Every wave train belongs to an object. Geodesic distance makes it wrap around other obstacles rather than pass through them.
  2. Kind → spectrum. Land emits long round swells, rocks short sharp chop, open corners far weak swell. Scales are related: riders sit on swells from the same source. This fixes Shawn's R2 complaint that "scales are unrelated".
  3. Order decays into chaos. Near a source the dominant (w⁸) train rules, so you get crisp, readable, brush-catching crests. Far away the trains sum as interference, so the water is non-directional and tiles safely. This removes both "targets" and "wave rows".
  4. Scale-aware crest shaping. Long components stay round and short ones are sharpened, with λ^1.4 so the swells dominate. That gives the "acrylic painting" read: broad forms for drybrush and crisp highlights on top.
  5. About 2× relief (−0.74…+2.2 mm) with print guards: the rock band is ≥ 2 mm, a softplus trough floor and a deeper bed stop terraces, and a uniform 0.2 mm mesh stops facets.
  6. Robust to topology after R7e: rounded land offsets, enclosure-scaled reach and far emitters, so bays and ponds don't nest contours.

shorelab latest. The shore is a composition, not a texture. A real bank's waterline comes from where the hill-following ground G crosses WL. Water exists only in the basin. Bare ground is the same dirt as everywhere. Grass steers to the water. Reeds are both obstacles and wave emitters. The water is softened near the shore (sharpen 0.45 plus a blur), so ripples at the shoreline read as gentle. What's new at the end is entirely grass (stroke blades) and export hygiene; the water and bank have been stable since v0g.

dirtlab round 2. It has the same architecture as v1g: one height field, scales built in order and soft-maxed, so features keep their identity and get fillets instead of summing to porridge. Four things change: - bigger landform relief (2.2–2.5 mm) and no rim frame; - a density floor, so "tamped" ground still has clods; - larger and more numerous pebbles and stones, readable at tabletop distance; - a print-referenced acceptance metric: ≥ 0.2 mm relief in 98% of 1.5 mm windows.

v1g looked fine in renders but sliced into smooth plains. Round 2 is the first dirt tuned against the printer's layer height. The library SoilCarpet, by contrast, is a dense uniform sum-of-blisters at the 0.1 mm scale with no landform, no stones and no patchiness.

5. Collapsing each kind into ONE parameterised definition

5.1 Shared mechanisms

  • Distance fields to sources: EDT (L1, L5, rings), geodesic Dijkstra (emitted+), straight-line (edges, corners, far emitters), with smoothing inside the mask by normalised convolution. This appears in every water variant and in shore steering.
  • Crest profile on a phase: cos, ((1+cos)/2)^p, Stokes u + k(u²−1)/2, tanh taming.
  • Phase warp / coherence: wobble noise that grows with distance.
  • Gates and envelopes: e^(−d/decay), break gates, Hann segment fades, rim fades.
  • Combiners: sum, max, soft max (dirt), w^p partition of unity, √Σw² interference, order→chaos blend.
  • Tile-edge contract: the shared edge sine (water) or the constant edge height (dirt, 4.0 mm).
  • Print guards: minimum λ or feature size, trough soft floor, uniform mesh, stl_safe, slicer-island and open-air checks, the texture-per-layer metric.
  • Scatter primitives (dirt clods, pebbles, stones; reeds; crescents; strokes): Poisson/dart placement under a density field with soft max into a height field.

5.2 Water = flood + emitters

water {
  level: 2.6mm; bed: level - 2.2mm; edge_profile: sine(amp 0.6, λ square/2)   # tide/rim contract
  tide: noise(σ 9mm, rms 0.35)
  distance: geodesic(cell 0.25, smooth 1.2) | euclid
  emitters: [
    { from: land,   strength 1.0, decay 34mm*enclosure, band [5,16] growth 0.012 n 4, round_offsets k 0.45 },
    { from: rock,   strength 0.55*1.15, decay 10mm, band [2,4.5] growth 0.035 n 3, wake 0.9 },
    { from: corner, strength 0.3, decay 70mm, band [5,14] n 3, coherence_rate 0.5 },
    { from: reed,   additive, amp 0.45, decay 4.5mm, band [1.8,3] n 2 },
    { from: far(n 3, 220mm) when open_corners < 2 },
  ]
  spectrum: { amp_exp 1.4, sharpen_long 0.15, sharpen_short 0.9, split: geomean }
  coherence: { wobble 1mm + 0.06*d, short_decohere_exp -0.3 }
  break: { bias 0.9, sigma 7mm }
  combine: { dominant_exp 8, order_lo 0.12, order_hi 0.45, far_amp 0.7 }
  amplitude: 0.8mm; trough_floor: softplus(-1.1, 0.25); contact_lip: 0.3@0.6mm
}

Every historical variant is a point in this space:

Variant Parameter point
L1 / rings emitters = {land, rock}, distance = euclid, band = single λ, growth g, coherence wobble const, combine = nearest-kind blend, break gates, no corners
L2 point sources emitters = point sets on the boundary, euclid, single λ, combine = sum, no coherence growth
L3 structured / spectrum / sim-wind emitter = one far plane source (direction θ, spread), band, shoal = λ×f near obstacles, + rock bow/meniscus as tiny emitters
legacy rings emitter with a segment gate (random arcs of the boundary, offset d₀, one period) + noise tide
emitted single-λ trains + rider (a 2-component band), dominant_exp 8, no order→chaos (c ≡ 1), edge emitters
emitted-sum same with combine = linear sum
emitted-chaos + corners, wobble growth 0.06, order→chaos
spectral-chaos + bands, amp_exp 1.0, uniform sharpen 0.85
swell-chaos as above, the default
sim* not expressible as emitters (a PDE): rejected anyway; keep out of the language
combed / crescents a different primitive (traced strokes / scattered arcs): an optional strokes layer, rejected

So "rows", "chop", "rings" and "chaos" are just settings of distance, band, coherence and combine.

5.3 Shore = boundary between a ground region and a water region

shore {
  line: noise_wobble([σ14 amp11, σ5.5 amp5], normalise 2σ) | follows ground ∩ water.level
  bank: smoothstep(width 8mm, offset 3mm) from ground → water.bed
  strip: { width noise(0..4mm), surface: dirt }      # 0 width → grass reaches water
  water_near: { sharpen_short 0.45, blur 0.3mm }       # override of water params locally
  steer: { reach 9mm, away→along compression }         # consumed by grass
  emitters: reeds(clumps 3..5, in shallows -1.1..+0.2 of level) → water.emitters.reed
  basin: water only where mask ⊕ 0.6mm; else ground - 0.3
}

The shore isn't a surface algorithm of its own. It is a boundary with parameters that edits its neighbours: bank geometry, a strip using the ground's texture, local water overrides, an emitter set, and a steering field. That matches the existing Boundary(width_mm, layers=[…]) concept.

5.4 Dirt = ordered soft-max stack of scales

dirt {
  edge: 4.0mm; rim_flat: 0; texture_ramp: 1mm; rises_only: true
  landform: { noise σ[14,5] w[1,0.15], plain_frac 0.35, relief 0.9..1.6→~2.4mm, slope_cap 24°, edge_fade 9mm }
  roughness: fbm(1.6mm, 0.22mm, 3 oct)
  clods: scatter{ density 0.7/mm² × patch(σ6, floor 0.3), r 0.45..2.4 ^2.2, lobes 1..3,
                  harmonics[2,3,5], profile(flat 2.6), h/r .16..35 ≤0.75, sunk 0..0.5, soft 0.06 }
  grit: fbm(0.45, 0.08) + grains 0.12/mm²
  pebbles: scatter{ clusters 4..7 σ3..7, low_bias 0.8, background 0.12,
                    sizes [{0.5..1.5, 45..75/sq}, {2.2..4.5, 8..13/sq}],
                    shape facets(6..11, tilt 12°, max 65°) | worn 0.25, skirt 58° 5..8 sides,
                    sink 0.25..0.45, fillet 0.08/0.22, mound 0.2 }
  stones: rocks{ n 4..7, d 4.5..7.5, z_scale 0.65, bank 0.3, sink } 
  cracks: off | walks{ n 5, w 0.35, depth 0.25 }
  accept: texture_per_layer{ window 1.5mm, min_relief 0.2mm, ≥97% }
}

Points in this space: - SoilCarpet = only clods with circular profile p 3.5, no patch field, combine max, plus a tiny roughness: no landform, pebbles or stones. - L0 = only roughness at three scales. - grasslab mud = roughness + dome pebbles + a negative "dip" landform. - v1g = R2 with smaller numbers and a 0.4 mm rim frame. - dirt_relief = this stack minus landform and stones, exported as a relief offset.

That last one is exactly the "texture" sub-definition other kinds (shore strip, water land, rock floor, gaps between grass blades) should reference.

notes-variants-walls-trees.md · converted to HTML on 1 Nov 2026

Variant archaeology — walls / floors and trees / leaves

Scope: every wall, floor, tree and leaf surface variant that has been generated in this repo, from oldest to newest. For each one: how it worked, what it looked like, and why it was replaced. This answers Shawn's question ("I like the look of the last one, but … how did the other ones work, and what's different about the new ones?") and prepares the Rust rewrite.

Sources: git history (git log, git show sha:path), src/dharmatiles/walls/, src/dharmatiles/trees/, docs/design/walls-coursed-masonry.md (the experiment log E1–E27 with Shawn's verdicts), docs/meta/history/2026-07-03-leaf-placement-complete-history.md, docs/meta/history/2026-06-24-leaf-rendering-deep-history.md, docs/design-point-cloud-tree.md, memory notes (walls-campaign-research, organic-only-leaf-placement, tree-tile-perf-embreex). Images are under img/variants/ (section 6). Most are PNG renders committed at each step, recovered with git show. Four are new pyrender renders of existing stl/test/demo-*.stl.

1. Family trees (old → new)

2. Per-variant pipelines

2a. Walls

W1 — CutStoneWall gen-1 (E1–E4, ad24cd0). 1. _segments(spine): spine (square units) → outer-face segments, body to the left. 2. _layout: course heights sampled from course_mm (5.5–8.5), with the top course taking the remainder; bay cuts from bay_mm (11–20) rejected if closer than min_bond_mm (3) to the course below; quoin ownership alternates per course at corners. 3. Core slab = footprint inset reveal_mm (joints are reveals, not gaps). 4. _block_mesh: convex hull of a jittered box (8 corners plus face-centre points, pulled in up to chip_mm) with a 45° bottom chamfer ring (R10 overhang guard). 5. _round_edges → _blur_remesh σ0.7 (voxel occupancy + marching cubes) → broadband plane-wave _relief_field. 6. Per-block yaw ±1.2° and tilt ±0.7° (otherwise coplanar faces "melt"); joint 1.2 mm. 7. Manifold union; tile seams got flush plane cuts; stamp terrain_support_z / obstacle_mask.

Look: chunky, Hirst-like blocks with wide mortar lines and a soft, blurred finish. Superseded because, in Shawn's words, "I should be able to see the ends of the bricks, and the mortar should be inset there as well." He also found a raised plateau mid-face (from the face-centre hull points) and wanted the bricks nearly touching.

W2 — round 2 + texture presets (1aa7884). Same pipeline. Ends are always textured, joint_mm 0.45, face centres pulled in by 0.5×chip, and _TEXTURES presets (chipped 0.55/0.22/0.07, worn 0.30/0.60/0.10, hewn 0.80/0.15/0.16, dressed 0.15/0.12/0.03 = chip/roundover/relief mm). Verdicts: "incredible… beautiful", then "this looks great". Later changes were finish-level, not structural.

W3 — plane-wave aged relief (stone refactor, E28 production tiles, 65f212c). stone/finish.aged_relief (sum of plane waves, broad 6 / grain 16) is the single texture pass. Rejected at E36: on large flat faces, plane-wave sums turn into directional corduroy ("chop", "wrinkled fabric").

W4 — common stone relief (E36 9929d22, ff1a538). stone/noise.py adds isotropic value-noise fBm. stone_relief() computes −carve·smoothstep(fbm), which saturates into worn floors, plus a dish and curvature damping. The stats match the measured official floors (RMS 0.32, skew −0.99). Walls use the floor recipe with carve = min(0.62·relief/0.10, 0.62, 0.14·min dim). Two variants were rejected along the way: edge-weighted "structured wear" ("The floor was nice, the walls sucked. Undo that and fix the walls to look like the floor"), and shallow carve below 0.4 mm, which reads as engraved scratches. The same commit fixed the crease present since E1: hull jitter is now drawn per ring × side, not per corner.

W5 — rotated-frame remesh (c85026e). Same blur_remesh, but run in a frame rotated about 35°. Shawn found horizontal bands, then a washboard texture. Rejected.

W6 — EXACT blocks (48f2704) ★ current cut-stone kernel. 1. Hull points are two planar rings (top and bottom identical — "All should be sharp? These are bricks?" — so the bottom chamfer was deleted). 2. Hull face planes are deduplicated (np.unique on rounded N,D). 3. The SDF is the log-sum-exp smooth-max of the planes, τ = max(min(0.7·roundover, 0.22·min dim), 0.6·pitch). The LSE blend itself is the roundover. 4. skimage.marching_cubes on that SDF at pitch = clip(max dim/56, 0.18, 0.32), with pitch nudges 1.073 / 0.941 if the result is not a volume. 5. stone_relief (floor recipe) plus a 0.10 mm grain at 1.5 mm. 6. Placed with face recess 0.6, yaw/tilt; manifold union with the core; separate_pinches; clip.

Measured face ripple is 0.07 µm (was 17 µm), and face slope error 0.003° (was 12.6°).

F1 — Fieldstone E6–E11: one independent hull per cell (97b8f09 → 4e75d3a). 1. Chassis layout, but with wider ranges and throughstone merges (E7). 2. Each stone is the hull of Fibonacci-sphere support points on an ellipsoid↔box blend (blockiness 0.38–0.75) with per-point lumpiness, then _blur_remesh σ0.9. 3. Compensation for the shave from rounding: E6 _PACK_COMP_MM, E8 build oversize then rescale to fit the cell exactly. 4. Gap-fill attempts: pinning stones (E8), _OVERLAP_MM past the midlines (E9), then no overlap with real 0.9 mm gaps (E10). 5. Rubble hearting (_extra_parts) so voids show deeper stones rather than the core. This survived into later versions. 6. Instrument: wall_mortar_diag.py measured red-mortar pixels going from 17% → 0.4% → 0.00%.

Look: lumpy pebbles in mortar, or a flat slab with embossed lumps. Verdicts: E6 "terrible"; E8 "not enough"; E9 "rocks can't overlap — fused rocks, not a carefully stacked pile"; E10 "still not the reference … everything is up for grabs". Root cause, found at E12: neighbours never share edge geometry, so the stones can't look fitted.

F2 — Fieldstone E12 crack network (a7152cb). 1. Chassis courses and bays define the crack topology. 2. _bed(seg,z) is a cached two-sine wobble per bed line (_WOBBLE_AMP_MM, _WOBBLE_WL_MM). _drift(seg,course,t) is a cached slant/bow per head joint. Both stones beside a crack evaluate the same curve. 3. _outline(cell) is the polygon those curves bound. 4. shapely buffer(-(joint/2 + r)) then buffer(+r) rounds the corners; segmentize(_RING_STEP_MM) keeps the corner vertices. 5. The outline is lofted through the thickness with a gentle belly. 6. Subdivide → relief → Taubin. No blur-remesh (marching cubes terraces near-planar faces). Delaunay caps. 7. Rubble hearting behind. Build time about 5 s.

Shawn: "that's great. I love it".

F3 — E15 prism + roundover (f816f53). No belly or relief: a straight extruded prism of the outline, with a cosine-spaced arc inset on the face↔side edges. Mesh size halved. Asked for even rounder stones, which led to E16 (per-stone random roundover 0.9–2.0, capped at 0.22×size).

F4 — E17 pebble morph (ae6246a) → E18–E19. Each stone is sphere topology. The equator ring is the crack outline at mid-depth. Latitude follows a superellipse meridian s = (1 − |sin lat|^a)^b with a ∈ 2.0–3.2 and b ∈ 0.33–0.48 drawn per pole; poles drift up to 0.12. Rejected sub-variant: a true ellipse meridian, which looked like "melted dough". E18 set joint 0 (the stones touch and the crack is the two curvatures meeting). E19 made beds flat with z-scale s^p, _BED_FLAT_EXP.

F5 — E20–E27 → current ★ (b5e9605 … 1f7cdd1, then f7b4a2f). 1. Crack network as in F2. 2. Inward-only ring noise 0.04–0.16. 3. Superellipse sphere-morph, anisotropic flat beds (p 0.40–0.65). 4. Head overlap U(0.45, 0.95) and bed overlap U(0.35, 0.80), so pressed contacts fuse in the union. The top course is exempt and perched. 5. Proud faces 0.10–0.70 mm, with a 15% deep tail to 1.10. 6. _core_boxes → [] (no mortar). Hearting is about 990 rough rubble_stone chips (foot 3.5–5.5, spacing 2.8, setback 1.3). 7. stone_relief (common pass), separate_pinches, _END_MARGIN_MM 0.7. 8. Optional coping='vertical': on-edge slabs with a shared lean (_COPING_*).

E26 verdict: "I think I still see mortar". E27 fix, from his call: "smooth rubbles no good. should be lots of little rocks --- very rough." E27 verdict: "that is a thing of beauty".

B1 — BrickWall (E28, 7127517). This is pure chassis configuration: course 2.5–3.0, bay 5.0–6.6, joint 0.125, reveal 1.0, worn kernel (chip 0.25, roundover 0.35, relief 0.08). missing_prob 0.04 returns None, leaving a socket over the core. spall_prob 0.10 re-rolls the brick with chip = 0.35·min dim.

B2 — RegularBrickWall (a725978). bond='running': _running_heights gives uniform courses and _running_cuts gives half-brick stagger with _CLOSER_MIN 1.5. Yaw 0.25, tilt 0.15, recess 0.12. Shawn asked for "another variant within the same engine".

Floors. - FL1 (E32, grid stamper): one _block_mesh slab per DB square, each seated on its own terrain patch with a tilt and soil in the joints. Superseded by Shawn's "The slab should go right to the bottom of the terrain … The floor IS the terrain, replaces the soil." - FL2 (E33): full-depth slabs from z=0, terrain lowered to a 1.2 mm bed, engrave_cracks on 20% of slabs. - FL3 (E34–E36): official height; plane-wave drybrush, then the common stone relief. - FL4 (E57 ★): CutStoneWall(laid_flat=True). The height axis becomes the second plan axis and thickness becomes pavement depth with the outer face up. Courses/bays are pinned to an exact pitch with min_bond_mm=0; the core becomes a sheet reveal_mm below the top; hearting becomes joint chinking; one bottom clip; pull_mask keeps tile edges flush. The drystone floor is FieldstoneWall(laid_flat=True, course 8–15, bay 9–18). Shawn's prompt: "the floor should just be a wall laying on its side … with different sized bricks."

2b. Trees

T1 — ScaTree (1570e63). 1. trunk.py: bent spine, elliptical rings, bark ridge harmonics, root flare, Z-noise wrinkle. 2. Attractors in a crown ellipsoid. 3. Runions SCA: tips step toward attractors within the influence radius; sca_kill_r 2→3 consumes them. 4. Multi-root seeds along the upper 40% of the trunk; tip splitting on XY spread. 5. Da Vinci pipe-model radii. 6. Frustum mesh, then shared per-node rings (37887b3).

Look: a tangled, wiry bush (image tree-scatree-sca-0613). It was replaced within a day because the branches were chaotic and doubled back, and there was no FDM guarantee.

T2 — constructive (2663bc8). Deterministic forking: children are spread on a Fibonacci disc, then by zone-based repulsion (each parent owns an angular wedge, so branches never cross). Deleted the same day. No surviving render.

T3/T4 — surface SCA / envelope SCA (21839f8; space-colonization-envelope-tree.md). SCA with attractors on, or inside, an axisymmetric canopy envelope; a no-split zone in the lowest 20% of the crown; elevation minimum 20°. Deleted at 122cb47 in favour of CloudTree. No surviving render.

T5 — CloudTree / Tree ★ (trees/skeleton.py). 1. _sample_canopy: canopy side surface weighted by area of revolution (n_attractors 200), with no attractors on the flat bottom disk. 2. Optional Voronoi attractor groups (group_width_mm / group_height_mm 20), plus foliage_bulge_mm 6. 3. _branch_skeleton BFS: each branch owns attractors. main_dir is the blend toward the target given by branch_target, with smoothing_alpha 0.1. 4. Lookahead from next_pos: an attractor is stray if it falls outside branch_split_angle_deg × branch_split_eagerness, or needs a heading below target_fdm_angle_deg 35°. Stray clusters spawn sub-branches; at most max_branches_per_step 3; split by branch_fork_balance. 5. With one attractor left, _grow_to_leaf lands exactly on it (attractors are always terminal). 6. _compute_radii_bottom_up: r = (Σ r_child^e)^(1/e), e = 3, tips 1.0 mm. 7. build_branch_mesh: cubic-Bezier tubes with C1 prior_dirs, root flare, bark. The strict_fdm_angle_deg 26° audit emits a RuntimeWarning.

2c. Foliage and leaves

L0 radius-profile foliage (9e25ebc). Terminal tubes swell to foliage_radius_mm over their last 10.5 mm, with a dome cap, all in one array (no boolean). Look: tan cauliflower blobs (tree-cloudtree-radius-profile-foliage).

L0b noised clumps (d4277e1, e62475d). One separate solid per terminal branch: a cylinder-dome, later a structured rounded-cone sweep, with radial noise peak-shifted inward (it erodes about 2.3 mm). This is still the substrate today (_build_foliage_cluster_mesh, foliage_cluster_radius_mm 5.5, foliage_cluster_length_mm 10.5).

L1 single tip leaf. One keeled leaf per clump, so each clump read as a single petal.

L2 first tiled grid (7be6cee). Rows by arc along the branch, columns by circumference/col_step, droop toward gravity, plus leaf_cap_count apex fan. Leaves are concatenated, not boolean-unioned (16 s saved). Problems: a polar spike and apex gaps.

L3 branchlets (b38bf38 era). 1. Anchor on the clump. 2. Loft tube from a root circle to the leaf perimeter. 3. Blade at the tip, turned to face down-gravity. 4. Purple-face undercut detection, then per-vertex undercut solve.

Failed because a stem that bends downward is an FDM undercut, and the fixes produced "infinite complexity" (about 24 commits).

L5/L6 arc grid → Z-slice (afbe43e). 1. Slice the smooth pre-noise cluster at world-Z planes. 2. trimesh.section → shapely perimeter. 3. Sample n_col = ceil(perimeter / col_step). 4. Outward normal from the centroid. 5. _emit_leaf with the analytic contact angle.

Works on cylinders but goes bald near the apex on domes. The apex cap was patched six times.

L7 meridian-arc (4a866f3; placement.py). 1. _build_meridians: 6 azimuthal r–z curves at 64 Z samples. 2. _compute_row_z_positions: rows by surface arc, with bottom and top anchor rows. 3. _interpolate_meridian_normal per leaf. 4. Belly-dip contact-angle seat. 5. Jitter (leaf_pos_jitter 0.165, leaf_angle_jitter_deg) and lift (leaf_lift_mm 1.5). 6. Imbrication via a (φ,s) layer map. 7. Cross-cluster midrib-containment cull. 8. solidify_leaf (cone-framework raycast root walls).

Look: tidy shingled rows of large leaves (tree-meridian-placer), which "read as rows". It forces a chart onto unioned noised blobs, and about 66% of builds were wasted.

L8 greedy (1ac09ab; placement_greedy.py). 1. Pre-generate candidates and sort by world z. 2. Sweep once upward through a cheap-reject ladder. 3. Root oval built in the leaf's own frame (7f9e3ce). 4. Equal-depth oval seat (95d43d8). 5. Printability skew (13b9a39). 6. Graze translation to the standoff (c097837).

3× faster than meridian, but left coverage holes, read like pods, and its blue-noise uniformity "screams procedural". Its per-leaf primitives survived.

L9 shoots (96f77d1). Each accepted candidate marches a spine up-slope carrying 3–7 leaves in a herringbone; adaptive belly-dip seat; exact-distance root grid. Contributed two keepers but was "still judged ugly". All three placers were then rendered side by side, and the recorded verdict was "iterating placement parameters is not converging; restart from requirements."

L10 organic ★ (66d85e3 → 43a2d51; place_leaves_organic). 1. _union_surface: boolean-union all noised clusters into one placement mesh. 2. Area-weighted candidates (_ORGANIC_CANDIDATE_FACTOR 10) → maximal Poisson-disk dart throw on a normal-aware exact-distance grid. Spacing is _ORGANIC_SPACING_FRAC 0.45 × leaf width; _ROOT_BLOCK_COS stops disks blocking across seam V-walls. 3. Direction: down-slope rotated by _direction_field_angle (a smooth positional field, ±15°); the anchor is re-projected 0.25·L down-slope. 4. _shingle_standoffs: height-sorted standoff accumulation (Shawn's suggestion). 5. Smoothstep zone factor st between _ORGANIC_ZONE_HI 0.30 and _ORGANIC_ZONE_LO −0.45. Curl, lift and standoff scale with st; the underside end-to-end arch scales with 1−st. Curl is capped at 32° and tip lift is 1.2 mm. 6. _attempt_leaf_gen (placement_leaf.py): equal-depth oval seat, rigid frame, skew, belly-dip to _PROTRUSION_MM, base tuck, neck gate, solidify — all driven batched through embree (_drive_batched). 7. Cull leaves whose blades intersect exposed branch tubes. 8. A verification pass reports residual coverage gaps.

Shawn: "I really like the density and surface of the leaves — the placement is really nice... wow. That's amazing!" At deletion of the others: "they suck, this one is good".

4. What is genuinely different about the newest versions

Fieldstone E27 vs everything before. Construction order is reversed. Old versions made the stones first and then tried to fill the gaps (packing compensation, pins, overlap, hearting). The new version makes the cracks first and derives the stones from them. Both faces of every crack evaluate the same cached curve, so a bulge always meets a notch: the stones look chosen to fit, which a hull per cell can never achieve. On top of that, by construction: - Stone coverage is above 90%, because the outlines tile the face. - The cracks are V-grooves where two curvatures meet (joint 0), not mortar seas. - Pressed overlaps fuse the contacts, so nothing floats. - There is no mortar plane behind, only packed rough chips.

The superellipse meridian keeps the faces gently domed with fast fall-off at the silhouette, so the cracks stay dark and narrow (an ellipse made them doughy). Flat beds, perched top rocks and per-stone proudness supply the tone variation the photo references show.

Cut stone / brick / floor now (W6 + common relief). Three finish-level fixes: - Blocks are now exact. The surfaces were marching-cubes artefacts for a month (crease, bands, washboard). An analytic plane SDF with an LSE roundover gives zero ripple. - Texture is carved downward with isotropic noise, which matches measurements of the official pieces. Plane-wave corduroy is gone. - One chassis covers walls and floors (laid_flat), so any family can be a floor.

Organic leaves vs the meridian / greedy / shoots placers. The other placers imposed a chart (rows) or an order and then reactively culled problems. The organic placer makes each requirement structurally true: - No seams to manage: the clusters are unioned into one surface. - Coverage is guaranteed: maximal Poisson saturation leaves no bare patch wider than 2·spacing. - No rows: positions are random, but directions are coherent through the field. - Correct overlap order: height-sorted standoff accumulation means a higher leaf never sits under a lower one. - Undersides print supportless: a flush arched blade cannot re-enter the surface.

That is why the canopy reads as one continuous shingled mass instead of rows, pods or an artichoke. The per-leaf seating machinery from the losing placers was kept (placement_leaf.py).

5. Collapsing variants into one declarative definition per kind

Masonry (walls and floors)

Every wall/floor variant is masonry(layout × unit kernel × finish × assembly):

masonry {
  spine | grid                       # polyline (sq units) or tile grid (floor)
  orientation: wall | laid_flat
  layout {
    courses: range(mm) | exact(mm)   # 'irregular' sampling vs 'running'
    bays:    range(mm) | exact(mm)
    bond: irregular(min_bond_mm) | running(half) | none
    corners: quoin_alternate
    post: [throughstones(frac,max), split_tall(prob), crenellate(merlon,crenel,depth),
           coping(vertical, lean), ruin(level, wl)]
  }
  cracks: straight | shared_curves(wobble_amp, wobble_wl, drift, bow)   # fieldstone = shared
  unit {
    kernel: box_hull(chip, pull_mask)        -> exact_sdf(roundover)          # cut/brick/slab
          | outline_morph(meridian=superellipse(a,b), bed_flat_exp, pole_drift)  # fieldstone
    joint_mm, head_overlap, bed_overlap, proud, face_recess, yaw, tilt
    defects: missing_prob, spall_prob, crack_prob
  }
  finish: stone_relief(carve, dish, grain, scale_by=preset)    # ONE pass for all
  core: recessed(reveal) | none
  hearting: none | chips(foot, spacing, setback)
  openings: [opening(at,width,sill,head,shape,leaf,slot)]
  export: union, separate_pinches, clip(tile|bottom)
}

Presets are bundles of these values: - cut(texture) = box_hull + straight cracks + core + presets chipped/worn/hewn/dressed. - brick = small ranges + missing/spall. - regular_brick = running bond + near-zero yaw/tilt. - fieldstone = shared_curves + outline_morph + overlaps + no core + chip hearting. - floor(f) = f + laid_flat + exact grid pitch + bond none.

The rejected variants map to parameter values that should not be exposed: hull-per-cell fieldstone (cracks straight + ellipsoid kernel), mortar core for fieldstone, voxel blur kernel, bottom chamfer, plane-wave finish.

Trees

tree {
  canopy: envelope(height, radius, base_radius) ; attractors(n, surface-weighted, groups(w,h), bulge)
  skeleton: partitioned_growth(segment, split_angle, eagerness, target, fork_balance,
                               max_branches, smoothing, fdm_target, fdm_strict)
  radii: pipe(exponent, min_radius)
  wood: bezier_tubes + root_flare + bark
  foliage: cluster(sweep cone, radius, length, noise(coarse, fine))   # substrate
  leaves: organic(spacing_frac, candidate_factor, direction_field(amp, freq),
                  layering=sorted_standoff(step), zones(hi, lo), curl_cap, tip_lift,
                  arch_mm, skewer_cull=exposed)
          shape(length, width, thickness, fold, inner/outer curve, curl)
}

The old leaf placers do not fit as parameters of leaves (they were whole strategies). Keep only organic. Their surviving pieces (equal-depth seat, skew, graze, belly-dip, exact-distance grid) are internal primitives. Trees T1–T4 are not worth keeping as options either. One possible exception: if a "wild bush" look is ever wanted, T1's tangle could be a skeleton mode (skeleton: sca(kill_r, influence)).
